{"title":"\[External\] Virtual Try-On (VTO)"}
</properties>
<content>
<callout icon="👖" color="green_bg">
	FLUX VTO lets you generate virtual try-on results from a person image plus one or more garment references.
	It is optimized for low latency and is currently available to approved partner organizations.
</callout>
You can try it out directly here: [Black Forest Labs - Frontier AI Lab](https://showcase-vto.preview.bfl.ai/)
Your domain should be already whitelisted, but in case that you do not have access to the app: please let us know!

## Get started in 30 seconds
**1. Get access + API key**
You need:
- an approved API key
- organization access to the VTO route
If you are not sure your project is allowlisted, contact the BFL team.
**2. Submit a VTO request**
```bash
PERSON_B64="$(base64 < /path/to/person.png | tr -d '
')"
GARMENT_B64="$(base64 < /path/to/garment_top.png | tr -d '
')"
# Optional second garment (for bottom, jacket layer, etc.)
GARMENT2_B64="$(base64 < /path/to/garment_bottom.png | tr -d '
')"

jq -n \
  --arg person "$PERSON_B64" \
  --arg garment "$GARMENT_B64" \
  '{
    prompt: "model is wearing the provided garments, realistic studio lighting, clean background",
    person: $person,
    garment: $garment,
    garment_2: (if $garment2 == "" then null else $garment2 end),
    width: 1024,
    height: 1024,
    output_format: "png"
  }' > /tmp/vto_request.json

curl -sS -X POST "https://labs.eu2.bfl.ai/v1/flux-try-on" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -H "X-Key: $BFL_API_KEY" \
  --data-binary @/tmp/vto_request.json
```
Response:
```json
{"id":"xxxxxxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxx-xxxxxxxxxxxx","polling_url":"..."}
```
**3. Poll for your result**
```bash
curl -sS "https://labs.eu2.bfl.ai/v1/get_result?id=YOUR_TASK_ID" \
  -H "X-Key: $BFL_API_KEY"
```
When `status` is `"Ready"`, use `result.sample`.

## Performance tip
If you want to send 2 or more garment images, we recommend merging them into a single canvas first to keep latency down. In our testing, we could not notice any quality degradation from doing this.
![](https://prod-files-secure.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/d47c9781-7a36-4f60-98ec-c16fd7b286a6/ca070cd9-81da-4dfc-af0d-3fdc829da054/image.png?X-Amz-Algorithm=AWS4-HMAC-SHA256&X-Amz-Content-Sha256=UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD&X-Amz-Credential=ASIAZI2LB466TSKXVHBY%2F20260419%2Fus-west-2%2Fs3%2Faws4_request&X-Amz-Date=20260419T151853Z&X-Amz-Expires=3600&X-Amz-Security-Token=IQoJb3JpZ2luX2VjED8aCXVzLXdlc3QtMiJIMEYCIQC0IU1KnT7VUhHSIqkgiQ5AwoArcU%2F06ihVF1LUHbzxOQIhAPQIMbmN70dEIW7PirEXkNehDdbvEAll20eCWlSzNA0GKv8DCAgQABoMNjM3NDIzMTgzODA1Igx9fZNbpog3WtobzVYq3AMBb0KJtRgFhZ7mTVx2T9hwgLO7MnCtSoCwAbXJKnS6Fslwc%2FWlFluDFLzJUqGrc0BQ2cretuUBPuG3Lyzb04fUEv2e9eLKzOXY%2FTjng7cDQVcnYF%2BhgFahOcq3917dktQEqyo8R%2BtQRh9%2BldhiCtocjcH82GW3%2BMLD44Va3dspMsDStoiN1zHYWW4BJWl2iiwb7MerXfjJ2VxfuFYzij%2FWlIiSXISIND4gbZIJ4yLauKc5nlv2qdzHLkXNQ%2FR0RFvTGtrfeLMUVc9P%2Fj3hPXIde1H7fljQlWREZzPD3POf%2BSfrBFLJYQJ2ef%2BycTz0DwA5r34%2FvAtGrePMvnyb3cpfOU7c%2BFT6BIvcA283D4GQVPYt7aTPvbjPYNa2LBj%2B4cBEV7P9eDf8PGCfApVsfu61jcHc%2BHYknHl0fPilDqQI%2B7dkfREyuG809lok%2B%2BCoJj0Ta8jC8pcFBco1RWuJ1SJKi0I4ZLmiyKm3MzXBFT9yJEKrPiIwjuUlp12%2BN4rM3%2BwyW2PZfvHYPm%2FxDzEffLej%2B1IUgULCVqtOQvxdisshcmnZVfRxtFv9KhEZd8aCS61ocpqr6zlu1sunX5hgp5boeE0pYQP0kCgSYa%2BAQ2KqSzWoUrHvHg6ckzKgUDDC0JPPBjqkATlpW6N7IbwxoHMFBHethhyf6d8rFGu7Yy%2BDn5vfQnsZZeeVKM7cdH3jxAUBv6FVzWMk0ZNf0D8V78SQk7FlgKPgodXblp7R1WvROVy40N2h2zEeHpV7EB7HE6jjXqLgUwUtDVG503DS5NG%2FFQnX%2BBvWABKQTfVzdCpbKaJzhe4EkNTqyrEZKKFwwD%2FZ%2F2jccClSpXZ5LhhjrTZ%2FEkGG%2B3I3%2BFpn&X-Amz-Signature=8ed28db84ac290dbae0dacd5fb38349e10f7bf89d83f3d9ea5ec859a0ede5886&X-Amz-SignedHeaders=host&x-amz-checksum-mode=ENABLED&x-id=GetObj

## Mid-train T2I Distillation
---
The 4B and 9B base models were initially pre-trained:
- 4B - 670k steps
	- /blob/raw/home/dominik/logs/june/flux2beta/4B/2025-07-26T13-30-40_projects-2025_06_flux2-flux2_7325/checkpoints/step-670000
- 9B - 512.5k steps
	- /blob/raw/home/dominik/logs/june/flux2beta/9B/2025-07-28T10-27-44_projects-2025_06_flux2-flux2_7544/checkpoints/step-512500
These models were trained on the pre-train set, which was largely at the 256p resolution. Both models have trouble generating at 1k resolution. An example from the 4B below:<br>

Both models distilled to match the 32B teacher, and repa loss was used as well. Here we use the mid-train dataset (post-train to be used for guidance distillation and ladd). This initial size distillation is done with 64 nodes, as it will serve as a base for all future runs where we replace the text encoder with something smaller. Results below:

{"title":"\[Marketing Agencies\] Our Partnership Strategy"}
</properties>
<content>
### TL/DR
Marketing agencies are reorganizing around AI to move from slow, manual creative work to fast, automated, performance-driven production, supported by internal platforms like Omni, Vermeer, and Song. To serve them, BFL should offer a modular Agency License, API access, dev models, client-hosted rights, and optional reseller terms, anchored in clear licensing and meaningful minimum commits.
## Summary
---
1. Marketing Agencies mainly want AI **to make campaigns faster, cheaper, and more effective;** creating content automatically, updating it in real time, and moving away from slow manual work and hourly fees toward results-driven production. 
2. To achieve this, these companies are rapidly re-organizing around by: 
	- Creating **dedicated innovation labs** and **technical teams** that blend creatives, engineers, and data experts. 
	- They are also building **internal AI platforms** that sit across the entire organization, giving thousands of employees access to generative tools inside unified systems (e.g., Omnicom’s “Omni”, Havas’s “Vermeer”, Accenture’s “Song AI”, etc.). 
	- Agencies also aksi **shifting their business models** toward high-velocity, on-brand, AI-assisted production, focusing on workflows like iterative editing, brand-specific generation, and automated content pipelines instead of traditional handcrafted creative.
3. As a result, **Agencies need a flexible AI offering** that allows for API access for thousands of employees, dev models for technical labs to customize and fine-tune, and client-hosted rights for delivery work. <span discussion-urls="discussion://2bfc3702-22d5-802d-a497-001c96c93c6c">Agencies are uniquely positioned to provide brand interpretation and workflow expertise, which would be challenging for BFL to replicate</span> at scale. Our goal is to offer high-performance models, clear licensing terms, and well-aligned incentives to ensure that customers realize the value of our models, and that it directly translates to<span discussion-urls="discussion://2bfc3702-22d5-801c-8591-001c5bc2dd41"> increased revenue for us and the agency</span>. 
4. The best way forward is a **modular Agency License** with four components: 1) API Access, 2) Dev Model Access, 3) Client-Hosted Add-On, and 4) Reseller Add-On. We will leverage minimum commits to capture value up-front, output caps to avoid losing value on scale, and rev shares/referral fees on efforts that require deeper partnership or commitment from BFL and the Agencies.
<empty-block/>
<callout color="gray_bg">
	### 📊 Marketing Agency Landscape  {toggle="true"}
		- The **Marketing Agencies** market size is estimated at \$453Bn in 2025, and is expected to reach USD 571.53 billion by 2030, at a CAGR of 4.8%. The key trends include **increased adoption of AI in creative development**, rapid scaling of performance-based pricing contracts, and cookie-less personalization technologies are reshaping how brands evaluate agency partnerships. [Source](https://www.mordorintelligence.com/industry-reports/global-marketing-agencies-market)
		- The key players continue to be fairly concentrated, with the top five groups: WPP, Omnicom, Publicis Groupe, Interpublic Group, and Dentsu, collectively accounting for a significant portion of 2024 billings. Omnicom’s planned merger with Interpublic, valued at over \$13Bn, is set to create the world’s largest holding entity and unlock substantial procurement and back-office synergies. <span color="green_bg">**We collected feedback and use cases from both Omnicom and IPG (and a handful of their subsidiaries)**</span>. WPP defends its leadership by funneling AI spend into its proprietary Open AI Studio that automates copy, imagery, and media-mix forecasting. <span color="green_bg">**WPP has also expressed interest in our models, and we will be setting up time with them next week**</span><span color="green_bg">. </span>
		- **We’re already following through on inbound from some of the biggest Agency Holding Companies: **WPP, Omnicom, and IPG. Once we have these deals in place, we can approach the rest: Dentsu, Havas, and Stagwell, in addition to smaller creative agencies: *VML, TBWA, RAPP, Razorfish, Havas, Doner, Digitas, Huge, Publicis Sapient or* Media Agencies: Mindshare, Starcom, Magna, Carat, Assembly, OMD.

	> *We build the standard for visual intelligence.<br><br>***The standard **means high performance and quality, lean, open, fast, affordable, always available; not clunky – a beautiful, minimalistic, reliable, no-bullshit product which makes it a no-brainer to use because it is so ridiculously easy to build on and get good results.<br><br>**Visual intelligence** is the ability to understand and simulate (i.e. create) the underlying geometry, relationships, and interactions between objects in a physical universe. ### Goal
We need to build the best and most intuitive user experiences around FLUX 3. We need to start exploring directions right away and build for future.
#### What is UI/UX?
The UI layer is our abstraction to make the value proposition of our model capabilities easy to grasp.
We see it already today with some of our customized endpoints: Endpoints are abstract and not necessarily intuitive. We can make clients understand our capabilities WAY easier, if we just showcase the intended use and what it actually generates within a frontend.
We see this pattern already today with images and this will be even more amplified once we add more modalities on the input and output.

### Our path
1. **Competitor & market analysis:<br>**Check every possible model and product in the world to understand their strengths and weaknesses.
2. **User interviews:<br>**Talk to creators and power users to understand what are their pain points, what’s working and what’s not working.
3. **Build with insights:<br>**Coding is not the challenge, building the right thing is. We need to understand the gaps in the workflows from super users and address them in our product.
4. **Feedback research:<br>**As an outcome, build Product x Research muscle. Regular exchange between product and research, where user and use-case insights inform training priorities and model capabilities shape product direction.

{"title":"\[WIP\] BFL Data Strategy 2026"}

<content>
<empty-block/>
---
### Primer - The importance of data for generative modeling 
The goal of generative modeling is to approximate a data distribution and - in the case of current frontier AI models - to be able to sample from that distribution in order to create new datapoints from that distribution. The learning algorithm which is employed ensures that the model can approximate the true (and unknown) distribution of the data the model is trained on as good as possible and is therefore the first central cornerstone for training a frontier model. However, the second - and sometimes underestimated - component, is the dataset which is used to train the model. This dataset represents the data distribution the model is targeting learning. It’s easy to imagine that a model won’t be competitive even with the most powerful learning algorithm, if the data distribution it’s trained on (aka the training dataset) is containing low quality training examples - garbage in, garbage out. This is why training data acquisition and processing is strategically extremely important for every foundation model company. As a result of this, we need to treat data as a first class citizen at BFL as well. 
### Use Cases vs. Model Capabilities - Separating the target distribution from the conditioning method
**The difference between targets and the conditioning **
The training data for foundation models has two main interfaces with the learning algorithm during training, which are the following
	1. The* target: *The target is the actual ‘training example’ and it’s used as a reference point to calculate how good the model is performing the task it’s trained to do. From the difference between the model output and the target, the loss is calculated and then backpropagated through the model to update the weights. In fact, for most probabilistic approaches, the target also gives rise to the model input at training time. In those cases, the model input is often a transformation of the target (e.g. a more noisy version of the training example in the case of diffusion models). 
	2. The* conditioning: *Under conditioning, we subsume all additional input signals that go into the model (at both training and inference), apart from the random variable, that the model learns to generate. These conditioning inputs are what make the model controllable in the end. So if we take e.g. text-to-image models, we want to be able to generate images based (or conditioned) on text prompts. That means that, at train time, we need to have text prompts for each training image we want to use. Note that the conditioning can be more than one additional inputs, e.g. in the case of image-to-video, where we both use a text prompt and an image as conditionings to create a video. (The name image-to-video is very unfortunate, since it’s actually text+image-to-video).
From a data perspective, it’s important to separate these two interfaces from each other since they affect different aspects of what the model learns during training. 
	1. <span discussion-urls="discussion://341c3702-22d5-80e5-acbf-001ce05c84b7">The targets define the visual properties,</span> the data that the model learns to generate will follow. For instance, if a model was only trained on illustrated content, it would not understand photorealism and no matter if I would prompt for ‘a blue bird’, or ‘a photorealistic blue bird’, the model would always create images that would resemble illustrations 
	2. The conditioning defines how users can interact with the model and how they can apply control to the data that the model generates. For instance, if I only have a dataset of paired text prompts and images, and train a model to generate images conditioned on the text prompts, users cannot use that model for image editing, since training an image editing model would require to always have a set of images (e.g. two images showing the same person in different environments) and (one or more) text prompts describing how to arrive at one image based on one (or multiple) other image(s) in that set. In that sense, the conditioning define the set of tasks that the model is generally capable to do.
The above separation is helpful to understand the dimensions along which we should be thinking when it comes to data acquisition. We need to understand what we need along both these dimensions in order to create a well suited dataset for training a state of the art model. Failure to do so will make it likely that we end up with sub-optimal decisions for data acquisition and will make blind spots in our data strategy more likely.
**Separating use cases from model capabilities**
Based on the above separation of the targets (or target distribution) and the conditioning, we make the following definitions.
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	**Use Case**
	A *use case* describes the requirements for the targets that we would like to have in a training dataset. Understanding the *use cases* our models are used for will help us answer the question: *How should the target distribution look like, that the model learns to generate during training?*
</callout>
<span underline="true">Example:</span> If the use case would be film making, we would rather be looking to license data from movie studies and other types of data, e.g. ads, would not

## Overview
Central hub for tracking all Finance team projects — priorities, timelines, owners, and status updates.
> Use the **Project Tracker** database below to manage active and upcoming projects. Each project has its own dedicated page for detailed notes, updates, and comments.
---
## 🗂️ Project Tracker
*Add your projects to the database below. Switch between views using the tabs: List, Board (by priority), and Timeline (Gantt).*
---
## 📌 Quick Reference
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Priority</td>
<td>Meaning</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>🔴 Critical</td>
<td>Blocking other work or has an imminent deadline</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>🟠 High</td>
<td>Important, should be actioned this sprint</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>🟡 Medium</td>
<td>Scheduled work, good progress expected</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>🟢 Low</td>
<td>Nice to have, no immediate pressure</td>
</tr>
</table>
---
## 📝 How to Use This Space
<details>
<summary>Adding a new project</summary>
	1. Click **New** in the Project Tracker database
	2. Fill in: Project Name, Owner, Start Date, End Date, Priority, Status, and Category
	3. Open the project page to add detailed description, milestones, and running update log
	4. Use the **Timeline view** to visualise the Gantt chart
</details>
<details>
<summary>Updating project status</summary>
	Open the project record and:
	- Change the **Status** field to reflect current state
	- Add a dated entry in the **Update Log** section of the project page
	- Use **@mentions** in comments to flag blockers or decisions needed
</details>
<details>
<summary>Reviewing priorities</summary>
	Switch to the **Board view** to see projects grouped by priority. Review weekly as part of Finance team standup or planning sessions.
</details>

<content>
- **Moat**: build something that will be exclusively ours
	- custom datasets
		- Branch 1: Third parties offer building datasets based on instructions
		- Branch 2: Scale dataworks and build expertise internally
	- Unique tooling
		- Systematically identify failure points in VLMs
			- camera movement
			- counting
			- left/right
			- …
		- Build custom models that can give this information consistently → feed into captions
<empty-block/>
---
<empty-block/>
- Tooling
	- Easy and cheap way to train classifier
		- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2fcc3702-22d5-80bf-bc3c-001c189d8e0d">concrete: improved nsfw annotation</span>
		- understand dataset
		- oversample special data: e.g. train classifier to detect in the wild typography
	- Procedural generation of 3d scenes
	- setup for custom object detection finetunes networks
	- Build an automatic refresh engine for pretraining sets with up-to-date content
	- standardized evaluation framework for simple t2i and t2v datasets
	- global database to query datasets together
		- → global data viewer
- Research
	- What do we need from a pretraining set, can we increase convergence speed by better selection (e.g. demystifying clip)
	- finalize: research into defect tokens
	- these are maybe with Axels Team
		- dataset curriculum 
		- unlearning vs forgetting vs consistent annotation
	- IQA / VQA we are happy with
	- full data and real usage taxonomy
- Infrastructure
	- Better understand bottlenecks (especially for small models) and resource requirements
	- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2fcc3702-22d5-8072-8250-001c479ab512">Can we optimize storage cost for datasets by mixing more tiers</span>
	- Do we need higher performance languages than python for dataloading
	- Efficiently store and retrieve 100PBs of data
- Human annotation
	- increase max throughput of data works as high skilled labeling
	- use human to get exclusive data → actual moat ?
<empty-block/>
---
<empty-block/>
- T2I
	- Fresh-up for pumpkin and generally extend. Target 5-6B total → very roughly one month of pretraining 
		- note: pumpkin currently transcodes to jpeg90, in future, keep original encoding (unless it’s obscure)
	- Typography
		- Rendered Rich Text dataset for pretraining, scale to theoretic O(1B) of on-the-fly images
		- Rendered websites for midtraining, 200M samples
		- Better pumpkin subset
	- specialized datasets
		- raw, unedited photography
		- typography in the wild 
		- info graphics and slides
		- localized datasets and special world knowledge (either for RAG or training)
		- video-stills to rare photography, e.g. backflips …
		- “natural” transparency dataset
	- Revisiting post training dataset
- Image Editing
	- single ref scale up existing efforts massively \~100M each
		- local edits
			- identify video with static camera
			- inpainting data
		- reference-based edits
		- global edits
	- mutliref
		- build proper classification of possible edits
		- build datasets across relevant categories of \~100M samples
		- custom, exclusive datasets (e.g. data vendors or data works)
	- full analysis what are people doing with classic Photoshop → need to make sure we hit those things in editing
- Video
	- Finalize current \~500M clip dataset
	- iterate on categories for videos & improve split for midtraining
	- Get to 1B good clips
	- Proper diversity filtering
	- iterating on all filters
- Video Editing
	- i2v: same targets as for image editing
	- + video to video editing
		- editing that actually changes the video
			- attribute editing
			- camera angle
			- global changes
		- reference insertion (multiref equivalent) i+v2v
		- everything else probably a i2v case
- Audio
	- get first hire and start collecting some initial sets
	- target: full pre, mid, post set
- Interleaved data / VLM / Image and Video understanding
- Text
	- get first hire and start collecting some initial sets 
	- target: full pre, mid, post set
- Game-Engine-Model-Data “World-Models”
	- Software that captures video & keystroke
	- pay people to play games
- SVG / Vector graphics
	- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2fcc3702-22d5-80d6-a190-001c2da7f2f8">Exploratory 1M set</span>

### **3 Pillars of Growth/marketing ** {toggle="true"}
	**Fuel: Content and Brand**
	- → content, creative design, \[comms & PR, events & community, DevRel\]
	**Foundation: Product Marketing**
	- → research & positioning, launches, enablement, \[ecosystem & partners\]
	**Engine: Growth Marketing**
	- → inbound & web, outbound & demand gen, lifecycle, \[ops\]
### Scenario planning  {toggle="true"}
	<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>**Scenario**</td>
<td>**What We Have**</td>
<td>**Target Customer**</td>
<td>**Primary Growth Motion**</td>
<td>**Marketing Focus**</td>
<td>**Team Structure**</td>
<td>**Q1 Priorities**</td>
<td>**Revenue Model**</td>
<td>**Key Risks**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**A: Studio Success**</td>
<td>Production-ready Studio with collaboration, templates, brand kits, team features</td>
<td>Prosumer (\$10-50/mo), SMB teams (\$100-500/mo), Agencies (\$1K+/mo)</td>
<td>**Product-led growth loops**• Viral template sharing• Collaboration invites• Branded outputs• Word-of-mouth from "lovable" moments</td>
<td>**Fuel-heavy:**• Creator economy (YouTube, TikTok)• Founder social• UGC campaigns• Community building</td>
<td>Growth: 3-4<br>PMM: 2<br>Content/Creative: 2-3<br>DevRel: 1</td>
<td>1. Build template sharing loop2. Launch YouTube influencer program (20-30 creators)3. Onboarding optimization (instant try)4. 15 customer case studies5. Tier 2 weekly launches</td>
<td>Freemium → Paid tiers(\$10/\$50/\$100+/mo)**Target: \$13M ARR**</td>
<td>• Not a destination (still pre-production tool)• High churn if use cases are one-off• Canva/Adobe have distribution advantage• Video delay limits use cases</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**B: Playground Exploration**</td>
<td>R&D/experimentation platform for testing FLUX capabilities, model feedback, community showcase</td>
<td>Developers, AI enthusiasts, early adopters, researchers</td>
<td>**Community-driven loops**• Share amazing outputs → Drive sign-ups• Developer feedback → Product improvement• Social proof/inspiration gallery</td>
<td>**Foundation-heavy:**• Technical content• Developer education• Reddit/Twitter engagement• Research transparency</td>
<td>Growth: 1-2<br>PMM: 1<br>DevRel: 2<br>Content: 1</td>
<td>1. Build inspiration gallery with viral sharing2. Developer documentation3. Reddit/Discord community activation4. "Best of Playground" weekly showcases5. API education content</td>
<td>Freemium/loss leader for brand awareness**Target: \<\$5M ARR**</td>
<td>• Not viable revenue stream at scale• Acquisition tool only, not retention• Expensive to run (GPU costs)• Competes with API customers</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**C: FDE Motion Success**</td>
<td>Custom model training, dedicated support, white-label solutions, enterprise SLAs</td>
<td>Enterprise (\$300K+ ACV):E-commerce platforms, gaming studios, media companies, creative agencies</td>
<td>**Enterprise sales-led**• Account-based marketing• Proof-of-concept projects• Custom implementations• Partner ecosystem</td>
<td>**Foundation-heavy:**• Enterprise positioning• Industry-specific case studies• Sales enablement• Analyst relations• Event sponsorships</td>
<td>PMM: 2-3<br>Demand Gen: 2<br>Field Marketing: 1-2<br>Sales Enablement: 1Partners: 1</td>
<td>1. Verticalize by industry (pick 2-3)2. Build 5 reference customers3. Sales playbook + battle cards4. Security/compliance certification (SOC2)5. Partner with system integrators</td>
<td>Enterprise contracts\$300K-\$1M+ ACV**Target: \$30-50M ARR**</td>
<td>• Long sales cycles (9-12 months)• Unproven scalability (how many FDEs needed?)• Image-only = "non-critical" function• Procurement blockers• Margin pressure from customization</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**D: Hybrid (Studio + FDE)**</td>
<td>Studio as enterprise UI + customization layer for FDE clients</td>
<td>SMB through Studio → Enterprise through FDE upsell path</td>
<td>**Land-and-expand**• Studio PLG acquires users• Usage signals enterprise intent• FDE motion for customization</td>
<td>**Balanced across all three pillars:**• PLG for acquisition• Enterprise ABM for expansion• Content for both segments</td>
<td>Growth: 3PMM: 3Content: 2Demand Gen: 2DevRel: 1</td>
<td>1. Studio PLG loops (acquisition)2. Enterprise signal detection (product qualified leads)3. Verticalize Studio for enterprise use cases4. Build upgrade path to FTE5. Dual case study library (SMB + Enterprise)</td>
<td>**Blended model:**Studio: \$10-15M ARRFTE: \$30-40M ARR**Total: \$45-55M ARR**</td>
<td>• Split focus = slower execution• Different GTM motions compete for resources• Product-market fit unclear for both• Team needs to context-switch constantly</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**E: Playground → API Funnel**</td>
<td>Playground as top-of-funnel → Convert to API customers → Upsell to enterprise</td>
<td>Developers → Startups → Scale-ups → Enterprise</td>
<td>**Developer-led growth**• Playground inspires → API adoption• Usage-based monetization• Developer advocates• Ecosystem partnerships</td>
<td>**DevRel + Product Marketing:**• Technical content• API documentation• Developer community• Integration showcases• Hackathons/contests</td>
<td>DevRel: 2-3PMM: 2Developer Marketing: 1-2Partnerships: 1</td>

## Partner Overview
---
<details>
<summary>**Company Overview**</summary>
	### Adobe’s Lines of Business
	---
	Adobe is fundamentally a SaaS company, and the bulk of Adobe’s revenue comes from recurring subscriptions (creative, document, experience). Adobe increasingly sells bundled or integrated deals combining creative tools, document workflows, and experience/marketing stacks for enterprises.
	<table header-row="true">
	<colgroup>
	<col>
	<col width="403">
	<col width="300">
	<col>
	<col>
	<col width="222">
	</colgroup>
<tr>
<td>**Segment**</td>
<td>**Key Products / Services**</td>
<td>**What It Includes / Role**</td>
<td>**FY25 Revenue (Q3 annualized)**</td>
<td>**YoY Growth (approx.)**</td>
<td>**Notes / Dynamics**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Digital Media <br>**(\~75% of Rev)</td>
<td>**Creative Cloud** (Photoshop, Illustrator, Lightroom, Premiere Pro, After Effects, InDesign, Substance 3D, Express, Firefly, Frame.io, Stock, Behance, Adobe Fonts, Fresco)  <br>**Document Cloud** (Acrobat, Acrobat Sign, Acrobat AI Assistant, PDF Tools)</td>
<td>Core subscription business focused on individual creators, designers, photographers, and SMBs. Now deeply integrated with generative AI features via Firefly and Acrobat AI.</td>
<td>**\$4.46 B** for Q3 FY25 → **≈ \$18 B annualized**</td>
<td>**≈ +12 % YoY**</td>
<td>Growth fueled by Firefly adoption, Express/Frame.io expansion, and Acrobat AI monetization.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Digital Experience <br>**(\~25% of Rev)</td>
<td>**Experience Cloud** (Experience Manager, Journey Optimizer, Real-Time CDP, Campaign, Target, Analytics, Commerce, Workfront)  <br>**GenStudio** (Performance Marketing, Firefly Services, AI-powered content supply chain)  <br>**Experience Platform (AEP)**</td>
<td>Enterprise marketing, analytics, commerce, and personalization software. Increasingly positioned as the “content supply chain” layer for enterprises, powered by Firefly and GenStudio.</td>
<td>**\$1.48 B** for Q3 FY25 → **≈ \$6 B annualized**</td>
<td>**≈ +9 % YoY**</td>
<td>Fastest-growing enterprise unit. Benefiting from GenStudio adoption and AI-based marketing workflows; strong ARR expansion in subscription subsegment (\~ +11 % YoY).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Print & Publishing (Legacy)**</td>
<td>FrameMaker, RoboHelp, ColdFusion, Technical Communication Suite, legacy PostScript / eLearning tools</td>
<td>Legacy desktop publishing and print technology; now a small tail business supporting long-standing customers.</td>
<td>**≈ \$275 M annualized**</td>
<td>**Declining (-2 % to -5 %)**</td>
<td>Minimal strategic investment; mostly maintenance revenue.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Other / Services & Support**</td>
<td>Consulting, implementation services, enterprise support, partner integrations, model-hosting services (Firefly/NVIDIA), and training</td>
<td>Professional services supporting deployment of Adobe solutions; smaller consumption-based elements like Firefly credit packs and enterprise service contracts.</td>
<td>**≈ \$100–150 M** (est.)</td>
<td>**Flat / low growth**</td>
<td>Acts as an enabler for Experience Cloud and GenStudio adoption; margin-accretive but not a growth engine.</td>
</tr>
	</table>

# Adobe Creative Cloud Pro for Teams - Complete Application & Feature Table
<table header-row="true">

<td>**Photoshop**</td>

<td>Single User</td>
<td>Desktop</td>

<td>Single User</td>

<td>Collaboration</td>
<td>Desktop</td>

ID: 2bcc3702-22d5-805d-869b-e207d7bbd022

================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/2bcc370222d5805d869be207d7bbd022 as of 2026-04-17T13:55:58.070Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/2bcc370222d5805d869be207d7bbd022">
<ancestor-path></ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Adobe Pro Proposal"}
</properties>
<content>
---
<callout color="gray_bg">
	**Context:** A summary of a deal for Adobe to license our Flex model (external proposal linked below) specifically for use with Enterprise customers within Foundry. 💡<span color="green">***What has been added: non-negotiables and commercial tiers based on their desired terms.***</span>
	<unknown url="https://www.notion.so/2bcc370222d5805d869be207d7bbd022#30dc370222d580a69198ea4db7a6ab5c" alt="alias"/>
</callout>
## **A. Overall Value & Risks**
<table header-row="true" header-column="true">
<colgroup>
<col width="78.66192626953125">
<col width="438.66192626953125">
<col width="418.66192626953125">
</colgroup>
<tr>
<td></td>
<td>Opportunities</td>
<td>Risks</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>BFL</td>
<td>  • Adobe is one of a small handful of companies where BFL can strike a large closed-weights deal. <br>  • There is strong product-market fit between Flux models and Adobe's product surfaces, and given industry trends Adobe is in a highly likely position to pay a substantial fee for frontier model weights. <br>  • BFL cannot efficiently serve Adobe's end-customer base directly at scale, so a single high-value agreement with Adobe allows BFL to capture that market through one commercial relationship rather than fragmenting across thousands of ENT accounts.</td>
<td>  • Adobe products still hold significant market share in creative tooling. While usage of traditional creative software is at risk as image and video models get better, there is a substantial lag in market adoption of models that could give Adobe a durable advantage if allowed to distribute fine-tuned versions of our models. <br>  • Given Adobe's penetration of the ENT customer segment (some that directly overlap with BFL's current and target base) allowing broad distribution of Pro model derivates could cut off a large portion of both BFL's commercial licensing and API business). <br>  • The scope restrictions in this deal exist specifically to prevent this outcome.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Adobe</td>
<td>  • Allows Adobe to fine-tune a SOTA model on ENT customer data to deliver on key Foundry workflows. <br>  • Adobe already has existing ENT customers and relationships, and this deal allows them to deliver: 1) a differentiated, brand-safe generative capability on top of those accounts, 2) post-training and fine-tuning flexibility that no API-only arrangement provides, and 3) ENT customer brand customization at scale, which is the core commercial promise of Foundry.</td>
<td>  • Dependence on a 3P model for a flagship ENT product surface creates continuity and roadmap risk. <br>  • Adobe also needs attribution flexibility to market Foundry as a proprietary product, which creates tension with our interest in brand visibility. <br>  • And the scope of what Adobe can do with distribution, derivatives, white-labeling needs to be permissive enough for Adobe to build and market a real product.</td>
</tr>
</table>
## **B. Adobe’s Feedback & Our Recommendations**
**Current Status:** They have continued to stress the urgency of the deal, although we’re not sure what is driving this (e.g., turning our draft in a matter of days, which is unusual). They have positioned this deal as a "Strategic Partnership" designation alongside Google, Runway, Microsoft, and OpenAI, but this is not reflected in their changes to the agreement. 
***⚠️ The latest from my call w/ Ersin last night (April 9th)***
---
<table>
<colgroup>
<col width="132.66192626953125">
<col width="732.6604919433594">
<col width="321.6666666666667">
</colgroup>
<tr>
<td><span color="red">**Deal-blockers**</span></td>
<td>**  • White-labeling: **Despite Adobe legal scrapping all co-marketing commitments, he said he'll do joint PR, publicly position Foundry as collaborating with Black Forest Labs, and even offer BFL-labeled base models alongside white-labeled derivatives for customers who want them. ***He specifically wants fine-tuned models built on customer data to be white-labeled, ***since he views those as containing Adobe and customer IP, not just BFL's.<br><br>**  • Distribution: **Adobe legal removed our language limiting distribution, and Ersin himself admitted that “Foundry” is just a construct. **"Foundry-only" is enterprise-private, not product-surface-limited.** Ersin's understanding of Foundry scope = private to specific enterprise customers, not accessible to the general subscriber base. But ***he can't commit to limiting which Adobe application surfaces an enterprise customer uses to access their endpoint*** (e.g., a Photoshop panel for that specific enterprise). Amanda pushed back that powering multiple workflows across multiple products for a single customer is still distribution — even at 50 seats. Ersin acknowledged the distinction and agreed to tier that separately.</td>
<td></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><span color="green">***Nice-to-haves***</span></td>
<td>**  • Beyond Foundry Distribution & Internal Use: He proactively proposed a second tier for GA consumer surfaces.** Without much prompting, Ersin suggested structuring the agreement with a base enterprise tier (Foundry/derivatives, white-label + co-marketing) and an optional second tier that kicks in if any Adobe product team wants to deploy a derivative in consumer-facing GA products (CC apps, Firefly subscriber base, etc.). He acknowledged this would reach hundreds of millions of users and warrants meaningfully higher commercials. He even suggested measuring it by number of deployed model instances as a scale limiter.</td>
<td></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Other clarifications**</td>
<td>**  • He's only interested in derivatives for Foundry, not hosted-as-is.** Ersin confirmed he will not host \[Flex\] as-is,

## Infinite Canvas as AIs True Creative Interface
Picture the scene: You are art directing a brand pitch. Two monitors. On the left, you are generating images on the playground. On the right: Claude for prompt refinement, Figma for layout, a browser tab running an upscaler. Four tools. One brain. Zero flow state.
<br>Then it happens! After fourteen attempts, finally - the first decent image. Exact mood, exact light. You push your luck and try one more seed. Version 15 is a miss. Version 16 is worse. You want to go back, but the playground doesnt keep history - every session starts blank, and anything you didnt manually save is simply gone in a endless long history.
<br>Ten minutes of reconstructing prompts from memory later or ten minutes of scrolling down the playground until you find the image - your momentum is ***dead.***
<br>This is where professional AI design actually lives right now. The models themselves are extraordinary with photorealistic output in seconds. But the (most) interfaces wrapping them.. They are chat windows. Support ticket boxes basically.
We accepted the chatbox because the models were new and the outputs were miraculous enough that the interface didnt matter. That period is over since last year. The models are now amazing and the prompt box has become the bottleneck it always was. Designing complex layered visual work through a chat window is the wrong tool for the job - in the same way a telephone is the wrong tool for architecture. *You can communicate ideas through it. You cannot build in it.*
Designing visually complex, multi-layered work through a single text prompt in a chatbox is the wrong tool for the job and the infinite canvas is the only one that makes sense (right now).
## **The Problem Space**
The productivity wall isnt coming from the models. Flux and other Models have never been more capable. The wall is coming from the medium and the medium right now is fundamentally broken for visual work. 
### **The Linear Trap of Chatbots and Historys**
The most glaring flaw of traditional LLM interfaces and image generation tools (like Midjourney, our Playground or other Examples) is that they operate on a strictly chronological, linear timeline. You input a prompt at the bottom, the output appears, and the entire conversation shifts upward.
But design doesnt work that way. <span discussion-urls="discussion://343c3702-22d5-8047-a395-001c4c547f31">Design is spatial. It branches.</span> It is an <span discussion-urls="discussion://343c3702-22d5-80f8-8a91-001cbe8f412e">iterative</span> spatial and branching process.
A real creative process looks nothing like a chat thread. You generate a baseline, then split into three color directions. You take the second direction and explore four different compositional approaches. Then you pull back and hold the result next to the original to feel the distance traveled. That back and forth that simultaneous comparison thats where the actual thinking happens.
A chat thread and our playground do not support this. In a chat interface, previous iterations are buried under the weight of new ones. In the <span discussion-urls="discussion://33cc3702-22d5-80a1-8818-001c6dc6540b">Play</span>ground, the history disappears after a page reload, and bugs in the history feature prevent you from finding it again. This creates a severe psychological friction known as **Scroll Fatigue**. I find myself constantly scrolling up and down, trying to mentally hold the context of "Version 4" while looking at "Version 47." The interface treats every generation as a disposable, isolated transaction rather than a piece of a larger, evolving puzzle.
### **Context Switching and the Fragmented Mind**
Because the chatbox cannot serve as a workspace and professionals are forced into what we call the "Frankenstein Workflow."
You use a textbased AI to brainstorm the brief. You use a linear Chatbot to generate the raw imagery. You use a web-based Upscale. You use a heavy desktop software to composite the image. you use Slack to share the Images. 
This forces th

{"title":"An Introduction to Generative Models via the BFL API, our Playground and forestlabs.black"}

<content>
✨ 🌲
## Intro
The goal of generative modeling is to learn the underlying structure of a given data distribution, and to sample from it. If this learning process was successful, a generative model can be used to create entirely new, unseen samples from that distribution. This can, for example, be used to create new images from textual descriptions or “prompts”, or other images (think “img2video”).
The following tasks aim to teach about generative model capabilities that are currently available via the [BFL API](https://docs.bfl.ml/).
## What is an API?
An API (Application Programming Interface) functions like a universal translator and a delivery system for software. When two programs need to communicate with each other, the API provides a standardized way to package and exchange data between them. Each request usually comprises three key elements:<br>- **Endpoint**: the destination address to which the request is sent.<br>- **Proper formatting**: Using standardized data formats to ensure that both programs understand the data being exchanged.<br>- **Authentication**: Verifying that you have authorization to access the API, often through methods such as API keys or OAuth tokens.
For example, for a text-to-image API:
- **Request**: you send a text description such as “sunset over the mountains” along with your API key to the API endpoint.
- **Validation**: The API checks your API key to confirm that you are authorized to use the service.
- **Processing**: The request is processed by the image generation model, which creates an image based on your description.
- **Response**: The API returns the generated image or an error message if something went wrong, e.g. an invalid API key or an unclear description.<br><br>*“Sunset over the mountains”*, generated with **FLUX1.1 \[pro\] Ultra (raw) **

<empty-block/>
This standardized communication channel allows thousands of different applications to reliably request image generation without having to understand the complex AI model running behind the scenes. In addition, standardized error messages and code help developers quickly identify and resolve issues, making API integration efficient and robust.
![](https://prod-files-secure.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/d47c9781-7a36-4f60-98ec-c16fd7b286a6/5897ca0c-16f0-485a-9ea1-16672440a2f9/api_diagram_instructive.svg?X-Amz-Algorithm=AWS4-HMAC-SHA256&X-Amz-Content-Sha256=UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD&X-Amz-Credential=ASIAZI2LB466ZJCKGI4H%2F20260419%2Fus-west-2%2Fs3%2Faws4_request&X-Amz-Date=20260419T151922Z&X-Amz-Expires=3600&X-Amz-Security-Token=IQoJb3JpZ2luX2VjED8aCXVzLXdlc3QtMiJIMEYCIQD0VbvbO8djOeW0rThx8glcgP3AmXue4GEnVZu17R8CygIhAIwrQP7OuPMdgSkYaZo9mjM4HSR5rCKYxmhlcBidpBHvKv8DCAgQABoMNjM3NDIzMTgzODA1IgyrIp5Z1aE3M51liKwq3APeK7c%2BwtY3roJIYc6OGODXVcSsE3YWp6traQGILUz3Q2ZLSltzcjO93PW11HlFWvhqf2UwxNxvNcXwaoG%2BoE0CZ9k%2BCPnAbL5hDzc4UGvEDsDL8huunJrQ9TKQZhnxMenm%2FAUGRHarVaNP%2Bpyuw8MBPUJLOIMUBBNu4X2OLCDVAFm49kP9MvBOQK%2FZLTgkEI%2BIg1DyLCkWAxVd9KKYlrjoZzOqF%2F%2BRcFbyL0Usd5UKKemSmNdNVqX4sR0XNEms1X5mmLPps77keSgB3R20SVTCtQ55TCw5%2BuvhkO5xgCmMOVgGGfS6%2BOBLC%2BRtnuh46%2BieKGOInlrLgzQmD0BOsuvHLBtrXvWFE6D5WXKg7C9at9FXuD5BtHXX5SzNPZekVSFJtxvGK3y3T9YZwfkxt2x2cnXiGIpz1J5kAn2EWu9sMXByJr4AOH1iTxbiJKpEhPwbtXi9wEqz5XN1S8%2BI4OFeFHjwmuxIookUECCcwpPjHLjN%2F9PGIQwnKMXIMXzt6mbP%2BN8Bj%2BZU9g71k0yO%2B0MciJS02rlWpZz7BjYcwDOkl54tzGHAr%2BRzd8nsdecmiyBUn8IA9116535COMeu7pKWQecjNVafHHBtgUJrlkHhcRX5ZR0hkampvQkPrzDuzpPPBjqkAfRpe94uZy7MtsZ2y0vUYkssoTvR9%2FFG4b53%2Fg8WtEdFbr2kkAcaEj4fAojxeELMU6OKBepQfW%2F3tqOqlyrpB8EU846wXV%2F%2F39xENMboxtUo4HbWztG%2Fii3y50hiho%2BovH37s7E%2Fo4%2BDnCCLwtuJvPLIVJSJ3g66djbgod4ocdmhNut2ObfjJyd3mM83BNDs0eQGFbG%2FVwLiP4dsDpv2YxJGxHvS&X-Amz-Signature=da616c4737741ac

<properties>
{"title":"API Customers with special data handling"}
</properties>
<content>
Learn more about our default policies on handling personal customer data [here](/26fc370222d580e9bca6dd04bb91ba5d?pvs=25)
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	With some customers we have zero data retention and no training clauses in place. This page is a tracker shared between Legal, Engineering and GTM to track these cases and make sure we implement special data privacy policies for customers with whom we have contractually agreed to it. 
</callout>
In our default API contract templates we keep the flexibility to both save personal customer data, use it for analytics (e.g. for improving our services), and also to train AI models on it. 
However, **in reality we don’t train on any user input or output data so far (as of Sept. 2025)** so we can principally agree to not training on customer data if one really wants (we can use this to trade against other things in deals). 
Some customer even require stricter privacy policies and want us to **delete input text data directly when generating the result.** We refer to this as **zero data retention policy. **
> \[[This agreement overview](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1_EM_z6LHWPMwibWFDGbeZX2PFYaDSh-x/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=117009868738180578161&rtpof=true&sd=true) maintained by the legal team also contains information on data handling.\]

- Let’s Encrypt issue:
	- Major issue is free service with rate limits

- Compute
	- Half of us3 was given to research
	- We are losing eu1
	- What are the plans to acquire more compute? 
		- reviews already struggling to deploy 
		- Consistent gpu pressure
		- Optimizing scaling is helpful but in large burst of traffic + the additional traffic coming from new customers we need to have headroom to serve and scale. We have added low priority to allow other teams to take advantage of free gpus at lower production traffic

# Where we're going
![](https://prod-files-secure.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/d47c9781-7a36-4f60-98ec-c16fd7b286a6/906aee77-a843-43f8-82fb-fd7845b07616/image.png?X-Amz-Algorithm=AWS4-HMAC-SHA256&X-Amz-Content-Sha256=UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD&X-Amz-Credential=ASIAZI2LB466T4YB26F6%2F20260419%2Fus-west-2%2Fs3%2Faws4_request&X-Amz-Date=20260419T151937Z&X-Amz-Expires=3600&X-Amz-Security-Token=IQoJb3JpZ2luX2VjED8aCXVzLXdlc3QtMiJHMEUCICNbwXh0aW9j98TinV0dtmAAn1BNLhp8%2BRfEi2lbTsPcAiEAh9ujqp%2FGiHZi1rZWVF9ASiEH5ObwG8DjEykVj77ZWVAq%2FwMICBAAGgw2Mzc0MjMxODM4MDUiDNKpXuCSLUQ%2FfMkrvCrcA57MHcOalb9myLRgaMibNiiELAkvcjk3INMBhk%2BGiMn4Evbhs0dNEXKdBQx5jGg8UttZuR8v5q5rCDMj4gzjjnAQS2utT9xBr92jf6U4j%2F6Q1RVVxTTTTdwofv3yUjUECewcpvRjgeYLSIoGxYZDG4%2FQu6ju5QEhfVAGd8rMP%2Bp%2BgiHUPZH%2FYJ8U9IeySIWgkrf%2FcxTc8R2gmMXTi0i7tpLk%2FXlT8emj2OTJz1oHwdZDMwLwHFbIIMP1toaWdgiarkHMKCL%2BWwQrz%2B8540b8vFI%2B%2FvbvPPbK0S4uk%2BeEEe1bSvU%2B1q1vvyK%2FWAa%2BiZaKl2dxCU3WprVJZrBOgWYj3UwL7%2FyFymM4apCpm7boRPvtuIHOAjRe2GDhB%2BsXzK5TquPRQv7XSGYndAowNuFtvFfqGF9EB1C6QtIYZ9i35zXigcPlX8Je4vJqrUQaGnVk03LFzq5bHOPh2iM8zCCjvfpww%2FhWVuldt0kVMn1cChrZK2Cgru5%2BmMQXymW3QPuGCHDU9zJ5S6LbRDGu4a1Or5jc6O9VkKmJQXkUPwZNXriGaQqF6q8A6%2BEIbSsNifzsaEND61cql9wNIXJen27WhE%2BFIhFVhN67uCwX63AIHKebdM1ZTe9nWFCL9QpOMNfPk88GOqUBG%2BVShPJIagUI7qsSCJKDXzCcQPlU2t4K2r0Q7dAM92RjDXKAQANFiOxmvnslORI%2BKiBphtmXBdOUIuAQDXNIg3z1aemtf5h31DqnhH9KOQvf5bMgWv6ifoU%2FkqGDUnG%2Bw7utR7QqKgj2q5H1%2FflLSdlVpCij3elmGVLE%2FoigdHAwYA6ZfyoIeXV3mU0PlBQhmN7bSKqpLPsu3AnWizYBj03wppQW&X-Amz-Signature=68e9c9d42aa2671f0d6313342c6b93b49dcc21e6c80b72ec05959c50126844e0&X-Amz-SignedHeaders=host&x-amz-checksum-mode=ENABLED&x-id=GetObject)
We're not an image gen company. We're the frontier AI research lab powering visual intelligence and we need the world to understand that.
By end of 2026 we want people to know three things about BFL: 
- We ship world-class research at commercial speed
- We're the default for frontier devs and creatives because we’re uniquely suited to their needs (best models, open weights, open source, customization tools like LoRas, API is world class, FLUX Agent, multi-cloud, no lock-in)
- We're the foundational AI lab building the future of visual intelligence (why it’s important, what it unlocks, why we’re uniquely positioned to do it, launch of FLUX.3)
We are not "the open-source Midjourney." 
We’re not the cheaper Google alternative. 
We are not the sovereign alternative.
---
# What changed at the offsite
<columns>
	<column>
		We agreed to stop being reactive and start being intentional. That means: 
		- Quality awareness over quantity
		- More voices from the team (more BFL faces and not only Robin & Andi)
		- Becoming more visual 
		- More visible to the people who matter
		- Consistent presence on our own social channels, and leaning into the angles that make us unique: strength of the research team, open research / tech, 70 people vs big tech. 
	</column>
	<column>
		![](file://%7B%22source%22%3A%22attachment%3Af51fbf4c-118c-43a4-a3f8-5fd2f4f64ff7%3Aimage_(37).png%22%2C%22permissionRecord%22%3A%7B%22table%22%3A%22block%22%2C%22id%22%3A%2231ec3702-22d5-80c0-9b24-d224eb5d369f%22%2C%22spaceId%22%3A%22d47c9781-7a36-4f60-98ec-c16fd7b286a6%22%7D%7D)
	</column>
</columns>
It's not just one person doing comms and marketing at the same time. We can move to a marketing-led comms strategy. Not top-down, old-school PR - marketing drives the narrative, the positioning, the story.
**We need to clearly articulate why visual intelligence matters **and we need to do that before FLUX.3 lands, because FLUX.3 is when we can actually demonstrate the expanded scope.
---
# What we're shipping in the next weeks
The goal is to build momentum and shift the narrative before FLUX.3
**KV Cache launch (next week):** Klein 9B, 1.4–2x faster for image editing. NVIDIA co-launch confirmed, they're including us in their press release and channels.
**"How BFL approaches research":** A blog about our open research philosophy.  Show that we ship research and share it, when people join BFL, they can continue shipping research. Follows Self-Flow and the new research setup that encourages open publication.
**FDE work showcase:** The solutions team builds custom LoRAs (Amazon Ads) that beats NB pro. Show to the world that BFL is also more than just a research lab and we can help companies deliver real values. 
**BFL Vision: Why Visual Intelligence Matters:** Builds on Robin's North Star doc. Visual models are getting dramatically more general, agentic applications are exploding, and we're defining what visual intelligence looks like. This reframes us from "image gen" to "visual intelligence infrastructure." Needs to land big before FLUX.3
**Scorsese campaign: **Top-of-funnel branding. Expands our reach beyond the early adopter crowd.
**These need to be sequenced together, not run independently.** See <mention-page url="https://www.notion.so/f1ffd95f1d6e4c6fb376aa31312325d7"/> 
---

<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/287c370222d580868d37ff04b46d1485" title="Strategic Planning & Operating model"/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580268496de1d8a4ab2ec" title=""/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5808facd7c952b579a4cd" title=""/>

<properties>
{"title":"BFL Operating Model"}
</properties>
<content>
# Role of Operations at BFL {toggle="true"}
	<empty-block/>
	**Why now? **At 20 people, the CEO *is* the operating system. At 70+, that breaks. You're in every room, every decision, every hire conversation. This doc is the plan to get you out of the bottleneck so you can focus on what only you can do.
	- operations at BFL means turning our money in the bank into \$XM ARR by managing the **company's three biggest cost centers (compute and people, followed by data)**…while building the systems that let X people ship faster with x less headcount than incumbents
	- It’s operating system that governs how compute, people, data and risk are converted into frontier progress
	- If compute is our factory, Ops runs the factory: deciding what gets built, when, at what cost, and under what rules
	- **(1) The 3 main spend items**
		- Compute and infra: largest burn, the finance component. Compute is not a fixed cost, it's a strategic weapon to us. Ops owns provider strategy, training vs. inference allocation, and capacity planning tied to the model roadmap. Every dollar misallocated here is a model that ships late or ships worse.
		- People (second largest, fastest growing cost). the people we're hiring, frontier ML researchers are the scarcest talent pool. We're competing against orgs with 100x our headcount and 10x our compensation budgets for the same \~200 people who can push the frontier. Ops ensures every hire is strategic, not reactive, and that we build comp and talent systems that let us win despite the asymmetry.
		- Data (third largest, most underleveraged cost): training data is the “raw material” of the factory. Today we procure it reactively, without taxonomy, without feedback loops, and without a sourcing strategy beyond a handful of large providers. The wrong dataset costs model performance and release cadence. The right dataset at the right time is a competitive moat
	- **(2) The operational system layer = what makes spend productive**
		- **Compute governance.** Provider strategy, training vs. inference allocation, capacity planning tied to the FLUX 3 schedule. Compute is our single largest line item and every allocation decision is a bet on what ships next. Ops ensures those bets are deliberate, tracked, and tied to the model roadmap rather than made ad hoc
		- **Capital & talent deployment =** **Raise, allocate and track. **Fundraising and strategic finance - investor pipeline, fundraise execution, due diligence, strategic partnerships. Hiring strategy, comp systems, resource allocation, budget pacing, forecast accuracy, board reporting. The full lifecycle of capital operationalised: bring it in, deploy it across compute, people, and data, report on it. Essentially turning CEO decisions into tracked, measurable execution
		- **Research reality translation.** The specific interfaces that turn research breakthroughs into shippable products: the Research to Product bridge meeting, launch coordination, and the customer feedback → research loop. This includes the data pipeline (procurement → sourcing → evaluation → feedback to research) and the infrastructure that lets BFL expand into new modalities without reinventing the process each time. Today this is ad hoc and person-dependent. The ops job is to make it a repeatable pipeline in 2026.
		- **Cross-functional coherence & velocity.** The function-by-function interface model: Ops ↔ Finance, Ops ↔ People, Ops ↔ GTM, Ops ↔ Product/Eng. Launch coordination, bottleneck removal, decision rights. The reason models ship in weeks after completion, not months.
		- **Decision architecture & operating model.** The weekly/monthly/quarterly cadence that operationalises strategy: frequent leadership sync, monthly strategy day, monthly finance review, quarterly OKR cycle to see if we are off or on track. The infrastructure that lets people operate as one company without the CEO in every room.
		- **Corporate operations.** Legal, IT/security, compliance, external comms and employer brand. The "keep the lights on" layer that scales us quietly or breaks loudly.
	**<br>Ops doesn't do any function's job. Ops makes every function's job possible, measurable, and coordinated so BFL operates as one company, not \~7 teams that share a Slack workspace**
	<empty-block/>
	The job is ensuring: **Inputs are strong **→ **Engine runs fast **→ **Outputs improve**

PAGE 37: [page] Bi-annual reviews 
ID: 25cc3702-22d5-80fb-809f-f914b63fa22a

================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/25cc370222d580fb809ff914b63fa22a as of 2026-02-19T09:32:03.989Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/25cc370222d580fb809ff914b63fa22a">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/15dc370222d580b39ad8c587d2fc9747" title="Review Approach and Guide "/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/2557c22b137f4ac7965faa353f332dfb" title=""/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/d0817b0493e14f569d8ea2d24e3cb267" title=""/>

<properties>
{"title":"Bi-annual reviews"}
</properties>
<content>
## **Bi-annual feedback coupled with salary review**
- BFL has two structured feedback sessions per year, one from mid March to early April and the other from mid September to early October. Both be coupled with a formal salary review (the process for salary reviews are covered in a separate document).
- The feedback format for these is more structured than in the moment/real time feedback. This is to enable reflection of impact and performance over the last 6 months.
- If an employee has been with BFL for 3 full months by the time the performance review, they should have a review but it’s highly unlikely it will result in a salary increase
- The process will consist of the following elements:
	- Self review
	- Functional lead review
	- Review meeting to discuss feedback
	- Calibration (Leadership and Functional Leads)
	- Comp review
- The different reviews and the calibration cover similar areas and should focus on the last 6 months
- Reviews are based on impact and impact potential rather than experience/performance and will hopefully drive both individuals and leads to focus on the work that has had the most impact on the business and team.
- **Use this levelling **[guide](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/11cFz0x9HfIvfT2aV7KhT7wG7yR5yyMQES7fzSUY2QaI/edit?gid=405978997#gid=405978997) **for reference**
**Process**
1. [**Self review**](https://docs.google.com/forms/d/1B_9uFlSAIMAcl3Jib3pyUBQ1TIGK4z8XE2ELREqnuRQ/edit)
- Each employee completes a brief self-reflection along with career aspirations
- The self review will be shared with the reviewer ahead of the review date
1. **Lead assessment**
- Lead assesses their team member using:
	- Levelling [guide](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/11cFz0x9HfIvfT2aV7KhT7wG7yR5yyMQES7fzSUY2QaI/edit?gid=405978997#gid=405978997), own feedback and peer feedback (if needed for blind spots)
	- The purpose of the Lead review is to evaluate impact and impact trajectory, not effort or intent.
	- [Feedback pointers](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1vx4Fs2xfLCCDiRJS-_XsnjWYN-JBdDA8r_dFO8HU5OA/edit?tab=t.0#heading=h.i1aeisrgt138)
1. **Peer feedback optional**
- It’s the responsibility of each Lead to ensure they give well-rounded feedback to whoever they are reviewing. That most likely means they need to get input from a range of team members. 
1. **Review meeting**
- After the self and reviews are done and any potential peer feedback has been collected, the Lead books a meeting with their direct report to go through the feedback.
1. **Calibration (Leadership, Lead + People)**
- In the calibration session, Leads discuss each team members ratings. 
- The goals of calibration is to align on
	- Impact
	- Trajectory
	- Relative performance
- The session is NOT for storytelling instead:
	- Ensuring rating are consistent
	- Evidence-based
	- Consistent across teams
**Useful tips**
- Collect notes/examples in whatever way works for you throughout the 6 months just after one review and before the next.
- The notes can be super simple and should really just act as prompts for you. This will enable you to compile your review (whether self, lead or inner circle) quicker when you get to it and also avoid just giving examples of things that happened in the last few weeks (recency effect)
</content>
</page>

## 🛠️ How to Book via Google Calendar
1. Open **Google Calendar** and create a new event (or open an existing one).
2. Click **"Add rooms or location"** under the event details.
3. In the room search, browse or search the available rooms — each listing shows:
	- **Capacity** (e.g. "Room for 4 people")
	- **Equipment** (monitor and/or whiteboard, if available)
4. Select the room that best fits your needs and save the event.
5. You'll receive a confirmation if the room is available. If it's already booked, try an alternative.

## Ready-to-use narrative snippets
*Long form Narrative *[*here*](/27ec370222d5802eacd7d29555096d46?pvs=25)
## Company intro snippets {toggle="true"}
	**Ultra-short (1 line)**
	- Black Forest Labs – the frontier AI research lab for visual intelligence
	- We exist to power visual intelligence everywhere.
	- From Freiburg and San Francisco, we're redefining how the world is seen and understood
	**Short (2-3 lines)**
	- Black Forest Labs is a frontier AI research lab focused on visual intelligence. Our team pioneered Latent Diffusion, Stable Diffusion, and FLUX.1 – Today, these foundations power millions of creations worldwide, from individual artists to enterprise applications.
	- From our labs in Freiburg and San Francisco, we're building the foundational technology for the next era of creativity and visual understanding.
	**Medium (paragraph)**
	- Black Forest Labs is a frontier AI research lab building the foundational technology for visual intelligence. Our team pioneered breakthrough innovations including Latent Diffusion, Stable Diffusion, and FLUX.1 – models that now power products used by millions across creative tools, enterprise platforms, and social media. Rooted in foundational research and driven by relentless curiosity, we're not here to iterate on what exists – we're here to build what's next.
## What we do snippets {toggle="true"}
	**Product-focused**
	- We build AI models that turn imagination into reality – with precision, speed, and creative control
	- Our technology powers everything from creative tools to enterprise platforms, enabling anyone to create, scale, and govern visual content
	**Technology-focused**
	- We build multimodal foundation models that unify perception, generation, memory, and reasoning
	- Our research advances visual understanding – making powerful, controllable, and composable AI genuinely useful
	**Impact-focused**
	- We're making the visual world programmable – turning ideas that cameras can't capture into images, videos, and experiences
	- Our technology enables millions of people to fulfill their creative potential and enterprises to scale visual content with confidence
## Why it matters {toggle="true"}
	AI is transforming how we experience, interact with, and contribute to the visual world. Our technology communicates the ideas that cameras can’t capture. There’s a blockbuster film, video game, graphic novel, couture dress, soaring cathedral, and creative campaign in everyone - we help them realize it.
	<br>**Proof point:** partnerships. In just over a year since founding, our models power everything from creative tools (e.g. Adobe) to design software (e.g. architecture / gaming firm) and social media experiences (e.g. Meta). 
## Why we will win {toggle="true"}
	<empty-block/>
	**We’re frontier. **Black Forest Labs is building the foundational infrastructure for visual intelligence. Blending European research with American dynamism, we develop powerful, extensible, and global technology that pushes the capability frontier.
	- **Proof point: performance. Upon release, our models top public benchmarks for quality, control, and efficiency (e.g. ImgSys). Demand for these models and services is generating sustainable revenue to support our frontier research.<br>**
	**We’re pioneers. **Our founding team includes pioneers of powerful (latent diffusion), accessible (Stable Diffusion), and controllable (FLUX.1) visual AI. With a track record of breakthrough research, our small but exceptional team of \~50 rivals some of the largest companies in history.
	- **Proof point: market share. By user (e.g. Poe) and deployer (e.g. Artificial Analysis) share, we compete with Google and OpenAI, and surpass Alibaba, Tencent, and Bytedance.<br>**
	**We’re open. **We are deeply committed to open innovation. By sharing open technology, we enable researchers and developers around the world to build, test, and deploy new tools that we can scarcely imagine today. We apply layers of mitigation before, during, and after every release to combat the misuse of AI.
	- **Proof point: community. In the global AI race, our open models are the only open American or European models to rival DeepSeek for popularity (e.g. HF likes). Our founding team has contributed open generative models that total over 400M downloads—more than Google and Microsoft combined (e.g. HF total downloads).**
## Where we are going {toggle="true"}
	We are developing multimodal models that unify perception, generation, memory, and reasoning - laying the foundations for visual intelligence that responds to human intent. 
### **Use this as foundation for all writing tasks:**
`You are writing for Black Forest Labs (BFL), a frontier AI research lab building foundational visual intelligence infrastructure. We merge European research excellence with American dynamism to create powerful, extensible, and global technology.<br><br>BRAND FOUNDATION:<br>- We're frontier: Building state-of-the-art visual AI models that top public benchmarks<br>- We're pioneers: Our founding team created latent diffusion, Stable Diffusion, and FLUX.1 and Flux.2<br>- We're open: Deeply committed to open innovation while applying rigorous safety mitigations<br>- We're competitive: By market share, we rival Google and OpenAI<br><br>VOICE PRINCIPLES:<br>1. Forward Over Backward - Frame as possibility, not achievement<br>2. Active Over Passive - Use dynamic verbs that convey motion<br>3. Precise Yet Profound - Make every word cou

<page url="https://www.notion.so/32fc370222d5808097d5ef38f990116c">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/32fc370222d5800fa01cfef771495be4" title="Data Infrastructure — Hiring Guide"/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/1a1c370222d5807db2edf4324f3c7723" title="JDs, SCORECARDS AND PROCESSES"/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/1a1c370222d5802d92f1fc32471508f0" title="People/Talent resources "/>
<ancestor-4-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580268496de1d8a4ab2ec" title=""/>
<ancestor-5-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5808facd7c952b579a4cd" title=""/>
<ancestor-6-database url="https://www.notion.so/11ec370222d58020ad93e98fe0cc4607" title="Company Home"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Coding — Interview Guide (Data Infra)"}
</properties>
<content>
## Purpose
Evaluate practical coding ability + systems thinking in a data / infrastructure context.
This is NOT a pure LeetCode interview.
Focus on:
- Writing clean, correct code
- Reasoning about constraints (memory, I/O, scale)
- Ability to evolve a naive solution into a production-ready one
## Problem Options (Pick ONE per interview)
Interviewers should choose ONE problem based on candidate background.
### Option 1: Duplicate File Detection
Best for: Data systems, storage, I/O-heavy candidates
Focus:
- File I/O patterns
- Memory constraints
- Multi-stage filtering (size → partial hash → full hash)
- Scaling to large datasets
Reference: : [https://docs.google.com/document/d/1BculOrdJpFwSJ-2YDv_PuRfvQyJoaDgLkE5O5oiJg6w/edit?usp=sharing](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1BculOrdJpFwSJ-2YDv_PuRfvQyJoaDgLkE5O5oiJg6w/edit?usp=sharing)
### Option 2: Container Distribution
Best for: Infra / distributed systems candidates
Focus:
- Bottlenecks (registry bandwidth)
- Distributed coordination
- Peer-to-peer / tree distribution
- Failure handling
Reference: : [https://docs.google.com/document/d/1DKVWkG0ANemMbnI-gPpZeJcwmU5JJBoHkWf8iV2G0lo/edit?usp=sharing](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1DKVWkG0ANemMbnI-gPpZeJcwmU5JJBoHkWf8iV2G0lo/edit?usp=sharing)
### Option 3: AllReduce
Best for: ML infra / training systems candidates
Focus:
- Distributed communication patterns
- Tradeoffs (latency vs bandwidth)
- Correctness + deadlock awareness
Reference: : [https://docs.google.com/document/d/1L21_Lvx9crh5yZeHDnZXnTyrMKzk5yFysVj56k2_EZU/edit?usp=sharing](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1L21_Lvx9crh5yZeHDnZXnTyrMKzk5yFysVj56k2_EZU/edit?usp=sharing)
### Option 4: Streaming Data Pipeline
Best for: Data infra + ML pipeline candidates
Focus:
- Streaming vs batch
- Memory + chunking
- GPU starvation / pipeline design
- Reliability + checkpointing
Reference: : [https://docs.google.com/document/d/1Sy1TZEK_8iipxo-FL0jbKu_oOdrh-EoF9ovbdVXQYOM/edit?usp=sharing](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1Sy1TZEK_8iipxo-FL0jbKu_oOdrh-EoF9ovbdVXQYOM/edit?usp=sharing)
### Option 5: Pipeline Scheduling (1F1B)
Best for: deeper ML systems / parallelism candidates
Focus:
- Scheduling + dependencies
- Resource utilization (bubbles)
- System-level reasoning
Reference: : [https://docs.google.com/document/d/1irVX3XhsM1zT_IFE4O1bIH-dY_77r-jayGmVzPUO8IM/edit?usp=sharing](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1irVX3XhsM1zT_IFE4O1bIH-dY_77r-jayGmVzPUO8IM/edit?usp=sharing)
## Evaluation Criteria
Strong Yes:
- Writes clean, correct code quickly
- Identifies constraints (memory, I/O, scale) early
- Evolves solution into production-ready design
- Strong communication throughout
Yes:
- Correct solution with some guidance
- Understands tradeoffs but may miss depth
- Minor gaps but solid fundamentals
No:
- Struggles to produce working solution
- Cannot reason about constraints or scaling
- Weak communication or unclear thinking
</content>
</page>

<page url="https://www.notion.so/26ec370222d580f8843fd8fcfa132522" icon="☕">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/73cd64b3cfc9458e80237525f390cdf6" title=""/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/d0817b0493e14f569d8ea2d24e3cb267" title=""/>
<ancestor-3-database url="https://www.notion.so/11ec370222d58020ad93e98fe0cc4607" title="Company Home"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Coffee Chat"}
</properties>
<content>
<callout icon="🚫" color="gray_bg">
	The current program is on pause - Stay tuned for the updated tool for our coffee chat
</callout>
The coffee chat is a 30mins meeting each week with a randomly selected team mate that you will find automatically in your calendar in the weeks where you’re not off. 
Here’s a quick summary on how it works/why we have it:
- coffee chats are meant to be 30mins chats to mix up members of different teams on a regular basis as we grow - just to get to know each other.
- Chat about whatever you want (doesn't have to be work-related)
- based on your cal availability you will find a meeting slot with another BFL member in your cal in each week
- If the slot doesn't work (can be based on large timezone differences and packed calendars) simply check in with the team member you will meet and reschedule to a date that works for both
- if there's an uneven number of employees available in a week, one person has no meeting for that week. But feel free to simply reach out to someone else if you still want to participate and should be that person.
Happy ☕ -chatting!
<empty-block/>
</content>
</page>

<page url="https://www.notion.so/2bcc370222d580c0a01bc684dfc661d5" icon="🔍">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/299c370222d580148387d3714f523193" title="Customization & Support "/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/26bc370222d58061b731fd086a6bdf0c" title=""/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/26bc370222d580ce903ec33669b97868" title=""/>
<ancestor-4-database url="https://www.notion.so/26bc370222d580609794fac1fa01d8d9" title="GTM"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"Verification":"verified","title":"Collecting FLUX Use Cases Q**uestionnaire**"}
</properties>
<content>
<callout icon="📌" color="green_bg">
	Guide to collect & understand FLUX use cases at Partners & Customers.<br>Answers to these questions should be the basis to understand customization potential & effective SE engagement.
</callout>
<callout icon="📌" color="green_bg">
	We have a <mention-page url="https://www.notion.so/28ec370222d5808b97f6fa0151f3438d"/> Notes Template (called ”FLUX Use Case Qualification”) to automate note taking for these use case qualification calls. 
</callout>
## <span discussion-urls="discussion://2c2c3702-22d5-8095-91e8-001ce94fb04c">Overview</span>
- What does their platform do / What are they building? 
	- What service / product do they provide?
	- Who are their users? 
		- *Internal vs. external?*
		- *Consumer vs. Prosumer vs. companies?*
	- What do the users create / what are common final outputs? 
		- *Ideally, concrete examples*
	- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2c2c3702-22d5-80b9-8167-001c79fdb0eb">Are they already using visual AI models? </span>
		- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2c2c3702-22d5-80b9-8167-001c79fdb0eb">*Which other models are they using today?*</span>
		- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2c2c3702-22d5-80b9-8167-001c79fdb0eb">*What are their main pain points with those models?*</span>
- How strategic is image gen to their core business? 
- What happens to their business if the image generation quality/speed degrades?
- What KPIs are they tracking?
## FLUX Integration
- How are they integrating visual AI models in their product?
	- Who writes the prompt?
		- *End user writes full prompt vs. prompt template that is defined by customer only filled with end users details? *
	- Simple prompting interface (similar to playground) vs. prompt presets integrated in product functionality?
		- *“Integrated preset” could be a button that triggers a certain pre-written prompt, without exposing the prompt to the user.*
		- *e.g. consider virtual try on. A user could upload an image an click try on button. The platform could fully abstract away the prompt from the user.*
	- More complex workflow?
		- *Where is the prompting complexity managed in the usual case (user vs. workflow)?*
	- How can users customize models? 
		- *Upload their own data to finetune?*
		- *If yes, what type of data do they upload?*
- Which API / User Interface do they use?
	- Reseller vs. BFL directly vs. competitor API
	- Misc. SaaS UI vs. Playground
- Which requirements do they have in terms of (input and) output resolutions?
	- What are their preferred aspect ratios?
- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2c2c3702-22d5-8096-b85a-001c4bccc0e8">How are they exposing visual AI models to their internal/external users?</span>
	- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2c2c3702-22d5-8096-b85a-001c4bccc0e8">*UI vs API vs something else*</span>
	- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2c2c3702-22d5-8096-b85a-001c4bccc0e8">*(if UI) Would they be interested in BFL helping them to build a customized UI?*</span>
## Visual Examples
<callout icon="📌" color="green_bg">
	SEs need visual examples for **every** use case, before starting to work on it.
</callout>
**Generally we need to know what they tried so far:**
- Which models did they try so far & with which parameter settings?
	- Playground vs. API vs. self-hosted
	- Reseller vs. BFL platform
- Which prompts & inputs did they try so far?
- What are the core quality bars we need to meet?
	- What are the common errors? 
	- What are the no-gos? 
	- What already works well out of the box? 
**Please ask the customer / partner to share a drive / zip folder with:**
- Examples of desired output images (including prompts & input reference images)
- Examples of undesired output images (including prompts & input reference images)
## Volume Estimation
- How many images are they looking to generate per day / month?
<empty-block/>
</content>

	- [Slack](https://join.slack.com/share/enQtOTI3Mzc2OTQxODI0My1mNGViZjJhM2MwMDc2ZDFlZTEwODM3ZDhkNjg2ZDQ1NDMyYTQ3ZDhhMjc3N2RiYmVkZGY5NTkyN2M0ODEwZjJl) #**customer-escalations **whenever there are crucial escalations, e.g. API downtime, billing challenges, critical legal questions, etc. which do not fit in customersdeals-partnerships, put them here and tag the responsible person on the eng/research side. If not clear who is meant, tag @backend or in highly critical cases (e.g. API outage) @oncall
	- [Slack](https://join.slack.com/share/enQtOTI3NjU3MTU1OTQ2MS1iNTI1ZGQzYWQ2Yzk3MTlkZmM1YWE0YzI5YjJmNGRmNWQ3MDFmZmIzYTgxYTk2ZjdiODIyMjNiZWFhYmYzMGIy) #**bfl-help-commercial-legal-external **is your channel to request
