<properties>
{"title":"How marketing is prioritizing"}
</properties>
<content>
<callout icon="/icons/star_green.svg" color="green_bg">
	**Our focus for H1 2026 is to build quality awareness to increase model usage.**
	Win the position as the leader of building frontier models that are SOTA for quality and uniquely good for creative control at speed.
	We should be perceived as the “premium” brand that augments the human creator and honors the craft of making things — from movies and graphic design, to simulated worlds for robotics and games. We are anti-slop and anti-human replacement. 
	We’ll measure this by increases in impressions, website visits, and models usage (via API or licensing) from current baselines.
</callout>
---
<empty-block/>
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Criterion</td>
<td>Questions to ask</td>
<td>Score (1–3)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Goal alignment**</td>
<td>Does it build awareness with frontier devs or pro creatives? Does the partner/channel have wide distribution or tight network effects?</td>
<td>1 = weak fit / 3 = strong fit</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Investment required**</td>
<td>Can we run a lightweight test before committing? Does it match where our team has depth?</td>
<td>1 = high cost, unproven / 3 = low cost, testable</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Execution confidence**</td>
<td>Do we have the experience and bandwidth to deliver a clean test?</td>
<td>1 = needs heavy external support / 3 = within team strengths</td>
</tr>
</table>
Any opportunity scoring 7–9 total: pursue. 4–6: deprioritize or redesign. 1–3: decline.
# Prioritization framework
### Alignment with our goals
Does it build awareness with frontier developers and professional creatives at scale?
- Does the opportunity have wide distribution? Working with a partner like Krea has large distribution on social vs. a B2B enterprise company with very minimal reach. The difference between reaching hundreds of target users vs. thousands to millions.
- Is a given community tightly networked and lead to a multiplier word-of-mouth effect that reaches thousands. eg community hosted events with 50 AI artists in SF leads to 100X reach via word-of-mouth or quality leads.
- Weird cool ideas like a theater pop-up where people can stop by and watch a short AI film made by a creator in a cozy setting “BFL drive in theater”. Has potential to get shared heavily via word of mouth. 
---
### Investment required
Prioritize opportunities that allow us to test with lower spend instead of ones that require a large investment from day one. 
- We’d prioritize things like social, enterprise sales enablement, customer stories, working with a small group of influencers, supporting the community because they allow us to test raising awareness at scale quickly. 
- Investing \$100K in paid ads at this stage doesn’t make sense without having a destination product and a clear understanding of our customer acquisition cost + payback period
---
### Confidence level
Are we confident in our ability to deliver a clean test given team’s experience and internal resourcing bandwidth. 
- Marketing/Growth team today has deeper experience in product/growth marketing, developer experience, brand, and supporting enterprise sales. We should lean into those strengths! Prioritize channels like social, brand campaigns, sales enablement, developer relations etc. 
- Channels like demand gen conferences/events, paid ads and SEO can be figured out, but would likely need the support of externals in a large way. 
---
### Example scenario
Nebius (one of BFL’s compute providers) has requested working on co-marketing and events with them for NVIDIA’s GTC conference. 
Their target audience is other research labs/researchers and their reach/distribution on social platforms is low (500 average views on YouTube). 
<span color="red">They don’t align with our goal of quality awareness with frontier developers and professional creatives at scale. So we’ll de-prioritize and turn down this opportunity. </span>
---
### Other variables to think about
- Do they fit with our brand values of SOTA models, augmenting humans, and leading with openness? Higgsfield is an anti-brand and we should avoid working with them.
### Exception cases
- High-impact conferences where speaking gives us consistent credibility within the research community without paying large amounts for sponsorships or co-hosting. 
<empty-block/>
</content>
</page>

PAGE 91: [page] How to create review Deployments in Hoellental?
ID: 2eac3702-22d5-800b-adf3-ebc8d71876bc
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/2eac370222d5800badf3ebc8d71876bc as of 2026-01-22T13:43:43.057Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/2eac370222d5800badf3ebc8d71876bc">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/330c370222d5804e8990ebd98ead6e6d" title="Solutions Knowledge "/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/26bc370222d58081bd39f8a2634a53d3" title=""/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/26bc370222d580ce903ec33669b97868" title=""/>
<ancestor-4-database url="https://www.notion.so/26bc370222d580609794fac1fa01d8d9" title="GTM"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"How to create review Deployments in Hoellental?"}
</properties>
<content>
If you want to change something of the pipeline of our Models, you will need to create your own Deployment. Luckily we have a very easy way on how to do that. 
### Step 1 
Change the Code in the Hoellental the way you need it
### Step 2 
Open a new PR (first-name/topic e.g. gianluca/size-adjustments). Push your local changes.
### Step 3 
Add Labels: Region (e.g. EU4) + Review 
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	This deployment may take between 15-20 mins
</callout>
### Step 4 
Call the API with the these two changes
```javascript
default: https://api.bfl.ai/
```
**How to adjust:** 
```javascript
https://review-[Your PR Number which can be found in PR-Url].[Region you chose as a label].bfl.ai
```
```javascript
example: https://review-3019.eu4.bfl.ai/
```
### Step 5 
Get a Review Deployment Key ([BFL Review Key](https://share.1password.com/s#jcvIJUtGe_lVg0wcVvZOYWV5ELmEJsPVdY4SgSae610) in 1Password if link doesn’t work) and make sure not to leak it (pls)
### Step 6 
Work with your new created endpoint just like you would with BFL
</content>
</page>

# The Idea
Modern diffusion transformers perform well, but they rely on full attention layers whose cost grows quadratically with sequence length. That becomes a problem as token counts rise with image resolution, video length, and world-model context. This project tests a<br>simple alternative: replace most attention layers with cheaper directional scan blocks, and keep only occasional attention layers for global mixing.
The core hypothesis is that image tokens do not need full global attention at every layer. Vision data lives on a 2D grid with strong spatial structure, so structured row and column scans may be enough to move information across the image if they are combined with<br>periodic attention. If that works, we get a model that keeps much of the quality of a DiT while using a more scalable mixing mechanism.
We already have early signs that this is plausible. In partial runs, several DeltaDiT variants are outperforming the DiT baseline at the same scale, although it is still unclear whether that gap will hold through a full 1M-step training run. The purpose of this<br>project is to turn that early signal into a clean result by measuring both absolute quality and FLOP-adjusted quality across a focused architecture sweep.
# Why This Matters
![](https://prod-files-secure.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/d47c9781-7a36-4f60-98ec-c16fd7b286a6/d7efbebe-39c2-4070-9690-131a6e2494e3/image.png?X-Amz-Algorithm=AWS4-HMAC-SHA256&X-Amz-Content-Sha256=UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD&X-Amz-Credential=ASIAZI2LB466TNFUTTVN%2F20260419%2Fus-west-2%2Fs3%2Faws4_request&X-Amz-Date=20260419T152058Z&X-Amz-Expires=3600&X-Amz-Security-Token=IQoJb3JpZ2luX2VjED8aCXVzLXdlc3QtMiJHMEUCIFg7HfTQa1rgfUBIWH2kdaSgt0FJQTndiqVuthfnljElAiEAoT2rZPPU%2BQKvDOTE6dcCHh3B4YecrHZay%2F0HrCLo%2FyAq%2FwMICBAAGgw2Mzc0MjMxODM4MDUiDHnxyW43ii%2FLm19yMSrcA3VR%2F9mgr%2FhfeDzvgfym9%2FVEhRrCtQSQBfvwsnKPKytWncdtbR%2BHhL1FAUPK37%2FSjHwAn6XUmbDhMhcXMN%2BAAVLTtnz%2BC2SgmyuotcL3rteFNhRBvdVQeed7Dv%2BNKgdg%2B1gtk99fqwFTFSrriNGd4DYNcabsdAqF6YoYr3Z5ggiKmg9Jv8cNv1vab5Poy%2Bjo2BW0fjtu6mhcZxZ64S2IkSSaLNLDmxT7riusITuXvrLMEEOiFSwVCAQG98kJwjqIiQQJdj08LxPATjoEdGZVaqLsxJ0kb90MHzF4RCWnxav9SSW%2BQ%2BTu%2FSeLENQLyzgQNPoa%2BlC1qzaa0bt6yseIlQIehra1x8TYyDUai978qSV4rK5GoJ76NaJyokS2pfYAG5PlIu13pnqiJW3vWmhtjqBVRLynxt1dhk%2FaySR376bWJFO07y%2BcFMvbEGr9zML%2FK9%2FcQ%2BRe2ONWDvHAK3DBArypfznDXGa6vUrYZ4kFMCXycrfpqd7A5vK6V5hTV0zsBpMV84gwzZe%2Bj1VUmwL6RBQcWtTALbE%2FbLrDAXKSx9Z%2BeV7KL%2BEolSaHCifVKWNwTzVBBTy2w8U4ck4Jo0KbsqofgYoJVgCnrstmI1NvoIvwezJu0WfbyVMX89wsMKTOk88GOqUBSQn1EoqVZ%2Fnw52qdr3A37BiHusPePSqUya9Z836sbf9R4%2F5ipNa6IZ7qwOyiUlkN52WHxVmeDTkDY7emeXbeV0h5CrHzbags%2FlUlBgMpPj%2BLSPEhk%2B4M%2B1jWecqX8Z7M0%2B0sIlHj%2F2eE0lb9WHWBgu%2BMydMGfRLTwGUM7BqZQ4BXkhrTg5heQS9mIiAK1%2Bp2GBQqKtqdb1R2FxrBunNQuWf5iloN&X-Amz-Signature=b860ce6978a405bb030d980906ea03b6a09ea3b039d7c3f243cd932fa0add180&X-Amz-SignedHeaders=host&x-amz-checksum-mode=ENABLED&x-id=GetObject)
Quadratic attention is manageable for moderate image sizes, but it becomes a real bottleneck once token counts grow. That matters for higher-resolution image generation, and it matters even more for video and world models, where sequence length grows across both space<br>and time. In those settings, the choice of token mixer starts to determine what scale is even practical.
This project is interesting because it is not just trying to make DiT cheaper in an ad hoc way. It is testing whether full attention is the wrong default primitive for visual generative models. If structured directional mixing can handle most of the local and medium-<br>range communication, then attention may only be needed intermittently as a global synchronization mechanism.
That is a useful result even if it only holds partially. A clear compute-quality tradeoff would already be valuable for potential distillation targets, or consumer models. A stronger result would be showing that a hybrid scan-plus-attention architecture matches or beats a standard DiT  while scaling more gracefully.
# What We Would Build
We start from an XL-sized DiT-style model and r

1. 15 mins call with Tim
2. 45 mins non coding with Cheng
This will be a QA style interview around training large transformer models on large GPU clusters. Things that we might talk about:<br>- Parallelization strategies such as DP (also HSDP / FSDP), TP, PP<br>- GPU compute hierarchies<br>- Efficient attention implementations
1. 45 mins coding with Tim
 In this interview we will debug some Triton code together. If you are new to Triton, it might be helpful to take a look at the tutorials ([https://triton-lang.org/main/getting-started/tutorials/](https://triton-lang.org/main/getting-started/tutorials/)). That being said, I am happy to help you with the syntax. We mostly want to see that you understand the fundamentals of GPU compute hierarchies and are comfortable using them in practice. Specifically we will look at a part of FLUX1 inference ([https://github.com/black-forest-labs/flux](https://github.com/black-forest-labs/flux)), though it’s not required to review the architecture beforehand.

## **Member of Technical Staff - Core Infrastructure / API Backend Engineer**
Freiburg (Germany), San Francisco (USA)
**What if the gap between a breakthrough research model and something millions of people can actually use is making inference fast enough and APIs reliable enough to matter?**
Our founding team pioneered Latent Diffusion and Stable Diffusion - breakthroughs that made generative AI accessible to millions. Today, our FLUX models power creative tools, design workflows, and products across industries worldwide.
Our FLUX models are best-in-class not only for their capability, but for ease of use in developing production applications. We top public benchmarks and compete at the frontier - and in most instances we're winning.
If you're relentlessly curious and driven by high agency, we want to talk.
With a team of \~80, we move fast and punch above our weight. From our labs in Freiburg - a university town in the Black Forest - and San Francisco, we're building what comes next.
But here's what doesn't show up in download numbers: transforming research models into production systems that serve millions of requests reliably. Models that generate in seconds, not minutes. APIs that don't fall over under load. Demos that convince people this technology is real. That's the infrastructure you'll build.
**What You'll Pioneer**
You'll be the bridge between research breakthroughs and production reality. This isn't about maintaining existing APIs—it's about taking models fresh from research, deploying them on robust serving infrastructure, and making them as reliable as possible so we can always show the world what's possible.
**You'll be the person who:**
Develops and maintains world class robust cloud architecture to serve machine learning models at scale.
You maintain the highest standard for operational excellence ensuring high availability and reliability of model serving infrastructure—downtime means users can't create
**Questions We're Wrestling With**
How do you make diffusion model inference fast enough for interactive experiences without sacrificing quality?
What's the right serving architecture for models that don't fit standard deployment patterns?
How do you balance multiple models while ensuing efficient use of compute resources
How do you build code that is both reliable in production but also enables fast iteration on new product ideas and customer POCs
What does "production-ready" actually mean for models that are still actively being improved?
How do you instrument systems to understand not just that they're working, but how well they're working for users?
These aren't theoretical—they're tradeoffs you'll navigate daily as research becomes product.
**Who Thrives Here**
Self driven and performance obsessed engineers. You are never satisfied with the status quo and will push operational excellence to the next level
**You likely have:**
Strong proficiency in Python
Extensive experience with RESTful API development —you've built APIs that real products depend on
Familiarity with containerization and orchestration technologies (Docker, Kubernetes) for deploying infrastructure at scale
Knowledge of cloud platforms (AWS, GCP, or Azure) and IAC
Experience with distributed task queues and scalable model serving architectures 
Understanding of monitoring, logging, and observability best practices for multi region cloud architecture 
Implemented CI/CD systems that quickly test and deploy code globally
**We'd be especially excited if you:**
Familiarity with MLOps practices and tools
Know database systems and data streaming technologies
Have experience with A/B testing and feature flagging in production environments
Understand security best practices for API development and ML model serving
Have built real-time inference systems with low-latency optimizations
**What We're Building Toward**
We're not just serving models—we're building the infrastructure that makes frontier AI research usable at scale. Every optimization you ship makes inference faster. Every API you build enables new applications. If that sounds more compelling than maintaining existing systems, we should talk.

### **1. Where are we spending disproportionate time troubleshooting?**
We’re in firefighting mode too often—handling \~<span discussion-urls="discussion://1c9c3702-22d5-8087-8430-001cfb8f7789">1 production incident per day, </span>mostly infra-related (e.g., outages, latency).
### **2. Which critical projects have insufficient attention?**
- **Infra Stability:** We need root-cause analysis and hardening to cut incident frequency—think better monitoring, chaos testing, or capacity planning. It’s foundational but neglected.
- **Cloud Security Posture:** Wiz and Vanta will be live, but we need to implement controls and leverage it fully—automated remediation and policy enforcement cannot be on the back burner.
- **API Security:** Daily incidents leave no bandwidth for deeper API testing. We have engaged third party for this already, and will have to coordinate.
- **Compliance Automation:** SOC 2/ISO 27001—proactive automation.
### **3. What tasks are being done by people too senior for them?**
Just a couple of things. I’m stuck in the weeds more than I should be:
- **Vendor questionnaire Reviews:** Chasing HyperComply follow-ups—operational, not strategic. But I have a plan to make it self-sufficient and get myself out of this by the end of April.
- **Basic Hardening:** I’d rather spend designing resilient architecture.
### **4. What skills do we lack for upcoming priorities?**
Given our incident rate and roadmap, we’re missing:
- **Infra Reliability + Security:** Someone who can debug production issues (e.g., Kubernetes, AWS) and secure the stack (e.g., Terraform, Wiz integrations).
- **Automation Chops:** Scripting skills to offload repetitive tasks—incident playbooks, compliance automation, alert triage.
- **API Security:** Expertise in securing APIs under load—threat modeling, runtime monitoring.
- **Compliance Workflow:** Practical knowledge of SOC 2/ISO 27001 to streamline audits without my constant oversight.
### **Hiring Needs (Next 6 Months)**
I’d push for one hybrid role—a **Security & Reliability Engineer**:
- **Must-Haves:** Infra/backend experience (e.g., Azure, Kubernetes), scripting (e.g., Python, Bash), and comfort with security/compliance basics.
- **Nice-to-Haves:** API security, familiarity with Wiz/Vanta/HyperComply, incident response experience.
- **Why One Role:** We need a versatile player who can fight fires, harden infra, support compliance, and also roll sleeves and make code changes—reducing our tactical load so we can focus on architecture, API security, and scaling strategy. A second resource might stretch us thin at this stage, but we can revisit it in 6 months.
### **Automation & Outsourcing Opportunities**
- **Automate:**
	- [ ] Incident response playbooks (e.g., auto-restart scripts, Wiz remediation triggers).
	- [ ] Compliance evidence pipelines (e.g., Vanta API to pull logs).
	- [ ] Monitoring/alert consolidation—less noise, faster triage.
- **Outsource:**
	- [x] Vendor reviews via HyperComply or a managed service—low-hanging fruit.
	- [x] External pentests—annual or semi-annual, to cover APIs/infra without taxing us.
- **In-House Priority:** Infra stability, security, and API protection must stay internal—too core to our product and too tied to daily ops.
**Profile**
- Beyond the technical skills (infra, security, automation), we need someone comfortable with the non-glamorous “grunt work”—think manual incident triage, chasing compliance evidence, or willing to interact with auditors/vendors—especially in our current high-incident environment.
- Not every candidate enjoys that, but it’s critical until we automate more (e.g., evidence pipelines alert workflows). I suggest we bring this upfront during interviews? I’d rather set clear expectations than backfill later. Let me know what you think!

<content>
<page url="https://www.notion.so/32fc370222d580019edff5d7ca486158">Infrastructure Engineer — JD</page>
**Team:** Infrastructure / Research Platform
**Goal of this hire:**
Build and operate the infrastructure that powers training and research, including cluster health, performance, observability, and reliability across large GPU fleets.
**Why now:**
As research demands grow, we need more infrastructure depth to keep systems reliable, performant, and scalable without slowing down model development.
**Open Headcount:**
We need people to join our research efforts — one based in the EU and one in the US. Both roles are focused on setting up and monitoring the cluster to ensure our large production runs and data processing pipelines run smoothly. A key part of the mandate is building and maintaining robust observability layers around our infrastructure so we can proactively detect issues, track performance, and keep research unblocked.
---
## Ideal Candidate Profile (ICP)
### Must-Haves (Non-Negotiables)
- Strong hands-on experience working with GPUs (training or infra side)
- Experience debugging performance issues in GPU systems (utilization, bottlenecks, failures)
- Ability to read logs and trace issues across systems (GPU, infra, application)
- Strong understanding of distributed systems or large-scale infrastructure
- High ownership — able to identify problems and drive solutions independently
### Strong Signals (What “great” looks like)
- Experience with GPU performance tuning (benchmarking, utilization, throughput)
- Familiarity with NCCL or similar GPU communication/debugging tools
- Experience identifying bottlenecks in training systems (GPU starvation, I/O, networking)
- Proven ability to proactively find issues (not just respond to incidents)
- Works effectively under pressure and can debug issues in real-time
- Experience working closely with research / ML teams
- Strong curiosity and drive to learn new systems quickly
### Archetype 1: GPU / Training Performance Engineer
- Deep understanding of GPU systems and performance (GPU burn, utilization, benchmarking)
- Experience debugging NCCL / multi-GPU training issues
- Focus on improving training performance and efficiency
- Able to quickly identify and fix bottlenecks impacting training speed
- Some experience with challenges involved data movement, be it for training, processing, or checkpointing
	1. Understands how to leverage the memory hierarchy effectively (object, lustre, NFS, NVME, on chip memory)
### Archetype 2: Infrastructure / Cluster & Observability Engineer
- Experience managing large-scale clusters (Kubernetes, Slurm, or similar)
- Strong monitoring and observability experience (Prometheus, Grafana, dashboards)
- Experience building internal tooling for reliability and debugging
- Handles node health, failures, and cluster-level issues (triage, automation)
- Focus on making infrastructure stable, observable, and scalable
### Nice-to-Haves
- Experience with Kubernetes or Slurm
- Experience managing large GPU clusters
- Experience with infrastructure cost/performance tradeoffs
- Experience building internal tooling for engineering teams
- Experience with multi-cloud or hybrid environments
### Not a Fit
- Pure backend/API engineers without infrastructure or GPU experience
- Data pipeline / analytics-focused profiles
- ML researchers without hands-on infrastructure or performance ownership
- DevOps engineers without experience debugging systems at scale
---
## Candidate Archetypes
Typical Profiles That Fit
- GPU / Training Performance Engineer
	(GPU utilization, benchmarking, NCCL, training bottlenecks)
- Cluster / Infrastructure Engineer
	(Kubernetes, Slurm, cluster management, reliability)
- Observability / Platform Engineer
	(Prometheus, Grafana, dashboards, monitoring systems)
- Startup Infrastructure Generalist
	(0→1 builder, owns infra end-to-end, strong debugging mindset)
	---

PAGE 105: [page] Large-scale Video Data Project

================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/2b1c370222d58046a04dea40a9b77d26 as of 2025-11-22T02:06:56.522Z:

{"title":"Large-scale Video Data Project"}
</properties>
<content>
<span discussion-urls="discussion://2b1c3702-22d5-809a-9ef7-001c78a8ccb2">We are in the process of acquiring O(100 PiB) of video data</span>
- There is 1.4BN videos available from wyndlabs. We need to decide how much we would like to acquire. I think we should do some cost modeling for different dataset scales and do the maximum we are comfortable spending.
- <mention-user url="user://129d872b-594c-810c-ae65-000212532294"/> is also acquiring a large common crawl video set.
<empty-block/>
Action items:
- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2b3c3702-22d5-80bf-adef-001c283f72bf"><mention-user url="user://214d872b-594c-81eb-b15a-0002c48ba904"/></span><span discussion-urls="discussion://2b3c3702-22d5-80bf-adef-001c283f72bf"> PR and link current Captioning workflow</span>
	- Next step: Can we optimize speed?
- <mention-user url="user://129d872b-594c-817b-8874-0002e58fa2f4"/> reaching out to storage companies (don’t exactly remember the names)
<empty-block/>
Questions to answer:
- Where are we storing the data? 
	- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2b1c3702-22d5-80fe-9850-001c4179e720">To answer this we first need to figure out where we want to train with this data. My assumption is ideally everywhere.</span>
	- How does transfer work?
	- How are we storing the data? 
		- For Azure Blob storage a flat structure is better.
- Processing of the data to prepare for training
	- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2b1c3702-22d5-80ef-83a4-001c6fca99f7">Captioning</span>
		- How long does it take given how many GPUs?
		- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2b3c3702-22d5-8062-88ad-001c5170876d">Do we have the final setup that we are confident with? In the past we have wasted quite a bit compute on re-captioning.</span>
	- <span discussion-urls="discussion://2b1c3702-22d5-8016-ac3b-001c91546d25">Clipping</span>
		- How long does it take given how many CPUs?
	- Other processing steps: embedding computation, motion scores
		- How long does it take give how many GPUs 
- Training
	- Will the amount of training compute we have available be a factor in the size of our datasets.
<empty-block/>
</content>
</page>

PAGE 107: [page] Lead Scoring Guide
ID: 343c3702-22d5-8159-8bf2-e0c9ec6fdea4
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/343c370222d581598bf2e0c9ec6fdea4 as of 2026-04-17T10:13:12.897Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/343c370222d581598bf2e0c9ec6fdea4">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-database url="https://www.notion.so/26bc370222d580609794fac1fa01d8d9" title="GTM"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"Verification":"verified","title":"Lead Scoring Guide"}
</properties>
<content>
## What is lead scoring?
Every inbound lead from the BFL contact form is automatically scored out of 11 points. The score tells you how likely a lead is to convert to a paying customer. Anything 4 points or above is flagged as sales-ready and should be followed up. Any lead selecting enterprise as their licensing tier is also flagged regardless of score.
---
## How points are assigned
Each lead is scored on five things:
### Image generation volume
How many images they expect to generate per month.
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Answer</td>
<td>Points</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1M+</td>
<td>4</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>100,000–1,000,000</td>
<td>2</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>10,000–100,000</td>
<td>1</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>0–10,000 or no idea</td>
<td>0</td>
</tr>
</table>
### Company size
Enriched automatically via Clay.
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Employees</td>
<td>Points</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1,000+</td>
<td>3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>251–1,000</td>
<td>2</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1–250</td>
<td>1</td>
</tr>
</table>
### Job title
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Title</td>
<td>Points</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>C-suite, VP, Director, Head of</td>
<td>1</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Everything else</td>
<td>0</td>
</tr>
</table>
### What they're interested in
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Selection</td>
<td>Points</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>API or flux-license</td>
<td>1</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Other</td>
<td>0</td>
</tr>
</table>
### Use case description
A Clay formula field checks for "flux" or "lora" in the free-text use case field. No AI credits used.
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Content</td>
<td>Points</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Mentions "flux" or "lora" (case insensitive)</td>
<td>2</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Neither mentioned or field is empty</td>
<td>0</td>
</tr>
</table>
If someone names a specific model, they've already tested it — high buying intent.
---
## Who to prioritise
Three types of leads are always worth a conversation:
**High volume users** — anyone selecting 1M+ image generations scores 4 points from that field alone. These are likely platform or enterprise customers.
**Large enterprises with unknown volume** — a 1,000+ employee company with an exec title and API or licensing interest will score 4+ even if they selected "I have no idea" on image volume. 
**Leads mentioning a specific model or LoRA** — even a small company with a low image gen estimate is worth a look if they've named a specific model. It means they've already built something with it.
---
## How leads get to Clay
Leads flow from Attio to Clay via a dedicated view called **"Lead Enrichment (Clay Integration Do Not Edit)"** on the People object. This view filters to only show:
- Leads where `Lead Source` = `contact-sales-form`
- Emails that are not personal addresses (no gmail, hotmail, icloud, yahoo, or outlook)
This means only genuine inbound form submissions with company emails are sent for enrichment. Do not modify this view.
---
## Scoring is a starting point, not a final answer
The score is a starting point for triage, not the final word. Always read the use case description before reaching out.
Lead scoring is an iterative process, so feedback is welcome on how we can further improve.
For the first few weeks, leads scoring 1–3 will also be compiled into a weekly digest for a quick team review, to make sure nothing good is being missed while the formula is being calibrated.
Geography and industry scoring will be added in a future update.
</content>
</page>

	This page is meant to capture the collaboration between legal, commercial and backend when it comes to capture important agreement details.

<span discussion-urls="discussion://28cc3702-22d5-804a-85f3-001caf73deea">Here is the Google sheet capturing the information: </span><span discussion-urls="discussion://28cc3702-22d5-804a-85f3-001caf73deea">[https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1am5Q-abUWcXmmjzk3etTAg7na5VuF66sg4KSWQwVPPY/edit?gid=0#gid=0](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1am5Q-abUWcXmmjzk3etTAg7na5VuF66sg4KSWQwVPPY/edit?gid=0#gid=0)</span>

## <span discussion-urls="discussion://28cc3702-22d5-8016-837b-001c0077065d">Legal Agreement Process</span>
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	Objective: Set up an efficient process enabling a smooth and fast collaboration between GTM teams and legal - progress is tracked in Slack and Attio
</callout>
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	Reminder: This is NOT a process where GTM throws an agreement over the fence to legal. We need to collaborate to reduce back and forth and increase deal speed
</callout>
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	ALWAYS take our agreements, NEVER take the customers papers
</callout>
## Largest blockers Jan - Sep 25 have been
- Lack of human resources ✅
- Communication gaps with external lawyers 🚧
- GTM team legal enablement & knowledge 🚧
## <span color="orange" underline="true">Legal Agreement Workflow</span>
1. **Pre-Screening**. For certain deals, e.g. with Enterprises, it makes sense to discuss sensitive topics before sending over an agreement from our end.<span color="orange"> A </span><span color="orange">[Non-Disclosure Agreement (NDA)](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1EkuIhQ3LHVsT1N2bwmZWFT6d7-eNDKg6?usp=drive_link)</span><span color="orange"> should be in place prior to discussion of these topics. </span>These topics are:
	- can we use their data to train our models?
	- do they need 0-data-retention?
	- can we ask to use their logo or would this trigger a lengthy approval process? Note: in general we should always push for logo, potentially give more discount, but we should not slow down a deal unnecessarily
	- Indemnification, see <mention-page url="https://www.notion.so/272c370222d580438e30cd01faca29ab"/> 
	- If you can get all necessary info to fill out the Order Form beforehand you can already insert it for them to check
	- **By surfacing these topics early we can avoid deal breakers and delays later during the legal phase of the negotiation**
2. **Adjust and send Template**. Business team chooses the right template from ([drive.google.com](https://drive.google.com/drive/folders/1nyW6pUruVWYqBv81Q8wfxN6P4n2qMyN0)), creates a copy for the specific customer in the customer’s dedicated Google Drive folder in the sub-folder “legal drafts”
	- Decide what template is needed - is an Order Form enough or do we need a full agreement? —\> Always try to avoid setting up a full API Enterprise Agreement. Some customers, e.g. startups, are totally fine with Order Forms.
	- Decide if Black Forest Labs Inc. or BFL GmbH should be used for the agreement
	- If non-standard deviations are needed, e.g. zero-data-retention, no training, non-standard indemnification level, **please consult with legal via **[**Slack**](slackChannel://black-forest-labs.slack.com/C08KH2QGF45)
	- <span color="orange">Once our draft is complete in the Google document (note: be careful to remove any Internal-only comments)</span>, download and send over to customer as Word Document so they can do their review
3. **Negotiate agreement**. You receive a redlined response from the customer
	- Update the status of the deal to “legal” in the CRM in case it makes sense (e.g. you are not mainly working on technical challenges and legal is happening on the side)
	- **Once you receive their legal redlines, make sure to read them, check their comments and leave comments for your legal team in the document to give them more context. Summarize how you have understood the redlines and your thoughts.**
	- Please click on “request form” in the [Slack channel ](slackChannel://black-forest-labs.slack.com/C08KH2QGF45)[**bfl-help-commercial-legal-external**](slackChannel://black-forest-labs.slack.com/C08KH2QGF45)** **and fill out the details
	- <mention-user url="user://161d872b-594c-8173-9504-000280a771da"/> will assign a member of the legal team to take care of the issue - a discussion in the document and/or on the Slack request might happen
	- Once redlines are complete from our end, Business team member sends back the redline to the counterparty
	- Repeat this process until the parties have agreed (or a party walked away from the deal - we had to do this a couple of times because of extreme indemnification requirements that a deal would not justify)
4. **\[If needed: schedule negotiation call\] **If needed a legal call can be set up - this should be avoided for small and medium sized deals
5. **Update document status**. Once legal review is done, update the status in Slack
6. **Save executed agreement**. When the agreement is signed, please upload the pdf to **both,**
	1. a newly created “Contracts” folder in the respective Customer/Partnership folder **and**
	2. the [Sales&Marketing/Sales/Customers/ZZ_Executed

<span color="pink">*Privileged & Confidential *</span>
### **Commercial** Topics {toggle="true"}
	<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
		Fundamentally, there are 3 revenue sources for BFL *(simplified!)*:
		1. **API Services Agreement **(We host the model and incur inference compute costs to provide an API that our customers use to integrate our models into their own products)
		2. **Commercial License Agreement (”CLA”) **for our Open Weights models (our customers download the Open Weights-Versions of our models and then self-host (i.e. they incur inference compute costs)
		3. **Playground** Customers purchase credits to use our Customer-facing interface called “Playground” (this is essentially a front-end to our API)
		*\[Additionally, there are various **bespoke agreements** with larger strategic customers and, if unavoidable, we sometimes enter into **Reseller** or **Evaluation** agreements.\] *
	</callout>
	1. **GoogleForm:** Our GTM-team uses [this Form](https://docs.google.com/forms/d/e/1FAIpQLSeBRq2a78VNqxJF6W92E-fseetbUz39HXgRccbvfeYgoCKgvg/viewform) to generate new drafts for agreements. The questions in the Form give you a good sense of the main issues that are usually considered as part of the negotiation. The Form is meant to speed up drafting and to make it easier for non-legal team members to assess legal implications of certain contractual provisions. 
	2. **Overview**: The below decision-tree shows the logic behind the Form and illustrates which documents will be drafted based on the answers provided. EU and Non-EU documents were forked *\[we work with boutique law firm *[*Aitava*](https://www.aitava.com/en/)* for Germany/EU-specific questions\]*. Page 2 illustrates the flow from initial Customer contact to executed agreement and how the legal team interacts with GTM. <br><br>*\*You’ll note that this does not contain the option of binding CLA and API Services together in one agreement - this option is currently being drafted. *
		<unknown url="https://www.notion.so/2c7c370222d580a59133c8e324b67b20#2c7c370222d580cda054c3e6fb35b93e" alt="drive"/>
	3. **Negotiation**
		1. If legal help is required for negotiation, GTM creates a ticket in . 
		2. If we need to clarify technical questions (e.g., certain cybersecurity requests from customers) we tap Workstreet’s knowledge bank through [https://black-forest-labs.slack.com/archives/C09J2DGC43T](https://black-forest-labs.slack.com/archives/C09J2DGC43T) to avoid asking internal team members repetitive questions. 
		3. You can leverage your [Legora Account](https://app.eu.legora.com/) to help review and draft clauses and we might automate the redlining process further with Legora playbooks.
		4. We use DocuSign to execute agreements
		5. An overview of all executed agreements can be found here: [https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1_EM_z6LHWPMwibWFDGbeZX2PFYaDSh-x/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=117009868738180578161&rtpof=true&sd=true](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1_EM_z6LHWPMwibWFDGbeZX2PFYaDSh-x/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=117009868738180578161&rtpof=true&sd=true)
	4. **Template vs. Master Documents: **For each possible document, there is a “Template Version” and a “Master Version”. 
		1. The **Template Version** is a customer-ready, clean document that only contains placeholders that are filled based on the answers provided in the Form (see above). <span color="red">Any edits to these documents will immediately go live</span>. 
		2. The **Master Version** serves as the internal legal team clause bank. It contains 
			1. fallback options for some clauses,
			2. Document comments and edits in tracked changes, or
			3. footnotes with additional information that might be relevant for negotiation. 
			The Master Version is meant as a <span underline="true">living document</span>. If you learn anything new in a negotiation, read case law, learn about changes in law or if there is anything else that might come in handy for our commercial templates, please drop it in here as a comment, suggestion or footnote. 
		3.  Links to documents (<span color="red">do</span><span color="red">** **</span><span color="red" underline="true">**NOT**</span><span color="red">** **</span><span color="red">edit templates</span>):
			1. Commercial License Agreement: 
				1. Inc.: Order Form ([Template](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1EiqB-a2_AxPefCuvKR47SjoLPQv-LGOYZsXyZZlvcy8/edit?usp=drive_link), [Master](https://docs.google.com/document/d/19wqAzgoTsRW38lHTSQuzR5Kdjuy0tOnaAjyuZR2c8ew/edit?usp=drive_link)); CLA ([Template](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1uaqAxkXiEWDobKhLjOubIis07vPPoGQhsePzx7fiwns/edit?usp=drive_link), [Master](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1KE4Q4oSk-KJXuR_ZXROkMKcBpzhT5uDIo4K9poBbTOc/edit?usp=drive_link))
				2. GmbH: Order Form ([Template](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1iE1_RyT3kAH_oumr9ozK8moJuuRcPbfE2Y7fSAux3Ck/edit?usp=drive_link), [Master](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1OsuRj1j3dqiCC5L9dFR6AT0ZFW6Eya-sG257oRatIFE/edit?usp=drive_link)); CLA ([Template](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1iE1_RyT3kAH_oumr9ozK8moJuuRcPbfE2Y7fSAux3Ck/edit?usp=d

## What we're launching/what’s changed
**Product/Feature Description**
The API supports LoRA inference. It is able to load a pre-trained LoRA efficiently and serve generations using this LoRA.
## Why it matters
**Who cares**
Our current production models can’t solve all tasks. A lot of value reside in these tasks since most production models can’t solve them yet.
Various teams (FDE, product) have demonstrated the ability to solve some of these tasks by training dedicated LoRAs.
But until this release we can’t use these checkpoints in production, heavily limiting their reach.

<content>
<callout icon="/icons/star_green.svg" color="green_bg">
	**Our focus for H1 2026 is to build quality awareness to increase model usage.**
	We’ll measure this by increases in impressions, website visits, and models usage (via API or licensing) from current baselines.
</callout>
# Three goals for marketing in H1 2026:
<callout icon="1️⃣" color="green_bg">
	### Build **quality awareness** for BFL & FLUX 
	**Black Forest Labs as a frontier lab.**
	Core channels: Press, Podcasts, founder socials, Publish research, Website/Blog
	Content: Press articles, vision of “Why visual AI matters”, Research papers
	**FLUX models best for reliable creative control at speed.**
	Core channels: Socials, YouTube, influencers/community, co-marketing w/ partners
	Content: Customer stories, use cases, model launches, Website
</callout>
<callout icon="2️⃣" color="green_bg">
	### Drive usage** & retention** for our models
	Drive conversions and engagement for the API:
	Core channels: Dev focused websites, docs, YouTube, socials, email, sales enablement
	Content: API docs, sales enablement/ABM, Website
</callout>
<callout icon="3️⃣" color="green_bg">
	### Establish foundation of an **enterprise GTM **engine
	Core channels: Sales motion, emails to existing users, enterprise marketing
	Content: Customer Case Studies, sales enablement material on pitch, model battlecards, etc
</callout>
# Who are we building for?
<callout icon="/icons/card_green.svg" color="green_bg">
	**Primary: the frontier developers building AI apps**
	The "highest expectation developer" builds visual apps and tools that creatives use directly (directors, creators, enterprises, agents). They need a solid API with special customization features (like LoRA training) that helps them fine-tune their apps while keeping costs low and speed high. Their main goal is creating AI applications that users actually stick with and keep coming back to, and they work at companies like Krea, Flora, Meta, Notion, Adobe, and Figma.

While tools like Gamma, Adobe Firefly, and Krea see high initial use, they're mostly used for one-off tasks like quick presentations or edits. The goal is to be essential for creating final, production-ready work with fast iteration.
</callout>

ID: 32fc3702-22d5-8095-b3fa-c9f27f6142ee

Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/32fc370222d58095b3fac9f27f6142ee as of 2026-03-26T21:40:48.207Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/32fc370222d58095b3fac9f27f6142ee" icon="icons/movie-camera_gray">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/2eac370222d580b099d2dc0355a52411" title=""/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/2eac370222d580bd968bf7a963302929" title=""/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/252c370222d580bda20dda595dabb585" title="Growth/Marketing "/>
<ancestor-4-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580cab1aae5ec0f8cac31" title=""/>
<ancestor-5-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5808facd7c952b579a4cd" title=""/>
<ancestor-6-database url="https://www.notion.so/11ec370222d58020ad93e98fe0cc4607" title="Company Home"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Martin Scorsese launch date decision doc"}
</properties>
<content>
## Context
AI for visual creation is still early and there are raging debates on copyright violations (Seedream, Sora), what it means for jobs, human replacement, and more. 
Generative AI and visual intelligence have not yet been positioned as clearly beneficial to human creators (to be fair Runway has done a good job caring about movie creators). 
The video Martin Scorsese did with BFL shows how FLUX actually augments his workflow and gives him a new tool to bring more creations to life vs. replacing people. 
FLUX and BFL are known in the AI creative developer community, but we have less general awareness with the larger creative community, AI engineers, and the general public.
We have the opportunity to make a positive impression on the general creative community and public by announcing that Martin Scorsese is an advisor to BFL + release a video of Martin Scorsese using FLUX for storyboarding. 
We’ve also invested time and \$100K+ in creating this video with Marty. 
## Decision we need to make
**Decide when to release the video and announcement to maximize quality awareness** with the general public (creatives, press, people interested in AI, tech twitter, etc) for Black Forest Labs as a premium brand used by creators with the highest standards. 
## Variables that matter
- Maximizing impact of something we’ve invested in
- Building quality awareness as a premium brand
- Downside of negative perception as “not shipping anything on models or product”
- Content in the video becoming stale given it’s focused on image gen/storyboarding
- Ideal messaging is “Martin Scorsese is now an advisor to Black Forest Labs”
- Converting awareness into model usage (destination experience, etc)
- Maximizing success of the future FLUX.3 launch
- Marty’s involvement post video launch and after FLUX.3
## Options
#### Option 1: launch at the end of April. 
Launching earlier builds brand awareness before FLUX 3, so when the big model launch happens, we're not starting from scratch explaining who Black Forest Labs is. The FLUX 3 launch will be so focused on the model itself that it'll be hard to squeeze in brand-building at that point. Getting the Scorsese moment out early creates a "brand step ladder" that makes the FLUX 3 launch stronger. 
- To keep the launch momentum going ahead of FLUX.3 we can continue staggered content drops — first the vision of visual intelligence, then Marty video, then an earned media piece, then Robin’s “how we do research”, etc.
- End of April gives us enough time to activate multiple channels including press, A16Z new media team, etc. 
- Downside is that we’re not launching any new model or product with this video. Sentiment from AI and technical creative community could be negative. 
	- We’d have to mitigate this with continuing value for the community (FLUX 2.1 etc)
	- Understand that we’re using this campaign to broader general awareness (it’s not targeted at the community that already knows us). We have to live with some negative sentiment.
#### Option 2: launch 2 weeks before the FLUX.3 launch. 
Launch the Marty Video very close to FLUX.3 as a major model launch a couple weeks later, avoiding the issue amongst the community (that already knows us) of Marty video being perceived as just a brand moment with no real release behind it.
- This requires our content drops of “why visual intelligence” and “how we do research, etc. to do alot more work to build brand awareness and impressions ahead of FLUX.3 launch. 
- Being so close to the FLUX.3 launch (\~2 weeks) could cause press to miss the FLUX.3 launch and cover only the Marty video. 
- We’re dependent on knowing an exact launch date for FLUX.3. Might be possible by end of April after pre-training, and Marty video launch could get delayed into mid/end of may. 
#### <span discussion-urls="discussion://330c3702-22d5-8084-98ab-001c338ee3e2">Option 3: launch after FLUX.3</span>
<span discussion-urls="discussion://330c3702-22d5-8084-98ab-001c338ee3e2">Connects Martin Scorsese to our video model. Feels like the worst option because the current video is focused on storyboarding images and could actually distract from the FLUX.3 multi-modality narrative. It would be much better to do more with Marty close to or after the FLUX.3 launch (eg another video, co-interview, etc). </span>
- <span discussion-urls="discussion://330c3702-22d5-8084-98ab-001c338ee3e2">If we thought it made sense, and we could not get anything out of Marty after FLUX.3, then saving the Marty video until after FLUX.3 could provide a major post FLUX.3 launch moment that drives more model usage. </span>
## Decision
Recommend going with Option 1 to launch the marty video at the end of April. 
We should be clear that we’re targeting the general public with the Marty video and building our brand awareness to make FLUX.3 successful. We need to invest in content drops before and after the Marty video to sustain momentum and brand building. 
We should expect negative sentiment from the community that knows us (Reddit, X, etc) and plan to mitigate. We should also be pl

PAGE 120: [page] Model versioning strategy 
ID: 326c3702-22d5-8012-87df-c12e8e0b85eb
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/326c370222d5801287dfc12e8e0b85eb as of 2026-04-08T11:45:35.509Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/326c370222d5801287dfc12e8e0b85eb">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580ae8cb9d4f9ca406bb2" title="Engineering"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Model versioning strategy"}
</properties>
<content>
## **Motivation**
Our current model naming is ad-hoc: `flux-2-pro`, `flux-2-pro-preview`, `flux-2-klein-9b-preview`, `flux-kontext-pro`, etc. There is no systematic way to distinguish between model generations, track when a model was released, communicate lifecycle status, or give customers a predictable deprecation timeline. 
We want to adopt a versioning model inspired by Anthropic's approach: **dated snapshot versions** for pinning, **aliases** for convenience, and a **formal lifecycle** with guaranteed notice periods. 
We propose to move to this new strategy starting Flux 3 and gradually phase out old endpoints.   
## <span discussion-urls="discussion://342c3702-22d5-807a-8a8c-001cd276b516">**1. Naming Convention**</span>
### <span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-80ce-be9e-001c9e5703a9">**1.1 Model Families**</span>
A model family is a product line that shares an architecture tier:
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>**Family**</td>
<td>**Description**</td>
<td>**Current equivalents**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-pro`</td>
<td>Flagship quality</td>
<td>`flux-2-pro`, `flux-2-max` </td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-klein`</td>
<td>Fast, cost-efficient</td>
<td>`flux-2-klein-9b`, `flux-2-klein-4b`</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-801d-85a9-001ce08cf69d">`flux-kontext`</span></td>
<td></td>
<td>`flux-kontext-pro`, `flux-kontext-max`</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-flex`</td>
<td></td>
<td>`flux-2-flex`</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><span discussion-urls="discussion://332c3702-22d5-8071-9770-001cfa497899">??? </span></td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
</table>
<span discussion-urls="discussion://33cc3702-22d5-80ac-9402-001ca93920e1">Family names are stable and do not change across generations.</span>
### **1.2 Dated Versions (Snapshots)**
Every model release gets a **dated identifier** using the format:
<span discussion-urls="discussion://32cc3702-22d5-80a1-938f-001c68ed52a1">`{family}-{generation}-{date}`</span>
- <span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-80fb-aaae-001c08d5bc3f">**generation**</span><span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-80fb-aaae-001c08d5bc3f">: major model generation (</span><span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-80fb-aaae-001c08d5bc3f">`2`</span><span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-80fb-aaae-001c08d5bc3f">, </span><span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-80fb-aaae-001c08d5bc3f">`3`</span><span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-80fb-aaae-001c08d5bc3f">, …)</span>
- **date**: release date as `YYYYMMDD`
Examples:
<table header-row="true">
<colgroup>
<col>
<col width="347.99998474121094">
</colgroup>
<tr>
<td>**Dated version**</td>
<td>**Description**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-pro-2-20260401`</td>
<td>FLUX Pro gen-2, released 2026-04-01</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-pro-2-20260715`</td>
<td>Updated FLUX Pro gen-2 snapshot</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-klein-2-20260401`</td>
<td>FLUX Klein gen-2, released 2026-04-01</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-kontext-2-20260401`</td>
<td>FLUX Kontext gen-2, released 2026-04-01</td>
</tr>
</table>
Dated versions are **immutable**: once published, the model weights and behavior behind a dated version never change.
### **1.3 Aliases**
Aliases are mutable pointers to the latest recommended dated version within a family and generation:
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>**Alias**</td>
<td>**Points to (example)**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-pro-2`</td>
<td>`flux-pro-2-20260715`</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-klein-2`</td>
<td>`flux-klein-2-20260401`</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-kontext-2`</td>
<td>`flux-kontext-2-20260401`</td>
</tr>
</table>
When we release a new snapshot, the alias is updated. Customers using aliases get the latest model automatically. Customers using dated versions are unaffected.
**Top-level aliases** (optional, for discovery):
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>**Alias**</td>
<td>**Points to**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-pro`</td>
<td>`flux-pro-2` (latest generation)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-klein`</td>
<td>`flux-klein-2`</td>
</tr>
</table>
### **1.4 **<span discussion-urls="discussion://326c3702-22d5-803f-8d4a-001cf67b54ab">**Quality Tiers**</span>** Within a Family**
Where a family has multiple quality levels (e.g., current `flux-2-pro` vs `flux-2-max`), encode the tier in the family name:
<table header-row="true">

<td>`flux-2-max`</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-kontext-2-{date}`</td>
<td>`flux-kontext-pro`</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>`flux-kontext-max-2-{date}`</td>
<td>`flux-kontext-max`</td>
</tr>
</table>
## **2. Model Lifecycle**
Every model passes through four states:
`Active → Legacy (optional) → Deprecated → Retired`
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td><span discussion-urls="discussion://342c3702-22d5-80a5-a2f5-001c6299de06">**State**</span></td>
<td>**Definition**</td>
<td>**API behavior**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Active**</td>
<td>Fully supported and recommended</td>
<td>Requests succeed normally</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Legacy**</td>
<td>Still works, will not receive updates, may be deprecated in the future</td>
<td>Requests succeed; response includes `X-Model-Status: legacy` header</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Deprecated**</td>
<td>Retirement date assigned; not available to new customers</td>
<td>Requests succeed for existing users; response includes `X-Model-Status: deprecated` and `X-Model-Retirement-Date` headers</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Retired**</td>
<td>No longer available</td>
<td>Requests return `410 Gone` wi

PAGE 121: [page] Neural Glue: Joint Multi-Modal Generation via Lightweight Latent Bridging
ID: 31ec3702-22d5-8091-8a5c-dc52df565035
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/31ec370222d580918a5cdc52df565035 as of 2026-03-10T19:01:30.735Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/31ec370222d580918a5cdc52df565035">
<ancestor-path></ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Neural Glue: Joint Multi-Modal Generation via Lightweight Latent Bridging"}
</properties>
<content>
## TLDR
Pre-trained modality-specific models (e.g., **FLUX.3** for images & video & audio, Kimi k2 for language) are **masters of their own domains** but "blind" and "deaf" to each other. To unify them, current approach opt to perform joint pre-training to bridge all modalities under a single backbone. This approach is extremely expensive and wasteful, as separate, modality-specific models already exist.
**Neural Glue** is a framework designed to create a joint multi-modal model from any two frozen pre-trained, single-modality models. Unlike simple conditioning, our goal is the **simultaneous generation of paired multi-modal outputs** (e.g., synchronized video-audio/ image-caption). By inserting a lightweight, learnable **Joint Latent Transformer** between the intermediate layers of frozen backbones, we enable synchronized, high-fidelity generation with a fraction of the compute.
## Technical Architecture: The Latent Unifier
### **💡 Motivation: The Physical Reality Hypothesis**
Our approach is grounded in the **Physical Reality Hypothesis** (e.g., [Isola et al.](https://phillipi.github.io/prh/)), which posits that different modalities: pixels, waveforms, or text, are simply **different projections of the same underlying latent observation** of the physical world.<br>If this holds true, the intermediate representations of two models already share a "hidden" commonality. We don't need to rebuild the world, we just need a **Latent Unifier** to act as the bridge. By operating on these internal representations, the Unifier adds the necessary cross-modal context, allowing each expert model to continue functioning as it always did, but now in perfect alignment with its counterpart.
### **💻 PoC: Joint Video-Audio Genration**
The following logic outlines a single training iteration. The goal is to optimize the **Unifier** (Projectors + Joint Transformer) to minimize the combined denoising loss while keeping the foundation backbones (W, S) frozen.
A self-supervised loss (e.g., Self-Flow style) will probably be added to reinforce relations between the unifier and single-modality representations.
<empty-block/>
![](https://prod-files-secure.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/d47c9781-7a36-4f60-98ec-c16fd7b286a6/caa13139-5508-4cee-aa4f-0cf4c177d032/image.png?X-Amz-Algorithm=AWS4-HMAC-SHA256&X-Amz-Content-Sha256=UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD&X-Amz-Credential=ASIAZI2LB4666E5VHUFA%2F20260419%2Fus-west-2%2Fs3%2Faws4_request&X-Amz-Date=20260419T152132Z&X-Amz-Expires=3600&X-Amz-Security-Token=IQoJb3JpZ2luX2VjED8aCXVzLXdlc3QtMiJIMEYCIQCuN25l2ngp3p1vuoEEc4ajEEH%2BRSRBkfkdboPasUpzfgIhANdzJ9RxatVF4fVwTXVZKEJJkaLfQzI0ReqDXZMJQjPsKv8DCAgQABoMNjM3NDIzMTgzODA1IgzOnF8TmecEgnkKrIgq3ANQhpr1Mh6%2FdBnTed1tzXkgx%2Fg6ym%2BtTY1FIIio50Q4HtsnybSXA4ir4bQMAQv65HQvz8KOb0LgCx0A0yFV8oZOgnSJ3FWIl9X9dapyxvaOWEz%2BGxSGx%2FjS3ylglbTQlnB3DRgiVzDUEPLspj%2BYGNGr5fEeyBT%2F%2F5H3vFQHomoiTg7eYjjzgWl7T2CJ6bYSV%2Bx0%2Bu0KrbOQldgAwEDjJOscLqWk84Na6wBKx4Yo2PYiNaC33IwcvKUrU%2BqBFii71hbZdMIOJW%2FgnYHbE%2BvSKEFEPWdYhL89u1v0nXkPD5Ju1vshsT1jmutfsx8LP98rJRfd1n%2FihQ4oGQgkmDo0Zg1AyB%2ByNv79y389dsZLKd%2FdRXSGJ5s1fGqVHWE3M3dOGXulvUCnRzejjsr%2FkbSB8ZMtITbIO%2BBE0iR4KRhSyJjf9KZMikaSAyJo5cP%2F9%2BoJKCM8gAYc9XOgYoBNR4Fqa96%2FV4O1AuDw6bZBbs8SMxvw2edKZtYHNGZG19Vz984qTFcEx%2FcbL0KCdFr2tLQeumkwwGGyMDeZ55W7y9RJHtGoc6kWAirjErdpkzxuOby3u%2B5WGWgibMaGMwVPR5I60VQOGWYKp%2BjRRrPviCzSzSjnXykcqbiGtgCKimTk3DD1zZPPBjqkATu585eruxFhPK0ZgzT7PcbkPZ9899O9U7k98%2BrHYI%2FUc2sXAcvw0YVQivXs%2F75oQ6ye78pFZM0oB3w%2FMItnDi0ugu0%2F8m5PewgqdXmouYEQ92ev%2BwdBgjXoHXi2gQPUmDwrj3SzMVJZz1qO8OW2QfNIIYKgLdXleypqe%2B9JEdKOg%2FPctOehPncmRiMp2bR1bI8wOzBnS207njWXjPr2tIKYkypd&X-Amz-Signature=eb9f68fce4cab80259cbc0f0fa2e3513da74f7780f1d9979da500314186b023a&X-Amz-SignedHeaders=host&x-amz-checksum-mode=ENABLED&x-id=GetObject)
**Figure 1:** ***Neural Glue** PoC. The input is processed by each modality separately. Intermediate features are then linearly projected and processed by the **Neural Glue** (AKA Latent Bridge) which performs joint attention and outputs contextualized representations per modality. The produced representations are then linearly projected back to the modality-specific spaces, and the loss function updates the Neural Glue weights and the projection weights.*
<empty-block/>
Below, we outline the pseudo-code for our proposed PoC above.
**Models:** `W` (Frozen Video), `S` (Frozen Audio), `Bridge` (Learnable Unifier)
```python
def training_step(text_prompt, video_audio_pair, t):

    # 1. Add noise to ground truth at level t
    noised_video, noised_audio = add_noise(video_audio_pair, t)

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # 2. Stage 1: Partial Processing in Frozen Foundations
    #    Extract intermediate representations from mid-point layers
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    v_rep_mid = W.initial_layers(noised_video, text_prompt)
    a_rep_mid = S.initial_layers(noised_audio, text_prompt)

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # 3. The Latent Unifier (The "Glue")
    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

    # A. Project into shared latent space
    v_joint = W_proj_in(v_rep_mid)
    a_joint = S_proj_in(a_rep_mid)

    # B. Synchronize via Joint Transformer (Cross-Modal Attention)
    v_sync, a_sync = JointTransformer(v_joint, a_joint)

    # C. Project back to modality-specific dimensions
    v_residual = W_proj_out(v_sync)
    a_residual = S_proj_out(a_sync)

    # ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    # 4. Reintegration & Stage 2: Final Synthesis
    #    Models continue processing, n

<ancestor-7-database url="https://www.notion.so/11ec370222d58020ad93e98fe0cc4607" title="Company Home"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Office Visit: Calendar Process"}
</properties>
<content>
To help us better coordinate office visits and ensure we're prepared when team members, clients, candidates, or other external visitors come to the Freiburg HQ.
## What to Do
When you or anyone else will be visiting our office, please add the visit to our [BFL Calendar](https://calendar.google.com/calendar/embed?src=c_77cf5b596fb7704516f85dafcb58fc31084e9000cf3629d8cc93b96c77baed33%40group.calendar.google.com&ctz=Europe%2FBerlin) with the following information:
- **Dates of the visit** - Include all days you’ll be in town
- **Who is visiting** - Name(s) of visitors (if possible, to include dietary restrictions if vegetarian, lactose-free, etc.)
- **Number of people** - If applicable (e.g., "Client + 2 team members")
- **Put Carolyne in CC on the calendar invite** - This helps maintain visibility and coordinate as needed
## When to Add
Please add visits to the calendar as soon as they're confirmed, even if details are still being finalized. You can always update the calendar event later if plans change.
## Why This Helps
Having this information on our shared calendar allows us to:
- Plan ahead for office space and resources
- Coordinate schedules when multiple visitors are in town
- Prepare for enough food for everyone joining
- Ensure a smooth experience for our guests
</content>
</page>

PAGE 127: [page] Onboarding Cheat Sheet
ID: 225c3702-22d5-80f0-a1a4-f84df8910ac3
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/225c370222d580f0a1a4f84df8910ac3 as of 2026-04-01T12:42:58.035Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/225c370222d580f0a1a4f84df8910ac3">
<ancestor-path></ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Onboarding Cheat Sheet"}
</properties>
<content>
## Cloning the repository:
follow [this guide](https://docs.github.com/en/authentication/connecting-to-github-with-ssh/generating-a-new-ssh-key-and-adding-it-to-the-ssh-agent) to add your **public ssh key **(can generate with `ssh-keygen -t ed25519`) to your github account to allow cloning.
when cloning, **use the ssh command**. e.g.:
`git clone git@github.com:black-forest-labs/schwarzwald.git`
# **Working with the servers:**
### **Gaining access to the cluster (only once per (computer, cluster)): **
request a team member to give access to the cluster using the public key (`.pub`)
### Setting up your virtual environment:
Follow the [instructions on the README](https://github.com/black-forest-labs/schwarzwald/tree/hila/multimodal_clip?tab=readme-ov-file#installation)
activate venv:
`source .venv/bin/activate`
## h100
allocating an interactive node:<br>`./scripts/getgpu.sh [number of gpus (default=1)]`
running your training code on the interactive node with a config yaml:
`bash `[`interactive.sh`](http://interactive.sh/)` <path_to_yaml>`
# gb200 cluster
start each python command with `PYTHONPATH=src`
allocating an interactive node:
 `./scripts/getgpu_gb200.sh 1` 
### launching runs:
follow [this README](https://github.com/black-forest-labs/schwarzwald/pull/595/files)
notice that** jobs may restart and run from scratch**, in this case, use the [modified sbatch script here](https://github.com/black-forest-labs/schwarzwald/pull/581/files#diff-2b6152f1bb6bd00966b8c375e6355dc6cb661e1850f0377eb41d773da6c2aca5R109)
<empty-block/>
resuming a run:
make sure that path_to_your_run_dir contains also the configs (not the ckpt dir)
`<original_run_command> --resume <path_to_your_run_dir> --seed 42` 
### evaluating an imagenet run:
only works from a login node (not arm)
1. activate dominik’s env:`source /shared/home/dominik/internal/schwarzwald/.venv/bin/activate` 
2. launch a run to evaluate your checkpoint: <br>`TARGET_VENV=/shared/home/dominik/src/schwarzwald-envs/sw0605 CH=4 PYTHONPATH=src python scripts/evalt2i/sample.py -l <path_to_your_log_dir> -s <ckpt> -name test  -ip` (the ip flag indicates that this is imagenet testing, for T2I don’t pass anything)
to view the graphs for the FIDs (with streamlit):
`PYTHONPATH=src streamlit run ./scripts/evalt2i/streamlit_metrics.py`
## **Port mapping examples:**
h100:
`ssh -i <path_to_ssh>/.ssh/id_ed25519 -J <user>`[`@h100cluster-login.bfl.ml`](mailto:hila@h100cluster-login.bfl.ml)` -L 8501:localhost:8501 <user>@slurmcluster-hpc-4`
gb200:
`ssh -i <path_to_ssh>/.ssh/id_ed25519 -J <user>@gb200-login-1 -L 8502:localhost:8501 <user>@ccw1-gpu-31 `
## Data debugging
1. use the `test_data` script to make sure everything works properly: <br>`PYTHONPATH=src python ./scripts/data/test_data.py <path_to_data/train_yaml>`
2. it is recommended to write a streamlit app to visualize your data, an example can be found [here](https://github.com/black-forest-labs/schwarzwald/pull/593/files#diff-01f1c5cc6a487e7316c5c01b8e222c066a740d8e428cc69a77370d2cebb22bb5) 
## Distributed training
### Distributed Data Parallel
- model is duplicated on gpus
- data is distributed across gpus
use `bfl.modules.distributed.ddp.apply_ddp` (where the model is defined, e.g., model.yaml). for example:
`strategy_config:  `
	`strategy_target: bfl.modules.distributed.ddp.apply_ddp  `
	`param_dtype: float32`
### Fully Sharded Data Parallel (model sharding)
- Model parameters are sharded across devices (not replicated like DDP)
- Gradients and optimizer states are also sharded
- Parameters are gathered only when needed for computation
- Much lower memory usage per device compared to DDP
need to wrap the model blocks with fsdp, see parallel dit example:<br>[github.com](https://github.com/black-forest-labs/schwarzwald/blob/main/src/bfl/modules/distributed/parallelize_dit.py#L135)
need to define the strategy in the config (where the model is defined, e.g., model.yaml):<br>[github.com](https://github.com/black-forest-labs/schwarzwald/blob/main/configs/baselines/t2i/model.yaml#L192)
<empty-block/>
## loading distributed checkpoints
use the convert function in `scripts.checkpoint.convert`  to convert the distributed checkpoint to a `safetensors` checkpoint:
`convert("<path_to_your_exp>/step-<step>/model", target="<path_to_save>/")` 
if you want your checkpoint to be saved with a certain format:
`convert("<path_to_your_exp>/step-<step>/model", target="<path_to_save>/model.safetensors")` 
## Logging
start logging ui command (this will give a port on which the ui will be hosted, do port mapping to see it locally): 
`streamlit run src/sbord/slimboard.py`
all the logs should be under **/blob/raw/\<user\> (not shared) to save space. set up a symbolic link from logs to  /blob/raw/\<user\>:**
`ln -s /blob/raw/<user>/06_2025/ logs`
once in the logging ui, place the path to the log under `/blob/raw/<user>/06_2025/`.
get log path from job id: `bash scripts/logof.sh` 
## masterboard logging
you can open a tunnel (and keep it up) to view logs and share them with others.
1. start a masterboard using<br>`PYTHONPATH=src streamlit run src/sbord/masterboard.py` (use ctrl Z + bg to run this in the background)
2. start a tunnel to your masterboard:<br>`/shared/bin/bfltunnel.sh -l <port to masterboard> -h <name of your tunnel>` 
new tunnel setup:
```plain text
1. Start tmux session
2. In one window start streamlit app on some port streamlit run src/sbord/masterboard.py --server.port 7509
3. In another window start the tunnel script ./scripts/bfltunnel.sh -l 7509 -h masterboard-${USER}, make sure they have same port
4. the tunnel script will ask you for a passworrd twice, it's there in 1password. Look for bfltunnel
5. Once you ent

# Welcome to the Forest 🌲
We're excited to have you join our growing team! Take a look at our current org chart <span color="green">[HERE](/17bc370222d580d7b8a9f0471ff142b5?pvs=25)</span>. 
During your first week,
- Complete <span color="green">[**this onboarding check-list**](/275c370222d580ee9fccdc96ebf1160b?pvs=25)</span>. You should receive this in your email inbox as well.
- You'll have a welcome session with our CEO Robin or someone else from the leadership team, where you'll learn about BFL models, our tech, and meet key members of your team.
- Play around with our models in the [Playground](https://playground.bfl.ai/)
<empty-block/>
We highly value regular check-ins, transparent communication and personal connections:
- On the third Wednesday of every month, we hold our **BFL Monthly All Hands**, where we share company-wide updates, insights from each department, and a look at our roadmap to bring everyone on the same page.
- Thursday [Demo Days ](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1zWq8j56FWv6OODq9XehLBCk0zOkkTpCAnXEOkE7XjTI/edit?gid=0#gid=0)where you can present anything you’ve been working on without prep, occasionally we also have customers come in to share feedback.
- We encourage active participation in team meetings and discussions.
- In-person company wide offsites 2x a year → Our next Offsite is first week of October 
- We have a public #private_dms channel on Slack 
<empty-block/>
Finding answers:
- Our single point of access to any information and questions is Claude. Find more information on how to use it effectively to find answers <mention-page url="https://www.notion.so/28ec370222d58076a535d4c9a11bf63f"/> 
### <br>Take time to explore our documentation:

PAGE 133: [page] Our Open Weights Opportunity
ID: 304c3702-22d5-802f-b407-da495ea60413
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/304c370222d5802fb407da495ea60413 as of 2026-04-16T15:37:31.031Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/304c370222d5802fb407da495ea60413" icon="💪🏼">
<ancestor-path></ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Our Open Weights Opportunity"}
</properties>
<content>
<callout color="gray_bg">
	**TL/DR: **Open weights licensing is BFL’s structural moat, and there is room to better frame this value. It represents the primary frontier image model enterprises can ***own***, ***customize***, and ***self-host*** without security, IP, jurisdiction, or supply chain risk. <span discussion-urls="discussion://317c3702-22d5-809d-9e81-001cf83f3765">Our previous self-serve price anchors</span> did not fully reflect the strategic upside of ownership and infrastructure control, and we can articulate more clearly why that control carries a premium. 
	The opportunity lies in 1) clarifying the enterprise value narrative, 2) aligning pricing with demonstrated willingness to pay, and 3) building tighter processes to convert high intent enterprise inbound, and 4) automating the long tail of startups and hobbyists so the team stays focused on high impact deals and strategic partnerships.
</callout>
<details>
<summary>☑️<span discussion-urls="discussion://317c3702-22d5-80cb-84b6-001c617b1830">** Next Steps: Decisions Needed**</span></summary>
	*Before this strategy is ready to execute, we need alignment on the following:*
	**🗳 Vote on the following: **
	- **Price point per tier:** **Are these the right anchors, or do they need to move? **We have good early validation on the Professional SKU at \$8K/month. We need a decision on Builder (\$500/month) and Platform (\$3,000/month). 
	- **Volume caps per tier:** **Do these caps create the right upgrade pressure, or are they too aggressive / too generous for the segments we're targeting? **The current proposal is 10K/month at Builder, 100K/month at Platform and Professional, and bespoke bands at Enterprise. 
	- **Add-on menu: Are there other add-ons worth building in? **The current menu covers fine-tuning/LoRA rights, additional models, additional domains, resell rights, and priority support. (e.g., on-prem deployment rights as a standalone add-on, evaluation license for prospects, fine-tuning compute credits)
	- **Definition of "ENT": What should that threshold be?** We need a working definition of Enterprise that lets us route inbound and prioritize sales effort without ambiguity. Proposal: any company with projected open weights ARR above \$200K/year qualifies for ENT level support and engagement. Anything below goes through the Platform or Professional path. 
	**💬 Input needed from the FDE team on Support tiers.** Before the add-on menu is final, we need the FDE team's input on what's actually deliverable at each support level, and whether there are any hard limits (e.g., number of supported tickets/month, response window by issue type) we should bake into the tier definitions before they're customer-facing.
</details>
<empty-block/>
## Our Strategy
---
<empty-block/>
📊 **A quick look at our Open Weights business**
---
BFL's [open weights models](https://bfl.ai/licensing) are distributed for free, but they generate direct commercial value by funneling developers into commercial licenses. Today, BFL offers two commercial licenses:
<columns>
	<column>
		1) The **Enterprise Commercial License **(via contacting our sales team);** **which brings in \$20M in annual revenue (not including Meta). This allows customers to host and fine-tune our models, and includes some ENT terms (e.g., indemnification). ENT customers are highly concentrated (80% of minimum commit comes from top 10 customers).

	</column>

<content>
## What is this Document?
<br>This document outlines both high-potential new customers and existing customers where we should go deeper.<br><br>Yes, this is an extensive list (by design). We don’t need to pursue everyone immediately, but having breadth gives us optionality, helps us learn from different buyer types, and ensures we're ready when the market moves. I believe this is a decent start at the companies and categories worth taking a swing at.<br>
<empty-block/>
**Note: This list isn’t perfect! <br>**Please feel free to add or even subtract companies. This should be a working document that we all collaborate on to ensure we get at-bats with the highest potential customers. There’s probably much more to be add in the “photorooms” of the world list.<br>Also, some of these could be existing customers that I missed, and if they’re worth going deeper into, please move them to the bottom list (of engaged customers). Lastly, I’ve included some companies that are in the pipeline but not yet closed or paying yet. {color="yellow_bg"}
## **How to Use This Document**
- **Owner Column**: Tagging team members to research and pursue specific accounts
	- This includes companies that may be in the pipeline. Let’s help each other out to close these deals. If anyone get’s too busy, or can’t break in, please re-assign. 
- **Reached Out?**: Track initial outreach status (leave blank until contact made)
- **Volume Score**: Estimated daily image generation volume (1-5 scale, 5 = highest)
- **Strategic Value**: Brand name recognition and market positioning value (1-5 scale, 5 = highest)
- **Next Steps**: Track follow-ups, demos, proposals, contracts, etc.
---

<content>
Current projects to cover:
- <span underline="true">Product Outpainting</span>
- <span underline="true">General Product Scenes</span>
# <span underline="true">Product Outpainting</span>
## Requirements
- <span underline="true">Product consistency</span>: the original product must remain visually identical in the extended output; no distortions, or structural changes to the source region.
- <span underline="true">Natural, understated extensions</span>: outpainted areas should feel like a seamless continuation of the scene, favoring neutral, realistic backgrounds over visually busy or surprising additions.
- <span underline="true">Latency</span>: no strict target currently (approximately 40 seconds), but lower is better as the pipeline matures.
---
## Customization Journey
### Generation Quality
- <span underline="true">Problem</span>: Most generative models tend to alter or distort the original reference region when extending an image, introducing color shifts, structural inconsistencies, or unwanted modifications.
- <span underline="true">Solution</span>: Performed a fine-tune of Flux 2 Flex specifically targeting the outpainting use case.
- Our fine-tune trains the model to treat the original image as fixed and inviolable while generating natural, coherent extensions around it.
- Additional prompt upsampling drives user preference for scene extensions, providing more room for customization as preferences evolve.

### Latency Improvements \[under review\]

# Workers
We need a good way to estimate our GPU needs
Right now we can partially estimate based on 
- TPS
- GPU/s
This can be done with this formula (little’s law)
Required GPUs = TPS × generation_time_seconds × GPUs_per_worker
This does not account for the gpus needed to support downstream shared processes
- Vllm deployments
	- batch size?
- Moderation deployments 
	- moderation input - batch size? 
	- moderation output - batch size? 
- Future support deployments
	- models for gtm endpoints
	- self hosted upsampling 
We assume no build up in the queue for this calculation as a build up in queue means increased end to end latency

### Threshold Analysis
To give our clients an idea of how moderation rates will change, I report moderation rates on negative content (false positives) and positive content (recall) for the current Azure model and the new in-house model.
How much a client’s overall moderation rate increases depends on the distribution of positive to negative prompts, and the distribution of classes of prompts that are being sent.
Note that the FP rates are calculated using the eval set, which is deliberately constructed to hold a lot of difficult examples. Hence, these FP rates are heavily inflated, and are used to illustrate the relative change between models.
