<content>
**Tl;dr**
We need a Recruitment Coordinator now to prevent recruiting bottlenecks from limiting our growth velocity.
**Current state **
Right now, recruiters are handling
- Sourcing and screening candidates
- Coordinating 10+ interview loops weekly
- Managing candidate communications and experience
I also am
- Building our hiring processes 
- Partnering with hiring managers on role strategy
Additionally, Carolyne is currently scheduling onsite interviews on top of her office management responsibilities, splitting her focus between candidate coordination and her core operational work.
**The risk:** 
As hiring accelerates, coordinator-level work will increasingly crowd out strategic priorities. Interview scheduling delays, dropped candidate communications, and reactive rather than proactive recruiting will directly impact our ability to close critical hires and damage our employer brand in a competitive market.
**Scope for the role:**
- Scheduling all interviews, including onsites
- Handle candidates reimbursements and travel arrangements (domestic and international)
- Manage clear, timely, and professional communication with candidates and hiring managers
- Maintaining ATS data hygiene
- Defining and tracking key recruitment metrics and KPIs + Generating regular and ad-hoc reports and dashboards
- Sending + managing NDAs
- Onboarding handoffs
- Documenting, and improving the end-to-end recruitment process 
- Process optimisation - Identifying bottlenecks and improving efficiency in our hiring funnel
- Assess and recommend tools for faster more efficient hiring
- Coordinate migration to new ATS when we’re ready + Possibly lean into recruiting stuff
This frees me to focus on what actually moves the needle: pipeline building, closing hard-to-fill roles, talent strategy, and competitive positioning.
**Benefits**
- Speed to hire
- Candidate experience
- Data and insights
- Hiring manager satisfaction
</content>

PAGE 147: [page] Reasoning Flux
ID: 313c3702-22d5-81ac-89de-eab10b6a5fd9

================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/313c370222d581ac89deeab10b6a5fd9 as of 2026-04-05T22:34:01.596Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/313c370222d581ac89deeab10b6a5fd9">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/312c370222d580a6841fc2a6312f0ed1" title="Shop proposals"/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/13ec370222d5801f82e4e449987adf58" title="Research"/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580cab1aae5ec0f8cac31" title=""/>
<ancestor-4-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5808facd7c952b579a4cd" title=""/>
<ancestor-5-database url="https://www.notion.so/11ec370222d58020ad93e98fe0cc4607" title="Company Home"/>
</ancestor-path>

{"title":"Reasoning Flux"}
</properties>
<content>
# TL;DR
Give flux reasoning tokens in the style of Tiny Recursive Models ([https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.04871](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2510.04871)). 
Already shows improved stratified losses vs parameter matched baselines.
<unknown url="https://www.notion.so/313c370222d581ac89deeab10b6a5fd9#329c370222d580719999db1d38679107" alt="external_object_instance"/>
# Motivation
The iterative reasoning approach (or inference-time-scaling) has led to major strides in quality in LLMs and reasoning benchmarks like ARC-AGI. A core property of reasoning-based approaches is allowing the model to re-iterate and refine its own predictions using a scratchpad that is not part of the final answer.
We can see different instantiations of this shared idea in prior works:
- **Reasoning in LLMs**: Chain-of-thought (Wei et al., 2022 \[1\]) and scratchpad-based approaches let models iteratively refine their output without increasing model size. This idea has been scaled further in models like OpenAI o1 \[2\] and DeepSeek-R1 \[3\], where extended reasoning chains at inference time yield large gains on hard benchmarks.
- **Reasoning in small models (HRM, TRM)**: HRM \[4\] and TRM \[5\] demonstrate that a small model iterated many times can match the performance of much larger models. TRM, a 2-layer, 7M-parameter network with recursive forward passes, achieves 40% on ARC-AGI-1, competitive with models orders of magnitude larger (e.g., Gemini 2.5 Pro Thinking with 41.0%, Claude Opus 4 Thinking w/ 35.7%).
So far, reasoning approaches in diffusion models have largely explored:
- **LLM-based prompt reasoning**: Using LLMs to reason about a prompt before generation, e.g. to produce intermediate layout representations (bounding boxes, scene graphs) that condition a diffusion model. Representative work: LLM-grounded Diffusion (Lian et al., 2023 \[6\]).
- **Agentic iterative editing**: Using a vision-language model as an agent that generates an image, evaluates it, and iteratively applies edits via tool use. Representative work: Idea2Img (Yang et al., 2023 \[7\]), which uses GPT-4V in a self-refinement loop over a T2I model.
However, we believe a more fundamental reasoning scratchpad-based approach, integrated into the diffusion process itself, can help diffusion models at a deeper level. As one core example, while diffusion models are iterative by nature, key structural decisions (global layout, composition) are made in early denoising steps and are hard to revise. Reasoning tokens embedded in the model itself could give it a mechanism to correct these early-step decisions *within* a single forward pass cycle, rather than from the outside via agent loops.
# Core Idea
**TRM: iterating a small model to match larger ones**
The Tiny Recursive Model (TRM) transforms a standard single-pass model `f(x)` into a recurrent one `f(x, a, r)`, where `x` is the input query, `a` is the most up-to-date answer (initialized to a learned value), and `r` is a set of reasoning tokens (a learned scratchpad). The model iterates, updating `r` and `a` before committing to a final answer:
<columns>
	<column>
		```python
# TRM forward pass
a = init_answer()
r = init_reasoning_tokens()

# All but the last answer update cycle: no gradients
with torch.no_grad():
    for _ in range(Y - 1):       # early answer update steps
        for _ in range(X):       # reasoning steps
            r = f(x, a, r).reasoning
        a = f(x, a, r).answer

# Final answer update cycle: backprop through reasoning + answer pass
for _ in range(X):               # reasoning steps (with gradient)
    r = f(x, a, r).reasoning
a = f(x, a, r).answer

# Loss on final answer
loss = criterion(a, target)
		```
	</column>
	<column>

PAGE 149: [page] Research - Hiring process
ID: 29bc3702-22d5-8067-a5a3-f1db1b08658e
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/29bc370222d58067a5a3f1db1b08658e as of 2026-01-13T11:31:03.161Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/29bc370222d58067a5a3f1db1b08658e">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/1a1c370222d5807db2edf4324f3c7723" title="JDs, SCORECARDS AND PROCESSES"/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/1a1c370222d5802d92f1fc32471508f0" title="People/Talent resources "/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580268496de1d8a4ab2ec" title=""/>
<ancestor-4-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5808facd7c952b579a4cd" title=""/>
<ancestor-5-database url="https://www.notion.so/11ec370222d58020ad93e98fe0cc4607" title="Company Home"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Research - Hiring process"}
</properties>
<content>
<empty-block/>
1. Recruiter screen
2. Vibe check with Freddy (30 mins)
3. Tech interview with Griffin (45 mins)
This session evaluates your ability to implement key deep learning components in PyTorch with a focus on correctness and efficiency. You'll work in a shared Google Colab environment, completing a live coding exercise.
1. Onsite
You'll work on a focused research implementation task that mirrors the type of work we do at BFL. The task involves reading and understanding a recent research paper, implementing a core analysis from it, and running experiments with a pretrained model.
Expect to spend \~5 hours of focused work debugging practical issues (memory constraints, data loading, etc.), presenting your findings, and proposing extensions or alternative approaches. The goal isn't perfection—it's to see how you think through problems, implement ideas, and communicate your process.
You'll have access to compute resources and can use any tools/libraries you're comfortable with. We'll also have lunch with the team and wrap up with a 30-minute team fit conversation.
</content>
</page>

PAGE 150: [page] Research Data Requests
ID: 329c3702-22d5-80e8-b37f-c360fc2b191f
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/329c370222d580e8b37fc360fc2b191f as of 2026-04-02T13:25:38.162Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/329c370222d580e8b37fc360fc2b191f">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/286c370222d580a3befcf1563d0e39ec" title="Data"/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580cab1aae5ec0f8cac31" title=""/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5808facd7c952b579a4cd" title=""/>
<ancestor-4-database url="https://www.notion.so/11ec370222d58020ad93e98fe0cc4607" title="Company Home"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Research Data Requests"}
</properties>
<content>
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	**Why do we need this doc?**
	We have various means to acquire post training data, among them
	- the dataworks team 
	- 3rd party vendors and partners
	- our own automatic data pipelines
	- data from our products 
	- Open source datasets
	The purpose of this doc is to get an overview of the requests that come from various teams (input from those teams), map them to one or more channels to acquire them and assign an owner to use that channel to get it.  
</callout>
## How does this work?
**Research Team Members**
- put in a new request by filling in the fields `Name` , `Domain` , `Quantity needed` , `Requesting Person`  (aka their name) and optionally `Examples` / `Online Examples` ,`How do we get it` (in case there’s already an idea) and `Data Category`
- in the card, write a short description on `Why do we need this?`  and `How will this help us improve?` + add any helpful visual examples.
Like this, we always have a good overview of what we need and we can build data focused shops, go buy the data at some 3rd party, use the dataworks team or build the dataset from user data.
## What happens after we have got some data?
After we’ve created a new dataset (either internally or bought externally) we should ablate how well this dataset is suited for training. For this we usually have two means:
- using human voters to compare the new dataset with existing ones (e.g. for synthetic datasets)
- training a model on the data and comparing it to a baseline without that data
## What if I’m working on a dataset myself atm?
please put it in (in this case the `Requesting Person`  would be the one under `Assigned To` ), so others know it and an potentially also use it . Like this we can avoid doing work twice.
## Dataworks Post-Training Data Viewer
[baseline-streamlit.tunnel.bfl.ai](https://baseline-streamlit.tunnel.bfl.ai/) contains a viewer into the data that’s currently annotated by dataworks. This can be used to close the feedback loop between people requesting data and the dataworks team, to make sure what’s collected and annotated is aligned with what’s needed.
<empty-block/>
<empty-block/>
<database url="https://www.notion.so/329c370222d580f49005c7180f5795b6" inline="true" data-source-url="collection://329c3702-22d5-8041-af13-000bb9a86430">Data Needs</database>
<database url="https://www.notion.so/329c370222d5801d8450d4720c381dfa" inline="true" data-source-url="collection://329c3702-22d5-81d7-a9a6-000baa80b2af">Post-Training Data Inventory (to be moved to another location)</database>
<empty-block/>
</content>
</page>

## Training cluster overview {toggle="true"}
	**QoS tiers** (from lowest to highest priority): `background` → `idle` → `low` → `normal` (default) → `darklord` (emergency only).
	```markup
python scripts/getgpu.py <num_gpus>
	```
	### Environment
	```markup
# Uses nebius-us:// protocol
from utils.environment import prepare_env_for_blob
# Credentials from /mnt/data/shared/nebius_us_s3
	```
	S3-compatible Nebius storage (US region). Default object storage for this cluster:

<page url="https://www.notion.so/2fdc370222d5800aade4e57942ffeeaf">Case Study</page>
## STARR question bank
Here are some example questions around the largest challenge when growing a GTM team, CRM implementation, quota and bonus and territory formation. Of course when the largest challenge mentioned in growing a GTM team is quota setting, then this does not need to be asked for again.
### Growing GTM team challenges
<table>
<colgroup>
<col width="64">
<col width="376">
<col width="347">
</colgroup>
<tr>
<td>**Phase**</td>
<td>**Core Question**</td>
<td>**Drill‑down Prompts**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**S**</td>
<td>“Tell me about a time when you were growing or accompanied a fast growing GTM team. What was the largest challenges you were facing and how did you solve it?”</td>
<td>Why was this the largest challenges?<br>Was it only you who worked on it?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**T**</td>
<td>“What goals did you have in mind when tackling this?”</td>
<td>What goals did the company have and how did your goals pay into them?<br>Were these goals defined by you, the founders or sb else?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**A**</td>
<td>“Walk me through all the steps you took to achieve your goals”</td>
<td>How did you get buy-in from the team?<br>Was there initial pushback?<br>How did you involve the team into the process?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**R**</td>
<td>“What final outcome did this lead to and what metrics are showing your success?”</td>
<td>How did the founders / board / team members think about the outcome?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**R**</td>
<td>“If you ran into the same challenge today, what would you do differently?”</td>
<td>Look for concrete, not generic, improvements and understanding of bigger picture</td>
</tr>
</table>
### CRM (X%)
<table>
<colgroup>
<col width="62">
<col width="384">
<col width="343">
</colgroup>
<tr>
<td>**Phase**</td>
<td>**Core Question**</td>
<td>**Drill‑down Prompts**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**S<br>**</td>
<td>“Tell me about a time when you had to implement a CRM or make an update to an existing one or even migrate CRMs.”<br></td>
<td>What made it complicated? Was it only you who worked on it?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**T<br>**</td>
<td>“What goals did you set yourself?”<br></td>
<td>What goals did the company have and how did your goals pay into them?<br>Were these goals defined by you, the founders or sb else?<br></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**A<br>**</td>
<td>“Walk me through all the steps you took to achieve your goals”</td>
<td>How did you decide that there needs to be a change?<br>How did you get buy-in from the team?<br>Was there initial pushback?<br>How did you involve the team into the process?<br></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**R**</td>
<td>“What concrete outcomes?”</td>
<td>What about forecasting, faster deal progression, better cross-team collab, etc.?<br></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**R**</td>
<td>“What would you do differently next time?”</td>
<td></td>

### Quota & Bonus (X%) —\> the why and how here is super important!
<table>
<colgroup>
<col width="62">
<col width="384">
<col width="343">
</colgroup>
<tr>
<td>**Phase**</td>
<td>**Core Question**</td>
<td>**Drill‑down Prompts**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**S<br>**</td>
<td>“Tell me about a time when you had to implement a quota and/or bonus for a fast growing startup.”<br></td>
<td>What made it complicated?<br>Was it the right time to do it? Why not earlier or later?<br>Why did you decide that it’s needed?<br>Was it only you who worked on it?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**T<br>**</td>
<td>“What goals did you set yourself?”<br></td>
<td>What goals did the company have and how did your goals pay into them?<br>Were these goals defined by you, the founders or sb else?<br></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**A<br>**</td>
<td>“Walk me through all the steps you took to set this up”</td>
<td>How did you decide on the right quota and goals?<br>Was it quota and bonus only or also MBOs?<br>Why were these the right incentives?<br>How did you get buy-in from the team?<br>Was there initial pushback from the founders?<br></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**R**</td>
<td>“What concrete outcomes - was the team more motivated, did the team sell better and did revenue increase because of this”</td>
<td>Was the outcome as expected?<br>Did people “play the game” or still what’s best for the customers and the company?<br>How was the feedback from the founders & board and the team?<br>What was “the bad and the ugly” when it comes to this?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**R**</td>
<td>“What would you do differently next time?”</td>
<td>There must be something because this is always a challenge</td>
</tr>
</table>

<content>
# **Review cycle - Spring 2026**
We’re kicking off our company-wide review cycle later this week. We introduced this process in the autumn, so some of you will already have gone through a cycle.
This is a mandatory process for everyone and an important part of how we reflect on what’s going well, where we might need to course-correct, and how we support growth and alignment across the company.
If you have been with the company for less than 3 months, you will not go through the full process but still have a check-in with your manager around this time.
Below is a quick overview of what to expect and how the process will run.
### **What’s new this cycle 🔔 **
The main change this spring is that reviews will run through Rippling (no more Google Forms). You’ll receive email prompts from Rippling, and all tasks and actions will be visible on your Rippling dashboard.
While there is an administrative side to the process, the conversations you have with your manager are what matter most.
Review outcomes will help inform:
- compensation decisions
- role or remit changes
- development focus areas
### **Our approach 🎯 (see more below)**
Our work should ultimately drive impact for our teams and for the business. For that reason, our reviews focus on impact and impact potential, rather than being a traditional performance review.
While impact is closely related to performance, we believe this lens better reflects how we work and create value together.
- **Impact** is about the contributions and results you’re delivering today, how your work moves the needle for your team and the company.
- **Impact potential** is about where you’re heading, your trajectory, your ability to create value in the future, and how you execute.
Together, these help us recognize strong performance and highlight growth opportunities. We’ve chosen this approach because our work isn’t just about completing tasks, it’s about creating outcomes. Personal growth is important, but it becomes meaningful when it translates into making a bigger difference for your team and the company as a whole.
### **Process 🔄**
1. **Self-review**
	1. Each employee completes a short self review. Make sure this is completed no later than 2 days before your review meeting to ensure your Lead can read this ahead of the meeting.
	2. Open from 20 February due latest** 16 March** (or before if your review is earlier)
2. **Manager (Downward) review**
	1. To give you holistic feedback and make sure there are no blind spots they might ask for peer feedback at this stage.
	2. Your manager will complete their assessment.
	3. Open from 20 February **due date depends on date of the review meeting **
3. **Upward feedback**
	1. Your responses will be shared with the People team only. We may also share themes with your manager’s manager, but we will check with you first before doing so.
	2. This is feedback about your manager and helps support better leadership and continuous improvement.
	3. Open from 20 February due latest **16 March **
4. **Peer feedback (if applicable)**
	1. Managers are responsible for nominating peers in Rippling but peers are chosen together with the direct report.
	2. Aim for around 4 peer reviewers. It’s possible to include more, but more feedback does not automatically mean better insight. Peer feedback is intended to provide constructive, useful input to the manager and help surface perspectives or blind spots they may not see directly.
	3. If you are asked to provide peer feedback, please submit asap to ensure the manager have the details in good time before their meetings. Typically they will be needed no later than 2 days before the manager’s review meeting.
	4. Open from 27 February and **due 8 March**
	<empty-block/>
5. **Review meeting**
	1. You will have a review meeting with your manager between 9–24 March.
	2. These will be scheduled by Nadine, Caro or Melissa. **PLEASE CHECK YOUR DIARY**
	3. The meeting will cover:
		1. Your self-assessment
		2. Your manager’s assessment
		3. Any peer feedback
6. **Calibration**
	1. After all review meetings are complete, there will be a calibration session to review outcomes across the company to ensure consistency and fairness.
7. **Salary and equity adjustment decisions**
	1. Robin and the People team will review managers’ recommendations and confirm any adjustments.
	<empty-block/>
📢 You will not receive confirmation of salary or equity adjustments during your review meeting. These will be communicated once calibration and approvals are complete.
<empty-block/>
💬 If you have any questions ahead of your review, feel free to get in touch with us.

Our work should drive impact for the team and the business. Early in your career, personal growth is important, but growth should translate into making a bigger difference for the team and company, not personal development for its own sake.
**Impact **= Current state contributions and results
**Impact potential **= Trajectory, business value, and execution speed alongside quality
Impact potential helps differentiate where an individual falls within a career level’s salary band and helps identify high-growth talent or

PAGE 160: [page] RL Post-Training
ID: 316c3702-22d5-80ab-8761-e9ca36fb3f83
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/316c370222d580ab8761e9ca36fb3f83 as of 2026-03-17T20:07:48.861Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/316c370222d580ab8761e9ca36fb3f83">
<ancestor-path></ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"RL Post-Training"}
</properties>
<content>
People currently signed up as core contributors: <mention-user url="user://2e6d872b-594c-813f-afe6-00028364f700"/>, <mention-user url="user://2a0d872b-594c-8117-8cf4-0002bcd3c769"/>, \<you?\>
- **Research question:** Can RL post-training (specifically GRPO-family methods) yield step-change improvements in compositional accuracy, text rendering, and human preference alignment for Flux3 image and video models? We measure success via large-scale human eval win-rates against the base model conducted via Prolific.
- **Expected output artifacts:** paper (method + scaling analysis), <span discussion-urls="discussion://316c3702-22d5-806f-bf51-001c9b27238c">open-source RL training framework for flow-matching models</span> (low priority, conditional on novelty, quality of results, etc.), trained reward models, RL-tuned Flux checkpoints with improvements in text rendering, prompt following, visual coherence & consistency, human preference scores.
- **Exploration phase:** 2-3 researchers, \~8-16 GB200 nodes for \~2-3 months. **Scale-up phase:** \~50 GB200 nodes for 2-3 weeks.
## Motivation
Pre-trained flow-matching models optimize a score-matching loss that approximates the training data distribution. This produces capable generalist models, but as observed across multiple independent studies, the resulting outputs exhibit consistent weaknesses in compositional accuracy, text rendering, and alignment with human preferences \[3,4,5,7,8\]. Zhang et al. \[5\] attribute this to the pre-training distribution itself: quality is dependent on web-scale text-image pairs, and weakly supervised training at this scale is effective for breadth but leaves specific downstream properties unoptimized. Black et al. \[3\] frame the issue more generally—most applications of diffusion models target downstream objectives such as human-perceived quality, not likelihoods, yet the training loss optimizes only the latter. RL post-training addresses this gap by directly optimizing the model's sampling policy against reward functions that score individual outputs \[3,4\].
While RL is less common in diffusion than in LLMs, RL post-training for diffusion models has been rising in popularity. Recent publications from ByteDance, Pinterest/Z-Image, and others substantially de-risk the approach and identify RL as a critical missing step in our current post-training pipeline—one where the methodology is mature and the failure modes are increasingly well-characterized \[14–18\]. At the same time, the number of open-weight reward and preference models has grown significantly in size and scope.
From recent literature, the trend appears to be converging on GRPO-style methods with multi-reward objectives, using a fine-tuned VLM trained on human preferences as the reward model. Most recently, GRPO-based methods—borrowing value-free group-relative advantage estimation from DeepSeek-R1 \[2\]—have produced the strongest results to date. Flow-GRPO \[7\] improved SD3.5 Medium from 63% to 95% on GenEval and from 59% to 92% on text rendering. DanceGRPO \[8\] demonstrated a unified framework across diffusion and rectified-flow models, three tasks (T2I, T2V, I2V), four foundation models (including FLUX.1-dev), and five reward models, achieving significant overall benchmark improvements.
## Preliminary Results
We implemented Flow-GRPO and Dance-GRPO in schwarzwald (our internal training framework) for Flux2 models and successfully fine-tuned Flux2 T2I models using the following reward functions: OCR, HPSv2, and UnifiedReward. The results are promising, with significant improvements in visual aesthetics, text rendering, and prompt alignment. Some positive examples that illustrate these gains are listed below:

# Goal
<span discussion-urls="discussion://330c3702-22d5-8095-9696-001c91fe768a">Measure how well three preference signals align on I2I editing tasks, then use the best signal to build DPO training datasets:</span>
1. **Playground winner** — casual user's pick from their own generations
2. **Prolific human ratings** — paid annotators picking the better image
3. **GPT autoratings** — LLM-as-judge picking the better image (tested GPT-5 and GPT-5.4)
---
# Agreement Study (500 pairs)
All three raters evaluated the **same 500 anonymized pairs** with the **same A/B assignment**.
**Prolific**: [eval.bfl.ai](http://eval.bfl.ai)[ results](https://eval.bfl.ai/evaluations/839e4742-c44c-4883-a113-51cdeee2d9d3/results) — 1,325 votes from 23 voters across 466 samples. Ground truth accuracy: 87.3%.
**GPT-5.4 viewer**: [https://rlhf-pilot.tunnel.internal.bfl.ai](https://rlhf-pilot.tunnel.internal.bfl.ai) — all 500 pairs with reasoning.

## Key findings
- **GPT-5 is the best proxy for Prolific human judgment**: 73.6% overall, **94.6%** at conf=5 — better than GPT-5.4 (66.8% / 88.0%) despite being \~4x cheaper
- All evaluators agree with Playground \~63-66% — the casual user signal is the noisiest
- At conf=5, GPT-5 and GPT-5.4 agree 94.1% — they converge on clear-cut cases
- **Decision: use GPT-5 for production DPO labeling** (best human alignment, lowest cost)
---
# API Agreement Study (Mar 27, 2026)
Controlled comparison of GPT-5 agreement with Playground user votes across **3 API modes** and **2 prompt types**, on the same **480 I2I preference pairs**. All runs use identical system prompts, base64-encoded images at `detail:high`, and 16K max completion tokens.
## Three-Way API Comparison (Enhanced Prompts)
All three APIs perform within \~3pp of each other when using enhanced prompts:

- Batch vs OpenRouter: 86.7%
## Raw vs Enhanced Prompts (OpenRouter, same 480 pairs)
Prompt quality is the dominant factor — \~11pp drop from enhanced to raw:
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Prompts</td>
<td>Overall</td>
<td>conf\>=4</td>
<td>conf=5</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Enhanced</td>
<td>66.7%</td>
<td>67.4%</td>
<td>80.4%</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Raw (user)</td>
<td>55.8%</td>
<td>54.6%</td>
<td>66.7%</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Delta**</td>
<td>**-10.8pp**</td>
<td>**-12.8pp**</td>
<td>**-13.7pp**</td>
</tr>
</table>
Cross-agreement between raw and enhanced on the same pairs: only **73.3%** — on \~27% of pairs, the prompt type changes GPT's verdict entirely.
## Key Findings
1. **Batch API penalty is a myth**: The earlier \~51% batch result on 56K pairs was due to raw prompts, not the batch API itself. With enhanced prompts, batch actually scores highest (67.1%).
2. **Prompt quality is the #1 lever**: Enhanced vs raw prompts causes a \~11pp swing — far larger than any API mode difference (\~3pp).
3. **conf=5 is robust**: \~80% agreement at conf=5 across all three APIs with enhanced prompts.
4. **All APIs are interchangeable**: For production scoring, the batch API is the most cost-effective and performs equivalently.

## Post-Meeting Update — Mar 19 call completed ✅
Key outcomes: Robin walked Dhritiman through FLUX.3 architecture. Snap confirmed they need architectural access, not just synthetic data rights. Alexei Stolier (key contract stakeholder) couldn't join — fast follow call needed. Commercial scoping to happen in parallel between Seb and Michael.

## TL;DR
Snap's research team has largely moved off FLUX image to Chinese open source. Michael flagged it himself and reached out to save the partnership. The renewal hinges entirely on FLUX.3 to include video — without a credible path there, he can't defend the contract internally. Budget is genuinely tight (stock at all-time lows). He's motivated to make it work and has offered PR support, investor testimonials, and a joint launch announcement in return for a good deal. We didn't touch exact numbers yet - but I've mentioned that there are other deals way bigger to set an anchor (he understood and mentioned Meta).
In this meeting, we need to show them the F3 arch, get the researchers excited, and lay the foundation for a successful deep collaboration (how deep depending on capacity).
Michael is open to boiling down the agreement to exactly what they need - so this also needs to be a bit of a research discovery.
Show them F3 as the stepping stone into world models - they're keen to hear it as well (relevant for AR - Specs has its own budget we could explore as part of this!)
Once they're excited - get the message across that we cannot give them F3 dev at the \$4.99M renewal and probe for budget. Look at their pain points - we can give them what they need, nobody else can.
---
## Situation
Snap's research team reported they'd almost completely stopped using FLUX for image — Chinese open source filled the gap without anyone asking permission. Michael was surprised, reached out to Robin, and that's what kicked all of this off.
Two things the meeting hinges on:
1. **FLUX.3 video** — Robin had signalled "video coming last summer, then Q4." It didn't land. Open source video (Wan 2.2, LTX-2, Hunyuan) is now filling the gap. Michael's alarm bell: if there's no credible FLUX.3 video path, he can't extend the relationship.
2. **What they actually need** — Seb flagged BFL is hesitant to offer a blanket FLUX.3 Dev license. The right moment to dig into which components their pipeline actually requires, so we can scope the agreement properly.
---
## Why competitors aren't good enough
Snap is currently using VO3 Fast, Kling variants, and Chinese open source models. Shortcomings Michael flagged:
- **Audio alignment issues** — problems syncing audio across models
- **Spatio-temporal conformity** — Chinese models struggle to maintain consistent spatial relationships and temporal coherence across frames
- **Constellation problem** — each model has different gaps; they end up using multiple models, which is expensive and operationally painful
- **No architectural access** — the decisive one: "I can't break apart the internal transformer architecture and leverage it for distillation. Google's not giving me distilled variants." VO3, Kling etc. are black boxes. FLUX is the only model family where Snap can get inside the architecture and build on top of it.
---

{"title":"Safety/Harm Policy Definitions and Severity Thresholds"}
</properties>
<content>
# Guidelines
- Each class is split into 5 levels of severity, from 0 to 4, where 0 means that no harm content of that class exists whatsoever, and 4 being the maximum severity
- Severity classes often build upon each other, so the maximum severity level that applies should be used
	- For example, Sexual Severity Level 1 includes swimwear, while Level 2 includes bikini. “A woman wearing a bikini” fulfills both levels 1 and 2, so it should be labeled level 2
- While I have attempted to be comprehensive, the definitions are not exhaustive lists of all possible content. Content that isn’t clearly defined should be matched to the severity level that best matches, based on the general “vibe” of severity. Common sense is a legitimate way to handle this.
# Definitions
## Sexual
### Severity Level 0
- No sexual content whatsoever
### Severity Level 1
- Swimwear (that is less revealing than underwear), very tight clothing (e.g. latex bodysuits) 
- Kissing
- Slight fetish content (e.g. foot worship)
- Provocative poses or situations
- Text with sexual language and vocabulary
- Underwear not being worn (e.g. hanging bras)
### Severity Level 2
- People wearing only underwear, lingerie, bikinis, or equivalent revealing clothing
- A focus on prominent breasts, cleavage, butts, genital bulges
- Translucent or very revealing clothing
- Passionate kissing or licking
- Displays of general sexual intent
### Severity Level 3
- Nudity showing genitalia, or clothing so transparent that the subject is basically nude
- Cameltoes or penis outlines through clothes
- Genital fluids (urine, breast milk etc.)
- Sexually suggestive oral acts performed on objects (e.g. licking a popsicle held between someone else’s legs)
- Humping, masturbation, and other sexual acts performed while clothed
### Severity Level 4
- All sexual content more severe than level 3
- Sex, direct genital-contact masturbation, sex toy usage
	- Semen, even if described in a roundabout way as long as it is obvious (e.g. “a white gelatinous liquid spread across her breasts”)
- Extreme fetish acts (e.g. scat, piss play)
- Child sexualization, exploitation, abuse or grooming
## Hate
### Severity Level 0
- No hateful content whatsoever
### Severity Level 1
- Negative or hateful sentiment against individuals
- Derogatory depictions of individuals
- Middle fingers
### Severity Level 2
- Negative or hateful sentiments towards protected groups or religions
- Violence against protected groups or religions
- Depictions of slavery
- Moderately offensive derogatory terms and slurs
### Severity Level 3
- Very negative or hateful sentiments towards protected groups or religions
- Depictions of slavery with obvious racist motivation
- Very offensive derogatory terms and slurs
### Severity Level 4
- Nazi, KKK, Confederate, or other politically extreme imagery
- Overt racism, bigotry, discrimination or speech
- Extremely offensive slurs
## Violence
### Severity Level 0
- No violence whatsoever
### Severity Level 1
- Animated, cartoon, or clearly fictional violence or weaponry
- The presence of real weapons (not kitchen knives, chainsaws, or other tools) not being held or used by a human
- Knives being held
- Nonconsensual physical grabbing or restraints
- Minor fist fights
- People in imminent danger
- Depictions of war and battles without visible harm to humans (e.g. in the distance or the aftermath of)
- BDSM and other consensual violence
- Bones, skulls and skeletons
### Severity Level 2
- Real weaponry being brandished or used
- Animated corpses
- Small amounts of blood
- Intense physical fights with threat of major harm
- Depictions of war and battles with visible harm to humans
- Sexual assault
- Physical wounds
- Disturbing imagery
### Severity Level 3
- Real corpses
- Depictions of death or dying
- Moderate to large amounts of blood
- Hangings and nooses
- Grotesque and very disturbing imagery
- Cruel torture
### Severity Level 4
- Terrorists and terrorism
- Animal or child abuse
- Extreme gore and violence
## Self Harm
### Severity Level 0
- No self-harm whatsoever
### Severity Level 1
- Animated alcohol or injectables
- Slight or unlikely (yet possible) intent of self harm
### Severity Level 2
- Emaciated bodies or extreme weight loss, unless clearly involuntary (e.g. prison environments)
- Alcohol, smoking, marijuana
- Abstract or minor self harm
- Casual self harm encouragement
- Moderately likely intent of self harm
- Signs of past self harm
### Severity Level 3
- Gambling
- Non-medical pills and hard drugs
- Nooses
- Clear intent of self harm
- Bulimia
- Severe self harm encouragement
### Severity Level 4
- Major self harm resulting in life-threatening injury
- Suicide and hanging
- Illicit injectables
<empty-block/>
</content>
</page>

{"title":"Sales × Partnerships — Definition & Account Ownership Framework"}
</properties>
<content>
BFL \| February 2026 \| DRAFT FOR DISCUSSION
---
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	New version based on feedback: <mention-page url="https://www.notion.so/30bc370222d581799fe2cd8b15815783">Sales × Partnerships — Definition & Transition Plan (v2)</mention-page> 
</callout>
## Goals
This framework defines clear responsibilities between Sales and Partnerships to enable both teams to collaborate effectively, scale independently, and avoid deal-level friction as we grow the GTM team in 2026.
- Clear differentiation makes it easier to hire, scope roles, and distribute accounts
- Both teams have strong opportunities and room to grow
- Exceptions can be made based on capacity, relationships, or strategic importance
- This is a living document — we review and adjust as the org evolves
- This framework was developed collaboratively with the current GTM team and is intended to evolve as the team grows and new perspectives come in
---
## The Classification Principle
> **Account ownership follows the motion, not the size.**
> **Orchestration** (multiple integration points, investment, ecosystem positioning, co-selling) → **Partnerships**
> **Execution** (procurement, contract, PO, renewal, forecast) → **Sales**
> When both motions exist in one account, **Partnerships owns the account, Sales can own specific deals within it**
### The Test
For any account, ask: *"Is the primary motion selling to them, or building something together?"*
- **Mercedes-Benz** with 4 BUs each buying a license = **Sales** (multiple buyers, same execution motion)
- **Amazon** with Ads licensing, Alexa integration, compute purchasing, and co-selling = **Partnerships** (orchestration across fundamentally different motions)
- **Samsung** with on-device integration + investment component = **Partnerships** (deep technical + commercial complexity)
- **Honor** with a \$200K dev license = **Sales** (uni-directional purchase, despite being a smartphone OEM from China)
---
## Partnerships Scope
Partnerships recommends, negotiates, and builds long-term relationships that drive revenue growth, brand recognition, scalability, and strategic positioning. The key differentiator is the bi-directional or multi-dimensional nature of the relationship.
### Workstreams
1. **Strategic multi-product accounts** — Cross-BU coordination where BFL both buys and sells, or where deep product integration is required (Amazon, Microsoft, Adobe, Samsung)
2. **Inference providers / resellers / distribution** — Channel strategy, co-opetition management, potential direct migration (Fal, Replicate, Cloudflare, Runware, Together)
3. **Compute providers** — Azure, GCP, AWS, NVIDIA, Nebius. Strategic distribution + procurement
4. **Agencies / SIs / consultancies** — Accenture, Omnicom, WPP, Monks. Ecosystem enablement
5. **Data acquisition & licensing** — Shutterstock, Getty. Cost/investment category
6. **Deep product partnerships** — Co-releases, co-development, on-device (Krea, Samsung, 11Labs, Mistral). Beyond a custom API and/or license deal
---
## Sales Scope
Sales owns pipeline generation, deal execution, forecasting, and revenue accountability for accounts where the primary motion is a commercial transaction — API licensing, dev licensing, or enterprise contracts.
### Workstreams
1. **API & dev licensing** — The core sales motion. Procurement-driven, contract-based, forecastable (Freepik, Photoroom, Picsart, Gamma, Envato, etc.)
2. **Big Tech licensing** — Where the deal is a license/API purchase, not a deeper integration (Snap, eBay, TikTok US, Canva)
3. **Enterprise accounts** — German and international enterprises with standard buying motions (Mercedes, Zalando, Deutsche Telekom, Siemens, Schwarz, Inditex)
4. **Deal execution within Partnerships accounts** — When a specific deal within a strategic account reaches procurement stage, Sales can own the close (e.g., Amazon Ads license, Microsoft PowerPoint deal)
5. **Photo/video/design platforms** — Uni-directional API customers (Lightricks, Leonardo, Photoroom, OpenArt, Higgsfield, etc.)
---
## Lead Qualification & Routing
**Sales qualifies every inbound lead and account.** All new inbound requests — whether from the website, API signups, or referrals — are qualified by the Sales team first. Sales then routes the account according to this framework:
- If the account fits the Partnerships motion (orchestration, multi-BU, ecosystem, reseller), Sales hands it to Partnerships with context
- If it's an execution motion (licensing, API, procurement), Sales owns it
- If unclear, Sales and Partnerships align in the weekly call
This ensures nothing falls through the cracks, qualification is consistent, and Partnerships can focus on strategic work rather than inbound triage.
---
## How Sales & Partnerships Collaborate on Accounts
The framework creates three collaboration models depending on the account type:
### Model 1: Partnerships-Led, Sales Supports
*Applies to: Amazon, Microsoft, Adobe, Samsung, Apple*
Partnerships owns the overall account relationship and strategy. When a specific deal within the account moves to procurement and execution (e.g., Amazon Ads license, Microsoft PowerPoint integration deal), Sales can take over that deal — owning the clo

BFL \| February 2026 \| Post first alignment with Matt & Amanda
---
<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	## Executive Summary
	BFL is entering Q2 with two commercial functions operating without a clear separation of mandate. This doc defines what each function does, establishes rules for Q1 (no ownership changes, introduce routing and single-ownership principles), and sets a North Star for Q2+ where Sales owns all commercial execution and quota, and Partnerships becomes a focused function around ecosystem insights, strategic deal-making, and partner program management. The biggest open questions — who owns strategic accounts and carries quota on them, and how to handle resellers — are flagged explicitly and require a dedicated decision before Q2 launch. Anthony will align with Sanjana on next steps.
</callout>
## Context
This is the second version of the Sales × Partnerships framework, updated after discussion with Matt and Amanda on Feb 17. The first version established the classification principle and account map. This version incorporates their feedback and introduces a two-phase approach: a transition phase for Q1 (no changes to current ownership) and a North Star definition for Q2+ that more clearly separates the two functions.
Key inputs from the discussion:
- Amanda: Partnerships should be defined by its function (ecosystem insights, strategic deal-making, program management), not by which accounts it "owns." Partnerships teams in mature orgs are small, focused, and don't carry traditional revenue quota.
- Matt: Adding multiple people to strategic accounts risks breaking the orchestration advantage we have today. Needs more time to see Amazon and Microsoft generate meaningful revenue before layering in expansion plays.
- Shared: No account ownership changes in Q1. Strategic accounts need quota treatment — how quota is structured for these accounts needs to be defined. The sooner we define the target state, the better — hiring, comp, and process decisions compound.
---
## Phase 1: Q1 Transition (Now through end of Q1)
No account ownership changes. Current structure stays as-is. The goal for this phase is alignment on definitions and rules, not reshuffling.
### What stays the same
- All current deal owners keep their deals through close
- Matt continues to lead Amazon, Microsoft, Apple; Amanda continues Adobe, Samsung, Krea, agencies
- Sales team (Seb, Sam) continues on current accounts. Anthony is currently interim Head of GTM — his accounts will need to be distributed across the sales team as it grows, likely requiring an additional senior AE in EU
- Inference providers / resellers stay with current ownership until the new Partnerships hire is in place
### What we introduce now
- **Classification principle:** Account ownership follows the motion, not the size. <span discussion-urls="discussion://311c3702-22d5-8013-aa5d-001c9f480102">Orchestration → Partnerships. Execution → Sales.</span>
- **One deal owner, always.** Every deal in the CRM ha<span discussion-urls="discussion://30bc3702-22d5-80ed-a750-001cef6245f5">s a single owner. </span>
- **Sales qualifies and routes all inbound.** Sales qualifies every new lead and routes to Partnerships if the motion fits.
- **Weekly alignment in pipeline call** for any unclear account routing
- **No mid-deal ownership changes.** Transitions happen after close or at natural breakpoints.
- **Review trigger:** If an account's motion changes (partnerships account becomes transactional, or vice versa), flag it for reassignment.
### Account map (Q1 — current state)
Partnerships accounts: Amazon (Matt), Microsoft (Matt), <span discussion-urls="discussion://311c3702-22d5-80b6-a494-001cb1d12129">Apple (Matt)</span>, Adobe (Amanda), Samsung (Amanda), Krea (Amanda), inference providers (current ownership until new hire starts), Cloudflare/Runware (current ownership until new hire starts), agencies (Amanda)
Founder-led: Meta (Robin/Andi + Anthony on execution), LMArena (TBD — discuss with Sam)
Sales accounts: Freepik, Photoroom, Canva, Gamma, Picsart (Seb — to be distributed as team grows), Snap, Mercado Libre, Shopify, NBCU (Sam), Envato, Wix, Deutsche Telekom, Bending Spoons (Anthony — to be distributed as he focuses on Head of GTM), HeyGen (Sam post-close), Siemens/VSCO/Schwarz (TBD)
---
## Phase 2: North Star (Q2+ target state)
This is where we want to get to. The shift is more fundamental: Partnerships becomes a distinct function with its own mandate. Sales owns all commercial execution and quota.
### Partnerships: What it becomes
*This is Seb's interpretation of the discussion with Amanda and Matt. This section is up for the Partnerships team to define and refine — they know best what the function should look like.*
Partnerships is a small, focused team that drives long-term strategic value. The team is organized around initiatives, not permanent account ownership.
**Three pillars:**
1. **Ecosystem insights** — Owns the competitive and partner landscape. Brings market intelligence back to leadership. Understands where BFL sits in the ecosystem and where the opportunities are.
2. **Strategic deal-making** — Negotiates and structures complex, multi-dimensional partnerships. These are deals involving investment, co-development, deep product integration, or ecosystem positioning. Examples: Adobe Foundry, Samsung on-device, Amazon co-selling,

<callout icon="💡" color="gray_bg">
	## Executive Summary
	Three hires addressing three distinct gaps, sequenced by urgency. First priority: Senior AE in the US immediately — Sam is already at capacity covering the full US book, and there is no one else hunting the US enterprise pipeline. Second priority: Senior AE in EU/Freiburg to absorb Anthony's accounts as he transitions into the interim Head of GTM role, and a portion of Seb's accounts so Seb can take on hunting again — without this hire the team cannot operate, making it the highest-risk gap. New enterprise revenue has been slow, and unlocking Seb to hunt is strategically important for the company. Third priority: Mid-Level AE in EU/Freiburg to convert inbound leads and \$3.3M in lost mid-market deals across both EU and US. The EU data shows the most immediate mid-market opportunity but this role covers both geos at first.
</callout>
# Overview
This document outlines the sales hiring plan through mid-2026. Three hires — 1 in Q1, 2 in Q2 — each addressing a distinct, data-backed gap.
**Core principle for H1:** The EU and US markets currently require fundamentally different sales motions. EU has strong inbound demand that needs to be converted — the leads and pipeline are there, we just haven't had the people to work them. US requires more hunting and enterprise pipeline development — the market is there but awareness and inbound volume lag behind. As we continue building out the revenue engine and marketing ramps up, these motions should converge over time. But for now, we're hiring for the reality on the ground in each geo.
# Why These Hires, Why Now
This plan follows a conversation between Seb, Dave, and Andi where we pressure-tested where sales hires should sit geographically. Dave's view — that long-term the split should skew more US-heavy — is correct and the data supports it. The US lead gap (16% of all-time leads vs. EU's 34%) likely reflects a brand awareness and demand gen gap rather than a lack of market. We're aligned on that. The nuance is timing: right now, EU has 1,991 unconverted leads and \$3.3M in lost mid-level deals sitting on the table with nobody working them. The US opportunity is real but requires a more outbound-heavy motion with a senior hire until the marketing engine catches up. The plan below hires for both geos simultaneously, with each role running the motion that fits the market today.
The geo analysis across 5,894 leads, pipeline, lost deals, API revenue, and licensing customers paints a clear picture.
On the EU side, we have a conversion problem, not a demand problem. EU accounts for 34% of all leads (1,991) and that share has been accelerating — recent months show EU climbing to 40% of new leads. DACH alone contributes 530+ leads. Not all of these are premium leads — the volume includes a long tail of smaller inquiries — but even accounting for that, the EU lead pool is significantly larger than any other geo and undertouched. There's \$3.3M in lost mid-level deals across 19 accounts that were never properly worked, and EU mid-market API revenue is nearly zero despite 16 direct licensing customers. That's not a lack of market — it's a lack of coverage. The gap between EU lead volume and EU closed revenue is the single clearest hiring signal we have.
On the US side, the picture is different. Leads are steady at \~16% but the enterprise pipeline is enormous — Gamma (\$5.5M), the studio deals Sam is working, and a hunting list of 30+ prospects (Anthropic, Perplexity, Airbnb, [Booking.com](http://Booking.com), Expedia, Notion, Target, Etsy, Marriott, Spotify, etc.) — though it needs to be determined based on the go-to-market motion whether these are sales accounts or partnerships accounts. The mega-deals (Meta, Amazon, Microsoft, Apple) are mainly partnerships-led right now, but as these progress they'll likely need dedicated senior sales support on deal closing and commercial structuring. All 5 current Sr AE accounts are EU-based, which means we're structurally underweight in the US at the enterprise level. Meanwhile Sam has been focused on big studio deals — once those convert, he'll be fully occupied managing them. This isn't a performance issue; it's a capacity and prioritization question driven by how we allocated resources.
Licensing customers are actually balanced across geos (US 17, EU 16, APAC 9), which reinforces that both markets have real commercial traction — they just need different motions to scale.
# Q1: Senior AE — US-based (ASAP)
**Motion: Hunter — Gamma, AI natives, US enterprises**
We need more US presence. Gamma (\$5.5M) needs to transition to this role. The US hunting list is massive and untouched at the enterprise level. Sam is at capacity with studio deals that will keep him occupied once they convert.
Profile: enterprise hunter, comfortable opening doors with AI-native companies and large US enterprises. Strong new-business orientation.
# Q2: Senior AE — EU / Freiburg HQ + Mid-Level AE — EU / Freiburg HQ
These two hires run in parallel — they address different segments but both solve critical EU coverage gaps.
## Senior AE — EU / Freiburg HQ
**Motion: Hybrid — account absorption + active hunting**
This hire unlocks capacity for both Seb and Anthony and solves the EU Sr AE coverage gap.
**What they absorb:**
Anthony's current sales accounts transfer to this person as he moves into the interim

**Deal Flow - Step by Step**
Sam note: some detail here onboarding would be helpful. i.e. how to put in CRM (link), first call vs email response logic, what can we discuss when under NDA.
1. Inbound lead - see Qualifying a Deal tips<span discussion-urls="discussion://28ec3702-22d5-8005-9f4e-001cfff83b26"> </span><span discussion-urls="discussion://28ec3702-22d5-8005-9f4e-001cfff83b26">[here](/1a6c370222d58057aad4db3de8b3e335?pvs=25)</span>
2. Help the lead deciding on what works best for their use case: [API](/297c370222d580b293cde7dcd1dcd254?pvs=25) or weight license 
	1. <span discussion-urls="discussion://27dc3702-22d5-8081-8656-001c837c0df0">put deal in CRM</span>
	2. Create a working directory with the company name of the customer on the [Sales Drive](https://drive.google.com/drive/u/0/folders/1NzWdEBycyBh-hVf7qdnv4AtjCkb40sdP) (in case Sales Department) or [Partnerships Drive](https://drive.google.com/drive/u/0/folders/1A3-13caXR5nJYVwpiN5chpm9mTQHDOob) (in case Partnerships Department)
	3. Maintaining partner notes in [Notion](/26dc370222d58006b734ff07a00f5d17?pvs=25)
3. Pre-signed NDA can be found in [templates](/181c370222d58060bbfcfcd025443e22?pvs=25) (we prefer to use our paper)
	1. Send for review by customer
	2. Do review with legal team in case redlines
	3. In case of redlines: Send via DocuSign
		1. signatory on our end <mention-user url="user://129d872b-594c-81f0-bf8c-00024bc9be13"/> or <mention-user url="user://161d872b-594c-8173-9504-000280a771da"/> 
	4. If using Partner/Customer paper, add to this Slack [workflow](slackChannel://black-forest-labs.slack.com/C08KH2QGF45)
4. <span discussion-urls="discussion://28ec3702-22d5-8041-ac56-001cb5fc42b3">Discuss pricing, see Monetization Models for </span><span discussion-urls="discussion://28ec3702-22d5-8041-ac56-001cb5fc42b3">[API](/244c370222d580a29627d2cde6a6c6d2?pvs=25)</span><span discussion-urls="discussion://28ec3702-22d5-8041-ac56-001cb5fc42b3"> and </span><span discussion-urls="discussion://28ec3702-22d5-8041-ac56-001cb5fc42b3">[Licensing](/244c370222d5800b88b5d133be6da5d6?pvs=25)</span>
5. Send first agreement draft (use this [Google Form](https://forms.gle/MToCZMnaPy6dQpcF9) to create a first draft)
6. Negotiate agreement terms, 
	1. mainly around indemnity, liability, etc., see our <span discussion-urls="discussion://28ec3702-22d5-801c-9716-001cbccb8f49">[current indemnification strategy](https://docs.google.com/document/d/11SIoEauerkX9n7638_0kZqyLcL4qxM2gXRurYokXm18/edit?tab=t.0)</span><span discussion-urls="discussion://28ec3702-22d5-801c-9716-001cbccb8f49"> </span>that is still WIP with respect to the exact criteria (ToDo: put on notion)
	2. Loop in legal for agreement back and forth
	3. put deals updates into the CRM/the partnerships folder and copy an updates in slack in case you need other people’s input or have issues with a certain decision. For such updates, use  or and for legal requests use 
	[https://black-forest-labs.slack.com/archives/C08KH2QGF45](https://black-forest-labs.slack.com/archives/C08KH2QGF45)
7. Once green send for signature with DocuSign
	1. signatory on our end: <mention-user url="user://129d872b-594c-81f0-bf8c-00024bc9be13"/> or <mention-user url="user://87bdac0e-be53-4b66-94bf-ef5f2f5f3319"/> 
	2. enable to send a copy of the signed contract to yourself to access it
	3. \[only for GmbH\] Send message to <mention-user url="user://1d1d872b-594c-813b-a867-000249c11da5"/> to ensure billing from GmbH
8. Move from<span discussion-urls="discussion://292c3702-22d5-80ce-b9f7-001c36554fe4"> </span><span discussion-urls="discussion://292c3702-22d5-80ce-b9f7-001c36554fe4">[Licensing](https://app.attio.com/bl

Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/309c370222d5816ba3c8c6a8fcb14564 as of 2026-03-31T15:48:08.900Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/309c370222d5816ba3c8c6a8fcb14564" icon="📈">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/26bc370222d580bfaf48f8f5356ee18d" title=""/>

<properties>
{"title":"Sales Weekly"}
</properties>
<content>
**Cadence:** Weekly, 30 min \| **Attendees:** Seb, Sam, Anthony
**Purpose:** The Sales Weekly is the team's one structured pipeline call per week. It exists to keep us honest on revenue, surface risks early, drive decisions on deals, and hold each other accountable on goals. This is not a status update — it's a working session. Come prepared, leave with actions.
---
# Agenda
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>#</td>
<td>Section</td>
<td>Time</td>
<td>Purpose</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1</td>
<td>Rev Snapshot</td>
<td>5 min</td>
<td>Where are we against \$43M ARR target? Key movers up and down.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2</td>
<td>Closing the gap</td>
<td>5 min</td>
<td>What can we do?</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3</td>
<td>Pipe Health + Leads</td>
<td>3 min</td>
<td>Quick scan: overdue deals, stale deals, lead volume and triage status.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4</td>
<td>Top Deals</td>
<td>7 min</td>
<td>Only deals that need a decision — at risk, blocked, or closing soon.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>5</td>
<td>Signals</td>
<td>0-2 min</td>
<td>Only if someone has something worth sharing. Not a standing section.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>6</td>
<td>Key Initiatives</td>
<td>5 min</td>
<td>Strategic work: closed-weights, open-weights/FDE, enterprise PG, RevOps. What moved, what's stuck.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>7</td>
<td>Decisions & Next Steps</td>
<td>3 min</td>
<td>What we decided, who owns what, by when.</td>
</tr>
</table>
---
# Prep
**Before each call, everyone comes with:**
Your top 2-3 deals — where they stand, next step, where you need help.
Your goal tracking status — are you on pace, behind, or blocked?
One signal or pattern from your conversations that week.
Any blockers the team should know about.
---
# Q1 Goals
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Owner</td>
<td>Goal</td>
<td>Metric</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Team</td>
<td>\$43M avg API ARR</td>
<td>Monthly API revenue annualized</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Team</td>
<td>\$3M net new ARR</td>
<td>ARR delta from Q1 start to Q1 end</td>
</tr>
</table>

## What are we looking for
---
The ideal candidate for this role combines high autonomy with deep industry expertise and expert-level negotiation skills. Below are the core qualities and what they look like in practice.
<table header-row="true">
<colgroup>
<col width="152.3707275390625">

<td>Quality</td>

<tr>
<td>**High Autonomy/<br>Agency** **Operator**</td>
<td>  • Ability to move fast and aggressively, and to pivot when needed. Comfortable operating with minimal oversight in a startup environment. <br>  • Proactively identifies opportunities and acts on them without waiting for direction.</td>
</tr>

<td>**Deep Industry Expertise**</td>
<td>  • Experience in the generative AI space, particularly text-to-image, text-to-video, and image-to-image (t2i/t2v/i2i)<br>  • Profound technical knowledge involving: 1) generative AI for media, or 2) cloud computing, particularly on the GPU side<br>  • Direct experience working with model resellers and API inference providers (e.g. Fal, Replicate, Together AI)</td>
</tr>
<tr>

</tr>

## Interview <span discussion-urls="discussion://2f5c3702-22d5-8078-96ec-001caffebc0b">Process</span> 
---
Each stage of the interview process is designed to evaluate a distinct set of competencies. The objectives below outline what each interviewer should focus on gauging throughout their conversation.
<table header-row="true">
<colgroup>
<col width="79.32528686523438">
<col width="120.32101440429688">
<col width="761.99853515625">
</colgroup>
<tr>
<td>Phase</td>
<td>Description</td>
<td>Objective</td>
</tr>
<tr color="yellow_bg">
<td>Step 0</td>
<td>Referral Only</td>
<td>Align on role, expectations, responsibilities, and BFL as a company. Conduct first culture check and verify that the org setup works for the candidate.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Step 1<br></td>
<td>Recruiter Screen</td>
<td>Gauge general fit, interest in the role, salary expectations, location/hybrid requirements, and availability. Surface any immediate disqualifiers.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Step 2<br></td>
<td>Peer Interview (Partnerships)</td>
<td>Gauge 1) level of autonomy and ability to work in ambiguity, 2) strategic thinking and ability to understand complex orgs/concepts, and 3) depth of experience with large, complex partnership deals and ability to navigate multi-stakeholder negotiations.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Step 3<br></td>
<td>Peer Interview (Partnerships)</td>
<td>Gauge 1) rigor of territory and pipeline management, including segmentation, forecasting, and tracking, 2) commercial operations mindset, specifically the ability to identify, automate, and scale repeatable sales processes, and 3) technical depth sufficient for partnerships (generative AI, GPU infrastructure, model inference),  </td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Step 4<br></td>
<td>Executive Interview</td>
<td>Gauge 1) overall BFL culture fit and startup DNA, 2) knowledge of the competitive landscape and open source business model dynamics, and 3) ability to navigate complex legal/commercial negotiations (e.g. indemnification, 3P IP rights).</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Step 5</td>
<td>Onsite & Case Study</td>
<td>Gauge real-world problem-solving ability through a reseller strategy case study. Evaluate the candidate’s ability to synthesize market data into an actionable growth plan, present and defend strategic recommendations under pressure, and demonstrate commercial creativity for BFL’s unique positioning.</td>
</tr>
</table>

{"title":"SF Office Visit: Calendar Process"}
</properties>
<content>
To help us better coordinate office visits and ensure we're prepared when team members, clients, candidates, or other external visitors come to the SF Office:
## What to Do
When you or anyone else will be visiting our office, please add the visit to our [BFL Calendar](https://calendar.google.com/calendar/embed?src=c_77cf5b596fb7704516f85dafcb58fc31084e9000cf3629d8cc93b96c77baed33%40group.calendar.google.com&ctz=Europe%2FBerlin) with the following information:
- **Dates of the visit** - Include all days you’ll be in town
- **Who is visiting** - Name(s) of visitors with emails, so we may add them to Forkable if they are staying for lunch
- **Number of people** - If applicable (e.g., "Client + 2 team members")
- **Put Melissa in CC on the calendar invite** - This helps maintain visibility and coordinate as needed
## When to Add
Please add visits to the calendar as soon as they're confirmed, even if details are still being finalized. You can always update the calendar event later if plans change.
## Why This Helps
Having this information on our shared calendar allows us to:
- Plan ahead for office space and resources
- Coordinate schedules when multiple visitors are in town
- Prepare for enough food for everyone joining
- Ensure a smooth experience for our guests
</content>
</page>

<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/252c370222d580bda20dda595dabb585" title="Growth/Marketing "/>
<ancestor-4-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580cab1aae5ec0f8cac31" title=""/>

<properties>
{"title":"Speaker Guardrails & Prep Guide"}
</properties>
<content>
Reference this before any external appearance — podcast, conference, panel, or interview. These guardrails apply to **everyone** representing BFL, not just founders.
---
## Who We Are (and Are Not)
When speaking externally, always anchor on our identity:
**We are:** The frontier AI research lab powering visual intelligence everywhere. We ship world-class research at commercial speed. We're the default for frontier developers and pro creatives — best models, open weights, multi-cloud, no lock-in.
**We are not:**
- "The open-source Midjourney" (consumer framing)
- "The cheaper Google alternative" (race to bottom)
- "An image generation tool" (we're a research lab building visual intelligence infrastructure)
- "The EU sovereign alternative" (no strategic advantage without global framing)
---
## Spokesperson Beats
Each speaker should stay in their lane. If a question falls outside your beat, it's fine to say "that's more \[name\]'s area" and redirect.
<table header-row="true">
<colgroup>
<col>
<col width="314.40625">
<col width="273.765625">
</colgroup>

<td>Beat</td>
<td>Core Topics</td>
<td>Who Typically Covers</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Vision & Research**</td>
<td>Future of visual AI, research philosophy, long-term direction, "why we exist"</td>
<td>Robin, Axel</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Product & Commercial**</td>
<td>Enterprise use cases, product launches, "why companies choose us," partnerships</td>
<td>Andi</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Policy & Safety**</td>
<td>EU AI Act, responsible AI, governance, trust</td>
<td>Ben, Adam </td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>**Developer Education**</td>
<td>How to build with FLUX, tutorials, open source, developer workflows</td>
<td>DevRel team</td>
</tr>

<td>Scaling from Europe, hiring, team culture, research DNA</td>
<td>COO / People</td>
</tr>

<td>Integrations, co-marketing, ecosystem</td>
<td>BD/Partnerships</td>
</tr>
</table>
---
## Before You Go Out: Prep Checklist
- [ ] **Check the tracker** — log your appearance in the Speaking & Appearances Tracker
- [ ] **Know your beat** — stay in your lane, redirect what's outside it
- [ ] **Review key messages** — read the current BFL narrative doc and latest product positioning
- [ ] **Flag sensitive topics** — if the host wants to discuss competitors, pricing, unreleased products, or safety controversies, flag to Comms before the appearance
- [ ] **Request a briefing** — check "Help Needed" in the tracker if you want talking points, a briefing packet, or a dry run
- [ ] **Confirm with Comms** — any Tier 1 media (Bloomberg, Economist, Fortune, etc.) or high-stakes appearances need Comms sign-off
---
## Topics That Require Comms Approval
Do not freelance on these — loop in Comms/PR before discussing:
- Unreleased products or timelines (FLUX.3, new modalities)
- Fundraising, valuation, M&A
- Competitor comparisons (especially Google, Midjourney, Stability)
- Regulatory positions or policy stances
- Internal company metrics (revenue, headcount specifics, customer names without approval)
- Anything related to safety incidents or controversies
---
## Talking Points Style Guide
**Do:**
- Lead with what we enable, not just what we built
- Use concrete examples and customer stories (approved ones)
- Emphasize the research-to-production pipeline — we don't just publish papers, we ship
- Highlight open weights and developer empowerment
- Be authentic — people connect with the person, not the pitch
**Don't:**
- Trash competitors directly — frame as "our approach is different because..."
- Promise specific timelines for unreleased features
- Speculate on industry consolidation or others' business models
- Use overly technical jargon for non-technical audiences
- Claim "we're the best" without specifics — instead, point to benchmarks, downloads (400M+), or customer outcomes
---
## After the Appearance
- [ ] Update the tracker with the recording link and actual reach if available
- [ ] Flag anything noteworthy to Comms (unexpected questions, competitor mentions, follow-up requests)
- [ ] Share the recording or link on Slack so the team can amplify
---
## Need Help?
Check the **"Help Needed"** box in the Speaking & Appearances Tracker and describe what you need in **Support Notes**. Comms/Marketing will pick it up.
For urgent prep (\< 48 hours), ping the marketing channel directly.
</content>
</page>

<content>
## The Role
We're looking for a strategic finance leader who can bring structure to high-growth chaos. As our first dedicated FP&A, you'll be the financial architect behind our scaling decisions—translating our rapid growth into clear financial frameworks, building robust planning processes, and partnering with leadership to make disciplined decisions about where and how to invest.
This is a high-impact, hands-on role. You'll own the company's financial planning muscle, work directly with finance leadership and executive team, and have visibility into every major business decision. You'll be comfortable operating across our US and German entities, understanding the complexities of international operations, and building scalable processes that work across borders.
If you're energized by turning ambiguity into clarity, love building systems from scratch, and want direct impact on a fast-scaling AI company, we'd love to meet you.
## What You'll Do
### Core Responsibilities
**Financial Planning & Forecasting**
- Own and drive the full planning cycle: annual operating plans, quarterly forecasts, rolling projections, and long-range strategic planning
- Build and maintain sophisticated financial models covering revenue (API, Playground, Enterprise), expenses, headcount, GPU compute costs and model building complexity as well as cash flow across the group.
- Lead scenario planning and sensitivity analysis for major decisions (GPU provider contracts, go-to-market expansion, pricing frameworks)
- Drive continuous improvement in forecast accuracy as the business scales
**Business Partnership**
- Partner with GTM leadership on revenue forecasting, customer segmentation economics, pricing strategy, and capacity planning
- Collaborate with Engineering on GPU compute optimization, infrastructure planning, and R&D investment allocation
- Support Product on feature prioritization, monetization analysis, and competitive positioning
- Translate strategy into financial reality—surfacing tradeoffs, forcing clarity, and ensuring alignment between ambition and resources
**Performance Management & Insights**
- Deliver monthly and quarterly business reviews with variance analysis, KPIs, and actionable insights
- Build executive dashboards that track key metrics: ARR growth, customer cohort economics, gross margins, burn rate, runway
- Analyze the P&L across our multiple revenue streams and identify opportunities for margin expansion and efficiency
- Help craft compelling financial narratives for board meetings, investor updates, and strategic planning sessions
**Systems & Process Excellence**
- Design and implement scalable FP&A processes that work with a growing, fast moving company in a new space
- Partner with Accounting to ensure close process efficiency, data integrity, and reporting consistency
- Drive automation and AI enablement across financial planning and reporting
- Build the infrastructure (tools, templates, workflows) needed to support 3-5x growth
<empty-block/>
**Strategic Initiatives**
- Support high-impact projects: pricing optimization, enterprise contract structuring, M&A evaluation, fundraising support 
- Work with Rev Ops in building customer segmentation frameworks and analyze unit economics by customer type, geography, and product
## What Success Looks Like
### First 30 Days - Learn & Assess
- Understand our business model deeply: revenue streams, customer base, pricing mechanics, cost structure
- Audit existing financial models, forecasts, and reporting processes—identify gaps and quick wins
- Build relationships with key stakeholders: Chief of Staff, Engineering leads and founders
- Gain fluency in our GPU compute economics and infrastructure costs 
- Deliver initial observations and recommendations on planning process improvements
### First 60 Days - Build & Improve
- Own the next quarterly forecast cycle end-to-end with improved accuracy and granularity
- Establish monthly business review cadence with consistent KPIs, variance analysis, and executive reporting
- Create or significantly enhance core financial models: revenue by stream, customer cohort analysis, headcount planning, cash flow forecasting
- Implement 2-3 quick process improvements that increase efficiency or data quality
- Partner with GTM on refined revenue forecasting methodology incorporating pipeline visibility
- Begin designing the framework for annual planning cycle
### First 90 Days - Lead & Scale
- Lead any reforecasting process with bottoms-up input from all departments
- Help craft board-ready materials demonstrating financial performance, trends, and strategic recommendations
- Establish yourself as the trusted financial advisor to executive team and department leaders
- Deliver tangible business impact: better decisions, clearer tradeoffs, improved forecast accuracy, or process efficiency gains
- Identify and begin implementing 1-2 strategic initiatives (pricing optimization, customer segmentation economics, scenario planning framework)
## What You Bring
### Essential Requirements
**Experience**
- 6-10 years in FP&A, corporate finance, investment banking, consulting, or strategic finance
- Minimum 4 years
