PAGE 179: [page] System Design
ID: 2fec3702-22d5-80b3-95cf-d8623cf16594
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/2fec370222d580b395cfd8623cf16594 as of 2026-03-31T10:13:32.211Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/2fec370222d580b395cfd8623cf16594" icon="📐">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/302c370222d5805bb374c9feb28c32f6" title="Remote"/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/2fec370222d580739e06ec07c6222557" title="Candidate Tasks / Interviews"/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/2fec370222d5805cb8b3fcf92fadb954" title="Interview Overview"/>
<ancestor-4-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5807fb7c0eb922fb34933" title=""/>
<ancestor-5-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d58077a66cd99e2610a55b" title=""/>
<ancestor-6-database url="https://www.notion.so/176c370222d5803c934adcd0a808503d" title="Teamspace Home"/>
<ancestor-7-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d58015a265e74c39291a26" title=""/>
<ancestor-8-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5802a8de1c30265a40e1f" title=""/>
<ancestor-9-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580a4a8e4d99fcae5a220" title=""/>
<ancestor-10-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d58049bca3fb73abb1cafb" title=""/>
<ancestor-11-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580ae8cb9d4f9ca406bb2" title="Engineering"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"System Design"}
</properties>
<content>
For remote whiteboarding 
- [https://excalidraw.com/](https://excalidraw.com/)
**Timing** 
- 1 hour 
**Structure**:
- Introductions - 2-3 min
- Brief discussion of technical assignment - we ask any questions we had (if the assignment  raised any questions) - timing depends on a situation but good to keep it minimal. Sometimes it can take longer if assignment raised questions we wanna clear up.  
- We move onto the system design discussion - 45 min
- We reserve time for the end section - questions listed below.  7-8 min
# Task Overview
Context: 
You have just implemented a small computation takes **\~5 seconds** per task. The worker loads a large model on startup (\~40 seconds cold start). 
# Communication with Candidate 
# Rubric
1. Where would you place broker, task backend, postgres etc and how would communication go end to end for one task ?  How would you provide the task result to the customer who submitted the task ? 
2. Your company has acquired a second GPU cluster in the same region. It has identical hardware.
	-  How do you distribute tasks across the two clusters? What are the tradeoffs between a shared queue vs. per-cluster queues with a router?
	-  At worker startup it loads a large model into GPU memory. How does this affect your scaling strategy compared to stateless workers? What happens when you need to roll out a model update across both clusters?
	- What happens if one cluster goes offline? How does the system detect this and react? Consider what happens to in-flight tasks on the failed cluster.
	- How do you ensure result retrieval still works when the task may have been processed by either cluster?
3. You are expanding from **US-East** to **EU-West** to reduce latency for European users and comply with data residency requirements. 
	- Draw or describe the target architecture. Which components are regional and which are global?
	- Users and their credit balances: does each region get its own database, or do they share one? What are the consistency implications of each choice?
	- \[Depend on the previous answer\]A user submits a task in EU-West but their account was created in US-East. How does authentication and credit deduction work?
	- Task routing: should a task submitted in EU-West always be processed by EU-West GPU workers, or can it overflow to US-East? What are the latency and cost implications?
	- How do you handle a regional outage? Can EU users fail over to US, and what does that mean for data residency?
4. Add a priority system - Product wants to offer **three tiers**: Free, Pro, and Enterprise. Enterprise tasks should be processed before Pro, and Pro before Free — but no tier should be fully starved.
	- How do you implement priority at the queue level? Consider weighted fair queuing, separate queues with priority consumers, or other approaches.
	- What happens to priority ordering when you have multiple clusters and regions from Parts 1 and 2?
	- An Enterprise user submits 10,000 tasks at once. How do you prevent this from starving other Enterprise users? Is per-user fairness different from per-tier priority?
5. Scale this architecture 100x - 
Traffic has grown 100x from the original design. You now serve 50,000 concurrent users submitting \~200 tasks/second across both regions.
Discuss the evolution of bottlenecks as you scale up. For each bottleneck, explain how you would address it and what becomes the next bottleneck after that.
1. Cascading failures - scalability event aka new model launch with unpredictable traffic.
You are launching a new model. Based on previous launches, you expect a **10–50x traffic spike** within the first hour, but the exact magnitude is unknown. The spike is bursty — it may come in waves as social media posts go viral.
<empty-block/>
**Guidelines for discussion: **
- There are no single correct answers. We are looking for structured thinking, awareness of tradeoffs, and the ability to reason about failure modes.
- Feel free to draw diagrams, write pseudocode, or reference specific technologies.
- When you propose a solution, also state what it does NOT solve or what new problem it introduces.
- If you make an assumption, state it explicitly. 
<empty-block/>
**At the end:**
- Ask the candidate feedback on technical assignment
- Ask the candidate if they feel they had a fair chance to represent themselves in this round 
- Reserve time for candidate’s questions to us
<empty-block/>
</content>
</page>

PAGE 184: [page] Technical Project Discussion — Interview Guide (Infrastructure)
ID: 32fc3702-22d5-8050-8235-e658c1acd49f
URL: 
================================================================================
Here is the result of "view" for the Page with URL https://www.notion.so/32fc370222d580508235e658c1acd49f as of 2026-04-02T22:11:55.700Z:
<page url="https://www.notion.so/32fc370222d580508235e658c1acd49f">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/32fc370222d5805c95d0ef480547b6ab" title="Onsite"/>
<ancestor-2-page url="https://www.notion.so/30ac370222d580a58e20e697f506c23d" title="Infrastructure Engineer — Hiring Guide
"/>
<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/1a1c370222d5807db2edf4324f3c7723" title="JDs, SCORECARDS AND PROCESSES"/>
<ancestor-4-page url="https://www.notion.so/1a1c370222d5802d92f1fc32471508f0" title="People/Talent resources "/>
<ancestor-5-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580268496de1d8a4ab2ec" title=""/>
<ancestor-6-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5808facd7c952b579a4cd" title=""/>
<ancestor-7-database url="https://www.notion.so/11ec370222d58020ad93e98fe0cc4607" title="Company Home"/>
</ancestor-path>
<properties>
{"title":"Technical Project Discussion — Interview Guide (Infrastructure)"}
</properties>
<content>
Evaluate the candidate’s technical ownership, decision-making, and ability to deeply understand and reflect on complex systems they’ve built.
This is a deep dive into ONE past project.
Focus on:
- ownership and contributions
- system design and architecture
- technical decision-making and tradeoffs
- debugging and problem-solving
- ability to reflect and improve systems
## What Good Looks Like
Strong candidates will:
- clearly explain a complex system end-to-end
- demonstrate real ownership (not just participation)
- explain why decisions were made (not just what)
- discuss tradeoffs and alternatives
- walk through real failures or challenges
- reflect on what they would do differently
## Core Question
Here's how we'll structure it: you'll have about 15 minutes to walk me through the project uninterrupted - the architecture, the key decisions you made, the trade-offs, and the challenges. I won't cut in during that time. After that, I'll go deep with questions for about 20 minutes. Then we'll end with a retro - how you'd approach it differently today. We'll save the last 5 minutes for your questions. Any questions before we start?"<br><br>"Go ahead - take a moment to think about which project you want to walk me through. It should be something where you made meaningful technical decisions and faced real constraints. Ready when you are."
"Hi \[name\], welcome. I'm \[your name\] - I'm on \[team\], focused on \[responsibilities\]. Today's format is a bit different from a typical interview. I want to hear you go really deep on one project - the most technically complex thing you've worked on, ideally in the ML infrastructure space. No slides needed; I care about the substance, not the presentation.
<empty-block/>
<details>
<summary>Candidate Presentation (15 min)</summary>
	> Do not interrupt during this phase. Your job is to listen and take notes. If they go off on a tangent, let them - it often reveals what they actually care about. If they finish early (under 8 min), that's a signal in itself.
	<details>
	<summary>What to listen for?</summary>
		- Depth markers -  note when you hear these
			- Do they lead with "what" or "why"? Strong candidates explain reasoning before mechanics.
			- Do they name specific systems, numbers, constraints  - or stay at abstract level?
			- Do they acknowledge trade-offs unprompted, or present everything as the obvious right call?
			- Do they mention other teams, stakeholders, pushback? Or present as if they worked in a vacuum?
			- How do they talk about failure? Do they surface it voluntarily, minimize it, or skip it entirely?
		- Note-taking prompts (jot for Q&A)
			- Any decision they made that sounds under-explained - you'll push on this 
			- Any number that sounds impressive - verify it.   
			- Any team dynamic they glossed over - ask about it
			- Any technology choice that wasn't justified — surface it
	</details>
</details>
<details>
<summary>Deep Q&A ( \~ 20 min)</summary>
	”Thanks - really interesting. I'm going to go deep now. Some of my questions will be about things you mentioned, some will push into areas you didn't cover. There are no trick questions - I just want to understand how you think.”
	<details>
	<summary>Q1: Architecture and design decisions - “You chose \[X\]. Walk me through why that over \[Y\]”</summary>
		<details>
		<summary>Frame it as</summary>
			- "You mentioned \[decision\]. What were the real alternatives you considered, and what ruled them out?"
			- "At the time, what were you most uncertain about in that choice?"
			- "If someone on your team pushed back on that approach, what would their strongest argument have been?"
		</details>
		<details>
		<summary>Listen for</summary>
			- Can they steelman the alternative? Weak candidates can only defend their choice, not articulate the opponent's case.
			- Is their reasoning based on data/constraints, or post-hoc rationalization?
			- Positives:
				- Names concrete constraints that drove the choice
				- Acknowledges what they gave up
			- Negatives:
				-  Every choice was "obvious" or "best practice"<br>
		</details>
	</details>
	<details>
	<summary>Q2: Scale and constraints - “What broke first as this scaled, and how did you know it was going to break before it did?”</summary>
		<details>
		<summary>Variations</summary>
			- "What were the hard limits of your design - the ceiling you knew you'd eventually hit?"
			- "What assumptions in your architecture would have needed to change at 10x the load?"
			- "How did you validate it would hold at scale before you committed to it?"
		</details>
		<details>
		<summary>Listen for</summary>
			- Do they have mental models for failure modes, or did they discover problems reactively?
			- Can they distinguish what they knew vs. what they discovered?
			<details>
			<summary>Positives</summary>
				- Anticipates bottlenecks before they happened
				-  Has numbers - n

<callout icon="🎄" color="gray_bg">
	Next company wide closure during Christmas: 24 Dec. 2025 - 2 Jan. 2026. Please submit these days in Rippling as PTO.
</callout>
# Time off Philosophy
Our time off policy is flexible time off, in the sense that you can take what you need. Like work, we approach our time off with high ownership, and want to acknowledge that everyone is working hard to make BFL successful. 
We offer flexible paid time off, with the expectation that everyone takes **at least **the statutory allowance shown in your Rippling profile. 
<empty-block/>
**Note **
- This policy applies globally. 
- In Rippling, under **My Time**, you see your total holiday balance, which aligns with local statutory requirements. Make sure to use it all and have your balance at **0 or negative by the end of the year. **You can go into negative balance without any impact, all submitted time off days are paid. 
- All balances reset at the start of the year, no days accrue. 
- All leave is tracked through Rippling. Submit all your leave days include PTO, Sick Day, Child Sick day, Special leave, etc. through Rippling. 
- Please also log any public holidays you take in Rippling, so our teams across different countries stay aligned. These will **not **be deducted from your total holiday balance.
<callout icon="☝" color="gray_bg">
	For people hired through [Remote.com](http://Remote.com) - Please log in all your leave days on the Remote portal. 
</callout>
# **Standard Operating Procedure **
When you take time off, do the following: 
**Plan**
- Notify your team at minimum 2x the length of planned leave in advance
- For 2+consecutive weeks outside of the Christmas period, please ask your team lead 
- Submit requests through Rippling, once you have used your statutory days, your balance will go into negative 
- Ensure no conflicts with team deliverables like new releases or key meetings like offsites 
**Prepare**
- **Block your calendar using "Out of Office" instead of Event in your calendar. This automatically updates Slack so people are aware you’re away.**
- Ensure nothing will be blocked on you during this time. Either close out or delegate ongoing items and ensure there is an escalation path. Create a handover document for any ongoing projects if needed. 
- Enable email auto-response if you won’t check your email 
# Global vacation, public holiday & birthday calendar 
Get visibility into when someone might be off. 
Click on "+" next to "other calendars" and add URL. 
<empty-block/>
**Vacation calendar: **
webcal://app.rippling.com/api/feed/calendar/pto/company/5lqvg47ynryjmoye/8c098f4132a12b994e0b61937a61f22f0323a6582671c490b4dfc1f3c6e0aff8/calendar.ics?company=6740c060240cfae2554950a4
**Birthdays: **
[https://calendar.google.com/calendar/embed?src=k9kfehg90vftbqejgmrcjpnsabplgfh3%40import.calendar.google.com&ctz=Europe%2FBerlin](https://calendar.google.com/calendar/embed?src=k9kfehg90vftbqejgmrcjpnsabplgfh3%40import.calendar.google.com&ctz=Europe%2FBerlin)

- How do we tweak the standard OKR/rev-focused operating playbooks to help accelerate BFL’s research first focus?
- Need to make presentations/discussions more data-driven and factual: e.g. what is actual TAM per use case? e.g. Andi’s example on fal for GTM, e.g. Anthony’s analysis on Q1 update

<page url="https://www.notion.so/317c370222d5805fbac6fb1734902f80">
<ancestor-path>
<parent-page url="https://www.notion.so/312c370222d580a6841fc2a6312f0ed1" title="Shop proposals"/>

<ancestor-3-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d580cab1aae5ec0f8cac31" title=""/>
<ancestor-4-page url="https://www.notion.so/21bc370222d5808facd7c952b579a4cd" title=""/>

{"title":"VAE/SSL Pre-training"}
</properties>
<content>
# VAE+SSL Joint Pre-training for Video
## Research Question / Hypothesis
Can we make video VAE latents more learnable by jointly training with SSL objectives (DINO, [V-JEPA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08471)) — while keeping reconstruction quality intact? And if the learned representations are strong enough, can the encoder double as a foundation for downstream conditioning and planning?
The core idea: reconstruction-only VAE training produces latents biased toward low-level pixel info. More training compute → better reconstruction but generation quality stagnates or gets worse. [VTP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13687) showed this clearly for images (see Fig 1 below). Adding understanding objectives (CLIP, SSL) fixes this — generation improves with compute.
We want to do this for video, where temporal semantics matter and image-based SSL (like what [REPA-E](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10483) uses) isn't enough.
**Hypotheses:**
- Joint VAE+SSL training injects semantic structure into latents → better learnability for downstream diffusion
- V-JEPA style objectives may be better than DINO for video since they capture motion/temporal stuff. V-JEPA 2 hits 77.3% on SSv2 with a frozen encoder + attentive probe — that's pure motion understanding from SSL alone, no labels during pre-training. More importantly, it shows these representations transfer to planning: the same encoder enables zero-shot robot control after minimal post-training. This is the kind of latent structure we want in our VAE — if SSL quality is high enough, the encoder becomes reusable for downstream conditioning and planning, not just tokenization.
- This should scale — more VAE pre-training compute should keep helping generation (unlike rec-only which plateaus)
### Core figure from [VTP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13687) (Yao et al., 2025)
![](https://prod-files-secure.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/d47c9781-7a36-4f60-98ec-c16fd7b286a6/49acc9e0-3573-480b-8010-4e8081940b67/Screenshot_2026-03-02_at_9.54.07_PM.png?X-Amz-Algorithm=AWS4-HMAC-SHA256&X-Amz-Content-Sha256=UNSIGNED-PAYLOAD&X-Amz-Credential=ASIAZI2LB466V5C6XCYA%2F20260419%2Fus-west-2%2Fs3%2Faws4_request&X-Amz-Date=20260419T152300Z&X-Amz-Expires=3600&X-Amz-Security-Token=IQoJb3JpZ2luX2VjED8aCXVzLXdlc3QtMiJHMEUCIBhaRZiLC7JWqcE%2Bb8TNWmYu08lIA4%2Bf4j6y7tOiXkfqAiEA522UGoQLzRTlyn2kP0JA3IM9oXJbKngJtRYqABid1eIq%2FwMICBAAGgw2Mzc0MjMxODM4MDUiDJi7gm6HOJ7GUvjhZSrcA41XbNYCIk1Cp9EDShJhFfrck1oKW5eAyoFAuWxJQ20tbcgCo%2BbvNPwdQywVkFN5%2BYdc4T%2Fqff4aP5m6VoAmCFphcBuQ5nT2NmlATGOlVU85JRkPVnUgMxnqtcruw3iUhidznzUx7lrjkVPQ%2BdiGONWmXDW%2BarhI1%2BZgHYhMruslxYrvXHaddwe%2B7trdOxYWRodldqSEQ%2Fq%2FS5Eq2CA2C8SGBjiyhEsg0gFWk8z3XJOxj%2FHzpIIJIBHRg%2FEu6niSSz%2Bsk8HFpeRlbNzkWgtvpz20gEcb%2BTNABPggxQHUIx78voW0UuXKZCtpbNffU4qJ8RXG%2BCvahLELrZKPDqsEh2frzowvWwC9PporknWksrwDnkn%2BLHBW7PiizL0N0sQhDd5KDXhXud5dnCwElQK1lsgy1wbLNUFe8bpCq%2FVKZb33W61tXfqkR3ZUqU4JjCO5s%2B6nTQatIfizLe4%2B1j6WgvIXiLoPusBltWhhUUiD2hMY1BfsH2WAydUftj2g%2Fp3y%2FwFEFVlz9UkVZVk8rhRoK9RiQJJv28%2BlHVzTQJXmY4GFk2hAtSbrsTgwmXv7aWktVq19b93FKJ91yqKnp%2FA202tJGma6mZlZ%2BfqnEypTmWYMEE8gRMW28dTniUY2MMDQk88GOqUBXOrN03AeSpKU0t3GdCZtxkP%2B8Fa6SHrENIKEDDZIJbLxnP2E2u2X3ubWUkzE8gBqCQhMEgQ90F1yM9BMLQU%2BcRfNoOZxEjzvxKwN3b5RP7fube52BhCNlJWiubsFSIU4qOQLPB%2FXVgsk5qbKpket916JGuIdPq0EqyEw%2F8IoB4V0cGgkP5NnhTjT4KcrmYylCIm6wZymKB49pYE9d86PLPJhRivk&X-Amz-Signature=79fe210706f95736614a9a7112d81632966dccb57eb1537ca2d29ce6daa78981&X-Amz-SignedHeaders=host&x-amz-checksum-mode=ENABLED&x-id=GetObject)
*Fig 1: From VTP paper. SSL+AE and CLIP+AE show correlated growth in generation and understanding with compute. Reconstruction-only AE stagnates. We want to replicate this behavior for video with video-native SSL.*
## Why this matters
Current approaches each have clear problems (see also [BFL's representation comparison](https://bfl.ai/research/representation-comparison) and [Sander Dieleman's latent space post](https://sander.ai/2025/04/15/latents.html) for broader context on the learnability-reconstruction trade-off):
- [**RAE**](https://rae-dit.github.io/) (frozen DINOv2 + trained decoder): Good learnability but reconstruction is bad — color shifts, texture errors. Frozen image encoder, no temporal understanding for video.
- [**REPA-E**](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10483) (end-to-end VAE+diffusion via REPA loss): Strong image results but ties VAE optimization to diffusion training. Uses DINOv2 which is image-only — not ideal for video.
- [**VTP**](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.13687) (CLIP+SSL+rec for images): Proves the scaling thesis but image-only, hasn't been extended to video.
Our angle: **video-native SSL directly in VAE pre-training, decoupled from diffusion**. This gives us:
- Reusable tokenizer (not entangled with diffusion like REPA-E)
- Trainable encoder with reconstruction loss (not frozen like RAE)
- Temporal understanding from [V-JEPA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.08471) / [V-JEPA 2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.09985)
- Potential encoder reuse for conditioning and planning — if probe accuracy is high enough, the encoder has generic video understanding and can serve as a conditioning backbone. V-JEPA 2 demonstrates this concretely: their self-supervised video encoder, after post-training with

<content>
This guide helps interviewers assess candidates against our four core values. Each section covers one value, with a scoring matrix per question showing what strong, mixed, and weak answers look like.
> **How to use this guide:** For each answer, assess both the value-specific signals and the universal signals at the bottom of this page. A strong answer is specific, concise, and shows genuine ownership.
---
#### Contents (quick find) {toggle="true"}
	<table_of_contents color="gray"/>
# 🔥 Obsessed
We are a frontier research lab. The science has to be right, the understanding deep, the product beautiful.
#### Q1 — Walk me through how you've improved at your craft over the past year. What specifically did you do?
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>✅ Strong</td>
<td>🟡 Mixed</td>
<td>❌ Weak</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Names a specific skill gap they identified themselves, unprompted</td>
<td>Genuine effort but driven by a manager's feedback, not own initiative</td>
<td>Struggles to name anything concrete from the past year, references articles, books but nothing concrete even when pressed</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Describes a deliberate practice: course, mentor, side project, applied reading</td>
<td>Learning happened but no clear application or output to point to</td>
<td>Describes general busyness as the reason for not improving</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Can show output — shipped thing, measurable result, changed habit</td>
<td>Improvement was broad ("I tried to communicate better") without specifics</td>
<td>Vague platitudes: "I always try to keep learning"</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Improvement was self-directed, not performance-review driven</td>
<td></td>
<td>Only cites on-the-job experience, no intentional self-investment</td>
</tr>
</table>
### Q2 — Describe a time you went back and redid something even though it was "good enough."
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>✅ Strong</td>
<td>🟡 Mixed</td>
<td>❌ Weak</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The redo was self-initiated — nobody asked, no deadline pressure</td>
<td>Redo was triggered by user feedback or a bug, not proactively self driven</td>
<td>Can't recall a specific example — defaults to "I usually just ship it"</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Can articulate precisely what they identified as the additional opportunity and why they invested the time- what they identified as the value add</td>
<td>Knew something was off but waited until it caused a problem</td>
<td>Frames "good enough" as the right standard without tension</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The outcome was meaningfully better, and they can describe how</td>
<td>Redid it but can't explain what specifically made it better</td>
<td>Example is trivial or clearly hypothetical, not a real trade-off</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Shows pride in the work, not just in the efficiency of delivering it</td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
</table>
### Q3 — Tell me about a time you pushed back on shipping because you thought something wasn't ready.
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>✅ Strong</td>
<td>🟡 Mixed</td>
<td>❌ Weak</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Articulates clearly what the risk was and why they personally felt it was wrong</td>
<td>Did raise the concern but backed down quickly when pushed</td>
<td>No example — always deferred to the team or PM on readiness</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Pushed back despite pressures from: peers, manager, or deadlines</td>
<td>Concern was process or coverage, not about the quality of the product</td>
<td>Describes pushback as conflict-avoidant: "I mentioned it but didn't want to block"</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Used data or a concrete argument, not just a gut feeling</td>
<td>Framing is more about covering all bases (self serving in nature) than genuine care for the outcome</td>
<td>Example is about missing a test, not genuine quality conviction</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Outcome validates the concern, or they accept the cost if overruled</td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
</table>

# 🤝 Low Ego
The work speaks. The best idea wins, no matter who said it. Credit is shared. Nobody is above any task.
### Q1 — Describe a situation where you were wrong about something important. How did you realise it and change course?
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>✅ Strong</td>
<td>🟡 Mixed</td>
<td>❌ Weak</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The mistake was significant — technical direction, architecture, people decision</td>
<td>Admits the mistake but still somewhat frames it as "not entirely my fault"</td>
<td>Example feels transparently chosen to look good — the "mistake" had a great outcome</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>They realised it themselves or genuinely accepted it when shown evidence/ provided with rational </td>
<td>Changed course but waited until it was unavoidable to do so</td>
<td>Extensive reframing, context-setting, or blame-sharing before getting to the error</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Changed course visibly and without hedging or minimising</td>
<td>Example is low-stakes — admits being wrong about something minor</td>
<td>Can't recall a genuinely wrong call, or pivots to someone else's mistake</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Reflects on what they'd do differently- no defensiveness</td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
</table>

<content>
# **Summary**
We are entering a critical inflection point. The race to frontier-scale AI training is being won or lost on infrastructure. Our ability to train the next generation of models depends not just on acquiring GPUs, but on our ability to operate them at scale with maximum utilization, minimum downtime, and zero tolerance for wasted compute.
This memo outlines our team’s charter: to build the systems foundation that makes frontier training runs routine, not heroic. We are organized around two reinforcing pillars - Resiliency and Scalability - with the goal of turning our GPU fleet into a reliable, high-throughput training factory.
**The Core Problem**
> At 10,000+ GPU scale, component failure is not an edge case - it is a statistical certainty on every training run. A single wasted wall-clock hour on a 10,000-GPU job is not one hour lost - it is 10,000 GPU-hours burned, the equivalent of running a single GPU continuously for over thirteen months. A single undetected straggler node can silently degrade throughput by 10–30%. An unrecoverable failure on a multi-day run can waste tens of thousands of GPU-hours. Our mission is to make these problems invisible to researchers. {color="red_bg"}
---
# **Two Axes of Scale**
We are scaling simultaneously along two axes, and each multiplies the complexity of the other:
- **Total GPUs in fleet: **Growing cluster size from thousands to tens of thousands of GPUs. This increases the blast radius of systemic issues (firmware bugs, network misconfigurations, storage bottlenecks) and demands fleet-grade operational tooling.
- **GPUs per training job: **Scaling individual jobs to consume thousands of GPUs simultaneously. This tightens coupling between nodes, makes collective communication a first-class scaling bottleneck, and means a single faulty node can stall the entire job.
The intersection of these axes is where frontier training lives - and where most infrastructure teams break. Our charter is to ensure we don’t.
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>**Dimension**</td>
<td>**Current State**</td>
<td>**Near-Term Target**</td>
<td>**Why It Matters**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Cluster Scale</td>
<td>Scaling up</td>
<td>5K–20K GPUs per cluster</td>
<td>Enables frontier-class training runs</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Per-Job Scale</td>
<td>Scaling up</td>
<td>Full-cluster utilization</td>
<td>Unlocks models that require massive parallelism</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Mean Time to Recovery</td>
<td>Reactive</td>
<td>Automated sub-minute recovery</td>
<td>Prevents 1000s of GPU-hours wasted per incident</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>GPU Utilization (MFU)</td>
<td>Baseline</td>
<td>\>55% sustained MFU</td>
<td>Direct cost multiplier on \$100M+ infrastructure</td>
</tr>
</table>
# **Pillar 1: Resiliency**
At scale, reliability is not about preventing every failure - it is about detecting failures instantly, recovering automatically, and ensuring no researcher has to debug infrastructure. Our resiliency strategy spans four layers:
## **1.1 Proactive Health Agent**
We are building and owning a purpose-built health agent for our GPU fleet, developed in close partnership with NVIDIA to ensure CSP-agnostic operation. This is a strategic investment: rather than depending on any single cloud provider’s monitoring, we control our own detection and remediation stack.
**Key Capabilities**
- **Preflight checks: **Automated GPU/NIC/NVLink/storage validation before any node enters the schedulable pool. No job should ever land on a degraded node.
- **Continuous background checks: **Persistent health probes running alongside workloads - detecting degradation (ECC errors, thermal throttling, NVLink CRC errors) before it becomes a failure.
- **Active check harness: **On-demand deep diagnostics triggered on suspicious nodes: DCGM validation, NCCL loopback tests, memory stress, storage I/O benchmarks.
- **CSP-agnostic design: **Portable across infrastructure providers. We are not locked into any vendor’s health monitoring stack.
## **1.2 Comprehensive Logging Infrastructure**
Effective root-cause analysis at scale requires complete, correlated logs. We capture and centralize:
- **GPU error history: **XID error codes per node with temporal correlation - enabling us to identify nodes trending toward failure before they crash a job.
- **Node lifecycle events: **Every allocation, deallocation, reboot, and drain event - providing a full audit trail for any node involved in an incident.
- **System-level logs: **syslog, kern.log, dmesg, and access.log captured and stored for every node. These are the forensic record when something goes wrong.
## **1.3 Unified Monitoring & Observability**
Monitoring at this scale requires more than dashboards. It requires a single pane of glass that spans the entire fleet and provides both operator and researcher visibility:
**Operator View: Global Fleet Dashboard**
- Unified cross-cluster view of fleet health, node status, and active incidents
- Anomaly detection and alerting across all clusters from a single control plane
**Researcher View: Resource Dashboard**
- Per-user and per-job visibility into all allocated resour

<callout icon="💡" color="orange_bg">
	Benefits & highlights of each model and when to recommend them by use case. 
</callout>
## **TLDR;**
1. Recommended Standard for most Usecases = Flux.2 \[pro\]
2. Best Model = Flux.2 \[max\]                                   
3. Fastest = Flux.2 \[schnell\] (Upcoming Model)
4. Cheapest = look it up for [API pricing](https://bfl.ai/pricing) & [licensing pricing](https://bfl.ai/licensing)
5. High resolution = Flux.2 \[pro\] Ultra (up to 4 megapixel, all other images 1 mp) 
6. Edits = Flux.2 \[flex\] 
7. Saturated style (liked by consumers) = Flux.2 \[pro\]
8. Self-host = Flux.2 \[dev\] or Flux.2 \[klein\] (upcoming)
9. How does moderation work? [https://docs.google.com/document/d/1-Fn8bdZzxT5nWCfprIx_eLM7QahgZmysrKC9EknGfRU/edit?tab=t.0](https://docs.google.com/document/d/1-Fn8bdZzxT5nWCfprIx_eLM7QahgZmysrKC9EknGfRU/edit?tab=t.0)
<callout icon="📌" color="green_bg">
	**→ Flux.2 \[pro\] is our Flagship workhorse model, in most cases you cannot do much wrong recommending that one!**
</callout>

## Deployment Recommendations
Sometimes we provide inference containers or code that customers deploy directly on their infrastructure. If they ask about number of GPUs to use for their deployment, this is usually what we recommend:
<table header-row="true">
<colgroup>
<col width="115.6640625">
<col width="96.6640625">
<col width="235.66666666666666">
<col width="400">
<col>
<col>
</colgroup>
<tr>
<td>Model</td>
<td># of GPUs</td>
<td>Type of GPU</td>
<td>Additional Considerations</td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>FLUX.2 \[pro\]</td>
<td>4</td>
<td>H100, H200 or GB200</td>
<td>Fewer GPUs could lead to Out-Of-Memory (OOM) errors for larger images or higher numbers of reference images, as well as higher latency. Smaller GPUs (in terms of memory) could suffer from the same issue.</td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>FLUX.2 \[flex\]</td>
<td>4</td>
<td>H200 or GB200</td>
<td>H100 GPUs don’t suffice for the current setup in terms of memory. Fewer and smaller GPUs might suffer from increased latency and OOM errors.</td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>FLUX.2 \[klein\] 4B & 9B</td>
<td>1</td>
<td>H100, H200 or GB200</td>
<td>Klein can run on a single GPU. Using more than a single GPU will not reduce latency further, and smaller GPUs could suffer from OOM errors.</td>
<td></td>
<td></td>
</tr>
</table>
## FLUX Model Names and what they mean
- Number = version & sub version (.1, 1.1, .2)
- \[pro\] vs \[dev\] = closed source (API) vs. open weights (licensing & self-host)
- Ultra = High resolution (currently up to 4 megapixel)
- Raw = Fine-tuned for ultra realistic output

## FLUX .2

## <span discussion-urls="discussion://2bcc3702-22d5-8043-977b-001c9f370810">[FLUX.1 Kontext](/1fcc370222d580e2af0df93271590e2b?pvs=25)</span>
FLUX.1 Kontext \[max\]
- Doesn’t do much for edits
- basically built on pro and has a model behind it and picks what it thinks are best. (secret)
	- Thats why it costs more
- Helps a lot more with text to image
	- Likely better for text 
FLUX.1 Kontext \[pro\]
- best overall for price and performance likley (don’t share this with customers!!!)
FLUX.1 Kontext \[dev\] (open weights - [FLUX non-commercial license](https://github.com/black-forest-labs/flux/blob/main/model_licenses/LICENSE-FLUX1-dev))
## FLUX1.1
FLUX1.1 \[pro\]
- more stylized and less resolution
FLUX1.1 \[pro\] Ultra
- Based off 1.1. It can go much higher resolution than every model. 
- Ultra can do 4mp (2k res) image so resolution is way higher.
- Still very fast speed. 
- Worse at listening and prompt following than Kontext (personal preference)
FLUX1.1 \[pro\] Raw &
